# Usher M6 — Search Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Make the catalog answerable. PRD 03's fourth pipeline stage — the one every earlier milestone deferred — becomes real: a weighted full-text document, a typo-tolerant type-ahead path, an embedding for the tier that deserves one, Reciprocal Rank Fusion over the two, and a precomputed neighbour table that makes "more like this" a lookup rather than a computation. And ADR-0002's Meilisearch gate stops being a promise and becomes a number.

**Architecture:** The search document is a **stored generated column on `titles`**, so PostgreSQL recomputes it on every write of its inputs and no code can forget to. The embedding cannot be generated — it needs a model — so `title_embeddings` carries the `model_name` and a `source_fingerprint` of the exact text embedded, which turns "is this stale?" into a SQL predicate that drives the backfill, the gauge, and the test at once. `PostgresSearchIndex` (`adapters/search/postgres.py`) implements `SearchIndex`; `PostgresSuggestIndex` (`adapters/search/postgres.py`) implements a **separate** `SuggestIndex` port, because ADR-0002 gates Meilisearch to the instant-search box only and that is therefore the real swap boundary. `SearchService` (`services/search.py`) owns the `Embedder`, applies the query-side instruction prefix, fuses with RRF, and ranks in application code — retrieval and ranking stay separated exactly as PRD 05 insists. A new `JobKind.INDEX` and its handler keep embeddings current; a keyset-cursor backfill drains the stale predicate.

---

## The one thing this milestone must not get wrong

Every earlier milestone shipped state that something *wrote*. M6 is the first to ship state that is *derived* — and derived state has a failure mode the others do not:

> **A stale index does not raise. It answers.**

A `Title` that was enriched last night and never re-indexed does not produce an error, a log line, a failed job, or a degraded health check. It produces a search result set that is quietly, confidently wrong — missing the film you just added, ranking on an overview that has since been replaced. There is no timeout to catch it and no exception to translate. It is the M5 lesson in a new costume: **a channel that looks healthy while delivering nothing.** Here it is an index that looks healthy while describing yesterday.

**The decision: nothing in this milestone infers freshness. Every derived artefact is either fresh by construction, or carries the fingerprint of the input it was derived from so that staleness is a query.**

Three places that rule is made structural rather than remembered:

1. **The full-text document is a `GENERATED ALWAYS AS (…) STORED` column.** Not a trigger, not a job, not a queue. PostgreSQL recomputes it inside the same statement that writes `name` or `overview`, and there is no code path — none, including a bulk `COPY`, a hand-written `UPDATE`, or a future migration — that can write a title and skip its document. Half of this milestone's freshness problem is deleted rather than solved.

2. **The embedding carries `model_name` and `source_fingerprint`, so staleness is one predicate.** An embedding needs a model, so it cannot be generated, so it is a job, and jobs can fail, park, or never be enqueued at all. Rather than trusting the queue, `title_embeddings` records *what* was embedded (`md5` of the exact assembled text) and *by what* (`model_name`). Stale is then:

   ```sql
   -- one predicate, three consumers: the backfill's cursor, the
   -- `usher.search.embeddings.stale` gauge, and the test that proves the
   -- enqueue-on-enrichment path actually closes.
   e.title_id IS NULL
      OR e.model_name       IS DISTINCT FROM :model_name
      OR e.source_fingerprint IS DISTINCT FROM md5(<the same assembly the builder uses>)
   ```

   A backfill that is a predicate rather than a cursor over "everything" is self-draining, idempotent, and re-runnable at zero write cost — and combined with `enqueue`'s existing `WHERE jobs.priority < excluded.priority`, re-running it writes nothing at all.

3. **The fusion reports coverage instead of hiding it.** A title with no embedding is *absent from the semantic candidate list*, and RRF cannot tell "ranked last" from "never a candidate". `SearchService` therefore returns a `SearchOutcome` carrying `semantic_coverage` — the fraction of the filtered population that actually had a vector — and `SearchMode.FUSED` against a catalog with zero embeddings degrades to full-text **and says so**, rather than silently becoming full-text with a confident-looking blended score.

**The asymmetry is the point, and the plan names which half is which.** One artefact is guaranteed by the database and one is guaranteed by a queue plus a fingerprint. Task 6 is where that asymmetry is chosen deliberately; every later task inherits it.

### The degenerate-document trap, and why the fingerprint scheme must not create a second one

Measured, and it is not a corner case in a catalog that is mostly IMDb skeletons:

> **Every whitespace-only input embeds to the *identical* vector.** cos(`""`, `" "`) = cos(`""`, `"\n"`) = **1.0000, exactly.**

A title whose composed document comes out empty is not a bad result — it is a perfect unit vector satisfying every clause of the port contract, sitting at cosine 1.0 from every *other* empty-document title. That is a degenerate cluster of unbounded size pinned to the top of every "more like this" result, and no assertion about norms, dimensions or determinism can see it. Boundary call 4 (embed the enriched tier only) removes most of the exposure; it does not remove all of it, because an enriched title with no overview, no genres and no keywords is still reachable.

**So the document composer refuses to emit a degenerate document — and that refusal creates the second trap, which is worse.** If a refused title simply gets no `title_embeddings` row, it keeps matching the stale predicate forever: the backfill re-claims it every pass, the `usher.search.embeddings.stale` gauge never reaches zero, and the queue churns permanently on rows that can never succeed. **This project has shipped exactly that bug once already** — the watch-history repair whose merge carried the walk's instant was refused by the very row it existed to repair, and kept matching `played AND play_count = 0` for good.

**The rule, therefore: a refusal is a written outcome, not a skipped one.** A refused title gets a `title_embeddings` row with a `NULL` embedding, the current `model_name`, and the fingerprint of the degenerate text — so it stops matching the stale predicate, it starts matching a *separate* `embedding IS NULL` predicate that a diagnostic can count, and the moment enrichment gives it an overview the fingerprint changes and it is re-claimed exactly once. Task 11 owns the composer's refusal rule; Task 9 owns the nullable column and the predicate pair; the contract case that pins it is `test_a_title_whose_document_is_degenerate_stops_matching_the_backfill`.

Name-only skeletons, for the record, are *not* degenerate — measured pairwise cosine between unrelated name-only documents is 0.5867 (sd 0.055), and a skeleton still retrieves its own enriched form at 0.7638 against a 0.4751 cross-title mean. Crowded, but ordered. The refusal threshold is about *empty*, not about *thin*.

**What this costs — measured, not estimated.** A stored generated column is computed on every write of `titles`, including the bulk bootstrap, and unlike `bulk.py`'s `_SUSPENDABLE_INDEXES` it cannot be suspended. Measured 2026-08-02 against `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` on this host, 300,000 synthetic skeleton-shaped rows through `INSERT … SELECT` — the bootstrap's own statement shape:

| | plain `titles` | with the generated column |
|---|---|---|
| `INSERT … SELECT` 300k rows | **734 ms** | **2,980 ms** (**4.06×**) |
| total relation size | 57 MB | 76 MB (**+33%**) |

Extrapolated to 1,271,138 titles: **the whole-catalog bootstrap pays about +9.5 seconds and about +80 MB.** Against a bootstrap already measured in minutes, that is noise, and it buys a freshness guarantee no amount of code can equal. **The generated column is confirmed, and the trigger-maintained side-table fallback is not taken.**

**And the design has one hole, which is verified rather than suspected.** The expression needs a custom `IMMUTABLE` wrapper (see below), and **PostgreSQL does not recompute stored generated values when a function's body changes.** Demonstrated directly: a row stored as `'alpha':1 'beta':2`, then `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` to return something else entirely — the stored value **did not move**, while a fresh evaluation returned `'zzzchang':1`. Worse, a subsequent `UPDATE` of that row *did* recompute it with the new definition. So changing the function silently produces a table where **some rows were computed by the old definition and some by the new, with nothing to tell them apart** and the split determined by which rows happened to be touched since.

That is this milestone's own failure mode reintroduced through the back door, so it gets the same treatment as everything else here — made structural, not remembered:

1. **Any migration that changes the wrapper's body must force a full rewrite of the column in the same migration**, and say so in its docstring.
2. **A test samples rows and asserts the stored document equals a freshly computed one.** Drift is then caught by the suite rather than by a user whose search quietly stopped matching genres. This test is cheap, and it is the only thing standing between the wrapper and a silent mixed-state table.

**Why not the alternatives.**

- *Rebuild the document in the `index` job alongside the embedding.* This is the obvious symmetry and it is wrong: it makes the cheap, always-correct half depend on the expensive, fallible half. A parked embedding job would then also mean a stale full-text document, and the two failures would be indistinguishable.
- *Trust the enqueue and skip the fingerprint.* The enqueue is one line in `EnrichService._apply`. Every path that writes a title without going through it — a migration backfill, a repair script, a future source of catalog updates — produces a silently stale vector with nothing to detect it. The fingerprint costs one `md5` and makes correctness observable instead of assumed.
- *Version the whole table with a `search_version` integer bumped on write.* Detects that something changed but not *what*, so a model swap and a text edit are the same signal, and re-embedding after a model change would require a full sweep with no way to confirm it finished.

---

## Scope: what M6 is, and nine boundary calls made deliberately

M6 owns PRD 03's stage 4 and all of PRD 05. Nine places where the edge was genuinely ambiguous, each decided rather than drifted into:

1. **No HTTP route is added.** `usher search`, `usher suggest`, `usher similar` and `usher index` deliver every capability through the CLI — the project's established second composition root, exactly as M2 did for `bootstrap` and M4 did for the whole ingest pipeline. M9 owns the HTTP surface and PRD 07's RFC 9457 error vocabulary, and adds routers over finished wiring. **PRD 05's `GET /titles/{id}/similar` is M9's**, and M6 builds the service and the precomputed table it will read. M5 added two routes because demand promotion is *defined* as a property of requesting a title and had no caller otherwise; nothing in M6 has that shape — M7 is the in-process consumer of similarity and it runs next.

2. **Weight class B — cast and crew — ships empty, and PRD 05 is corrected to say so.** There is no `Person`, `Credit`, `Collection` or `Image` table, domain model or port anywhere in `src/`; `ports/metadata.py:68-82` defers all four to M7 and M9 by name. The only place credits physically exist is `raw_payloads.payload`, and assembling a search document out of a *provider's* JSON shape would put a TMDb-shaped concept in `services/`. The document builder therefore takes a `credits: Sequence[str] = ()` argument that is always empty in M6, the weight class is reserved rather than repurposed, and — because the document is a generated column — the day M7 lands `Credit`, filling it is a migration, not a rewrite.

3. **No `title_search_names` table is built.** PRD 05 specifies a narrow `(title_id, name, kind, popularity)` table for autocomplete. Its justification is *aliases and people names* — one title contributing many rows. Neither has a data source in M6 (see 2), so the table would hold exactly one row per title duplicating `titles(id, name, kind, popularity)`: a second copy of the same data, a second thing to keep fresh, and a new instance of precisely the staleness problem this milestone exists to eliminate. The trigram indexes go **directly on `titles`**, and Task 12 measures the narrow-table variant against the direct one so the decision carries a number. When M7 lands aliases and people, the narrow table is the migration that adds them.

4. **Embeddings cover the enriched tier, not the 1.27M-row catalog.** This is PRD 05's own two-workload split taken seriously: catalog lookup is full-text plus trigram over everything, and the semantic tier is the library experience at 2k–10k. A skeleton title is a name and a year; embedding it produces a vector of the name, which is what full-text already does better and cheaper. The `index` job's population is `enrichment_state <> 'skeleton'` — for which `ix_titles_enrichment_state` is *already the partial index that exists*. This is what keeps the backfill a coffee break instead of an overnight job, and it is why there is no `index` job for a skeleton title at all: its document is a generated column, so a skeleton title needs no job to be fully indexed.

5. **M6 publishes no new client event, and PRD 09's wording is corrected.** The roadmap says M6 "publishes `title.updated` through the `EventPublisher` port M5 built rather than inventing a channel." The port is already used: `EnrichService._apply` (`services/enrich.py:211-221`) publishes `TITLE_UPDATED` after the commit, naming the changed fields. Nothing a client renders depends on the search document or the embedding, so a second `title.updated` on index completion would be an event with no consumer — which `ports/events.py:40-47` establishes as the thing this project does not do ("no member nothing emits"). M6 satisfies the roadmap's intent by *not inventing a channel*; the sentence is reworded to say that.

6. **Query expansion is not built.** PRD 05 names one LLM call rewriting an emotional query into narrative language as "the cheaper, better-evidenced lever" for mood queries. It is one call in front of an existing `embed`, `ports/llm.py` has no implementation until M8, and adding a second unimplemented port dependency to the search path buys nothing M6 can measure. M6 embeds the query as typed. The seam is `SearchService.search`'s query string, and M8 or M9 wraps it.

7. **Meilisearch is not added regardless of what the gate says.** ADR-0002's gate is a *measurement with a decision attached*, and PRD 09 lists Meilisearch under post-v1 candidates. Task 26 runs the gate against the real catalog and records recall@5 per typo class. If it fails, M6's deliverable is the recorded failure, the ADR updated with it, and a scoped follow-up — not a second stateful service bolted on at the end of a milestone. The `SuggestIndex` port (see below) is what makes that follow-up cost one class.

8. **Similarity blends the two signals that have data, and the plan says out loud that the other two do not.** PRD 05 specifies a four-way blend: embedding cosine, Jaccard over genres/keywords/cast/crew, MovieLens tag-genome cosine, and collection membership. Checked against the code rather than the prose:

   | PRD 05 signal | Data source in M6 |
   |---|---|
   | Embedding cosine | ✅ built by this milestone |
   | Jaccard over **genres, keywords** | ✅ `ARRAY(Text)`, `NOT NULL`, `'{}'` default |
   | Jaccard over **cast, crew** | ❌ no `Person`/`Credit` table exists (boundary call 2) |
   | MovieLens tag-genome cosine | ❌ **the importer has never been built** |
   | Collection membership | ❌ `titles.collection_id` is a bare nullable UUID with no table and **nothing in `src/` ever writes it** — `services/enrich.py:92` says it is "a FK to a table M7 creates" |

   So M6 ships **embedding cosine + genre/keyword Jaccard**, and `SimilarityService`'s blend is written as a sum of weighted terms over an explicit signal list, so that landing a third signal is adding a term and a weight rather than rewriting a scorer.

   **The tag-genome finding is a roadmap gap, not just an M6 deferral, and Task 27 records it in PRD 09.** `PHASES = ("imdb", "tmdb-ids", "crosswalk", "all")` (`cli.py:57`) has no MovieLens phase and `adapters/bulk/` has no `movielens.py` — yet PRD 01 lists `MovieLensGenome` among `BulkDataset`'s implementations, PRD 04 gives it a phase, a size, a runtime and a licence row, and PRD 05 *and* PRD 06 both name it as a similarity input. It is deferred **nowhere**: not in the roadmap, not in CLAUDE.md, not in the progress log. An obligation recorded only where it was assumed is one nobody plans — which is exactly the failure PRD 09 already calls out for ADR-0012. M6 does not build it (it is an M2-shaped bulk importer and PRD 04 owns the phase); M6 makes it visible and gives it an owner.

9. **The `usher.db.staging` lock is fixed here, because M6 is what makes it hurt.** M5 recorded it: `stg_jobs` and `stg_watch_states` are fixed shared table names, and `stage_records` does `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS` + `CREATE UNLOGGED TABLE` — two `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` locks on the same name — so concurrent callers serialise. M6 adds an `index` enqueue to `EnrichService._apply`, which is **one job per enriched title**: a single-row `COPY` through a table-level exclusive lock, on the hot path of the pipeline. Task 22 adds the small-batch path that `tests/integration/test_titles_route.py:430-445` already prescribes.

---

## The three 🔶 markers this milestone was told to settle

All three say "settle in M6" in the source. Each is retired in place with a `**Settled in M6** —` paragraph recording the answer *and its evidence*, never deleted silently (`ports/source.py:151` is the established form).

### 🔶 1 — `SearchIndex` is shaped like Postgres, not like a candidate generator

`ports/search.py:39` names four defects. All four are real and all four are fixed:

| Defect | Settlement |
|---|---|
| `index(title_id)` forces a Meilisearch implementation to fetch each title back out — 1.3M round-trips on a rebuild | The port takes a **document, not an id**: `index_many(documents: Sequence[SearchDocument])`. `SearchDocument` is a frozen slotted dataclass the *service* assembles from a `Title` it already holds, so no implementation ever fetches back. |
| `SearchRequest.filters: dict[str, Any]` has no key vocabulary, so two backends invent different ones | Replaced by `SearchFilters`, a frozen slotted dataclass with a closed vocabulary: `kinds`, `year_from`, `year_to`, `genres`, `owned_only`, `min_enrichment`. A backend that cannot express one raises rather than ignoring it — an ignored filter returns *more* results, which reads as working. |
| No `index_many`/`rebuild` for bulk operations | `index_many` yes. **`rebuild` deliberately not** — it would be a second path to the same state, exercised only by an operator, and the predicate-driven backfill already rebuilds from scratch by construction. A port method whose only test is its own test is a liability. |
| Semantic search needs the query *vector*, which ADR-0002 anticipates supplying to Meilisearch as `userProvided` | `SearchRequest` gains `query_vector: tuple[float, ...] | None`. The **caller** computes it, which is what makes the port engine-neutral and simultaneously settles who applies the model's query prefix (see 🔶 3). |

### 🔶 2 — should `suggest` be its own port?

**Yes.** `SuggestIndex` is a separate ABC in `ports/search.py` with exactly one method, `suggest(prefix, limit) -> list[SearchHit]`.

The argument that decides it is not tidiness, it is **dual-write visibility**. ADR-0002's whole case for Postgres-first is "no dual-write synchronisation, no ghost documents, no second stateful service." If the gate fails and Meilisearch is added for the instant-search box, documents must then be written to *both* engines — which is exactly the cost ADR-0002 refused. Splitting the port puts that cost in the type system: adding Meilisearch means adding a write path to a port that today has none, and that is a visible, deliberate act. Folded into `SearchIndex`, the same change looks like implementing a method that was already there.

Two consequences, both deliberate:

- **`SuggestIndex` has no `index`/`remove`.** `PostgresSuggestIndex` queries `titles` directly through a trigram index and writes nothing, so abstract write methods would exist solely to be no-opped by the only implementation. The day a second implementation needs them is the day the dual write becomes real and gets paid for on purpose.
- `SearchIndex` keeps `index_many`/`remove` for the semantic half, which genuinely is a written artefact.

### 🔶 3 — `Embedder` has no query/document asymmetry and no verified normalisation contract

**Both halves settled by measurement, and both resolve *against* the current wording.** Full numbers in "Facts" below; ADR-0022 carries the evidence.

**The query/document split is not built.** Measured over 210 paired observations (24 gold documents + 1,200 distractors per draw, 5 disjoint draws, 42 queries): the documented BGE query prefix moves MRR by **−0.0028**, 95% CI `[−0.0259, +0.0203]` — a confidence interval that excludes any benefit larger than +0.02. Applying it to **both** sides is significantly *harmful*: **−0.0663**, CI `[−0.1013, −0.0330]`. The experiment carries a power control — a deliberately wrong prefix moves MRR **−0.2497**, detected at P(>0) = 0.000 — so this is a measured null, not a blind one. Corroborated twice at the library level: sentence-transformers 5.6.1's `encode_query()`/`encode_document()` and fastembed 0.8.0's `query_embed()`/`passage_embed()` are each **bit-identical to plain `embed()`** for this checkpoint, because it declares empty prompts and its `config_sentence_transformers.json` has no `prompts` key at all. Both libraries already offer the asymmetric API and both make it a no-op here.

**So `Embedder` keeps one `embed`. But the docstring's current clause is deleted, because it is the actual hazard.** "Callers are responsible for any query-side instruction prefix their chosen model needs" invites a caller to apply the prefix uniformly through one symmetric loop — which is precisely the −0.066 condition, and nothing in the codebase could catch it. It is replaced by the measured fact: *this model requires no instruction prefix; do not add one; prefixing both sides was measured at −0.066 MRR.* A future model that genuinely needs asymmetry brings a new `model_name`, a new measurement, and a port change made on evidence — which is cheaper than shipping an unused asymmetry today and having every caller guess which side to use.

**The normalisation contract is true, and now verified rather than asserted — but for a reason the docstring never stated.** Norms are 1.0 to within **5.96e-08**, and `normalize_embeddings=False` returns **bit-identical** vectors: the flag cannot turn it off, because normalisation is baked into the *checkpoint* as a third module (`Transformer → Pooling → Normalize`), not applied by the library. Controls confirm it: the same backbone with `2_Normalize` removed returns norms **8.99–9.46**. Three consequences the port must carry:

1. **It is a property of this checkpoint, not of embedders.** A model swap that drops the normalise module silently returns norm-9 vectors and every dot-product score is then ~85× too large. The implementation therefore **asserts the norm on its first batch** rather than trusting a model card.
2. **After the `halfvec` cast the vectors are no longer unit** — norm drift goes from 1.19e-07 to **1.21e-04**, a 1000× change. Anything relying on "cosine == dot" must do so *before* the cast.
3. **The contract is only load-bearing under the inner-product operator.** Verified against real pgvector: `<=>` is normalisation-*invariant* (a vector of norm 5 in the same direction gives the identical cosine distance), while `<#>` is not. With `halfvec_cosine_ops`/`<=>` — what PRD 05 specifies — normalisation buys **speed, not correctness**. The docstring must say which operator it is for, or it reads as a correctness requirement that the shipped index does not actually have.

---

## The collision that has to be settled before any schema task runs

`PostgresTitleRepository._to_domain` (`db/repositories/title.py:80-81`) builds the domain model as:

```python
Title(**{c.name: getattr(row, c.name) for c in TitleRow.__table__.columns})
```

`Title` is a `DomainModel`, which is `extra="forbid"`. **Therefore any column added to `titles` breaks every single read of every title**, immediately and everywhere. The 1:1 correspondence between `TitleRow`'s 31 columns and `Title`'s 31 fields is not a style rule — it is load-bearing, it is recorded as a standing constraint, and the progress log is explicit that it must break loudly rather than be worked around.

A `tsvector` generated column **must** live on `titles`: a generated column's expression may only reference columns of its own table, so a `title_search_documents(title_id, document GENERATED ALWAYS AS (…))` cannot read `titles.name` and the side-table form is not available at all for the generated variant.

So the two requirements collide head-on, and Task 6 resolves it explicitly:

**`TitleRow` grows a declared set of derived columns that the domain deliberately does not model.**

```python
# db/models/title.py
#: Columns that exist on the row and deliberately have no `Title` field.
#: A search document is not domain state -- it is an index artefact derived
#: from domain state, and a `Title` carrying a `tsvector` would put a
#: PostgreSQL full-text type in `usher.domain`, which imports nothing.
#: Membership here is the deliberate act: a *bookkeeping* column added
#: without being named here still breaks every read, loudly, which is the
#: property the 1:1 rule exists for.
DERIVED_COLUMNS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"search_document"})
```

**There are three sites, not one, and the second is the one that gets missed.** Verified directly against the current code:

| # | Site | What happens without the fix |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `_to_domain` — `db/repositories/title.py:80-81` | `Title.model_validate({...})` receives an unknown key and `extra="forbid"` rejects it. **Every read of every title raises**, in every entry point. Loud and immediate. |
| 2 | **`update()`'s mutation loop — `db/repositories/title.py:179-181`** | It iterates `TitleRow.__table__.columns` and `setattr`s each one, excluding only `{"id", "created_at", "updated_at"}`. `fresh = _to_row(title)` never sets the generated column, so this `setattr`s `None` onto it, SQLAlchemy includes it in the `SET` clause, and Postgres raises **`ERROR: column "search_document" can only be updated to DEFAULT`**. This one fires on *writes*, not reads, so a task that only tests reading a seeded row will not see it. |
| 3 | The 1:1 assertion — `tests/unit/test_db_models.py:256` | `set(Title.model_fields) == {c.name for c in TitleRow.__table__.columns}` fails. |

Site 1 filters `DERIVED_COLUMNS` out of the dict; site 2 adds `DERIVED_COLUMNS` to its excluded set (`{"id", "created_at", "updated_at"} | DERIVED_COLUMNS`); site 3 becomes `{c.name for c in TitleRow.__table__.columns} - DERIVED_COLUMNS == set(Title.model_fields)` — so it still fails on an undeclared column, and it *additionally* fails if someone adds a name to `DERIVED_COLUMNS` that `Title` also models.

`_to_row` needs no change: it builds `TitleRow(**title.model_dump(...))`, which never mentions the column. `bulk.py`'s `INSERT … SELECT` into `titles` enumerates its columns by hand, so it is unaffected — **confirm that by reading it, not by assuming it**, because a generated column named in an `INSERT` column list is an error rather than an ignored value.

**Task 6's TDD order is therefore: a failing test that *updates* a title, before one that reads it.** Reversing that order ships site 2.

**Verify, do not assume, that a generated column round-trips through this stack.** SQLAlchemy must be told the column is read-only (`Computed(..., persisted=True)`), Alembic's `--autogenerate` must be checked by eye (it is already blind to CHECK bodies and triggers), and `_to_row`'s `TitleRow(**data)` must not attempt to set it. Task 6's first step is a failing test that reads a title back after an update, not a schema diagram.

---

## Facts this plan builds on, and does not re-derive

Measured, dated, and not to be re-litigated by an implementer. *(Sections marked ⏳ are owned by the task that needs them, named inline.)*

**Substrate** — measured 2026-07-29 against `pgvector/pgvector:pg17`, the image `tests/integration/conftest.py:124` already uses:
- PostgreSQL **17.10**; pgvector **0.8.5** (ADR-0002's floor, met exactly).
- `halfvec(384)` + `hnsw (e halfvec_cosine_ops)` create cleanly. `pg_trgm` **1.6**, `fuzzystrmatch` **1.2** available. `websearch_to_tsquery('english', …)` matches correctly.
- **No migration has ever created an extension.** M6's first migration is the first `CREATE EXTENSION` in this repository — `vector`, `pg_trgm`, `fuzzystrmatch`. The extensions are *available* in the image, not *installed*.

**Schema and migrations:**
- Migration chain head is **`f1a7d3c9e824`** (sweep index). M6's first migration sets `down_revision = "f1a7d3c9e824"`.
- **The revision-id convention is exhausted.** Existing ids spell `a…`→`f…` in chain order and the next letter is not hex. M6 continues with a second cycle keeping both readability and `ls` order: `fa…`, `fb…`, `fc…` — each sorts after `f1a7d3c9e824`, and the second character continues the sequence. Record this in the first migration's docstring so the next milestone does not re-derive it.
- `enum_column` compiles to `VARCHAR` with `native_enum=False` and **no membership CHECK**, so adding `JobKind.INDEX` needs **no migration**. Assert that `test_migration_matches_the_orm_metadata` stays green rather than assuming it.
- **`CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` cannot run in an Alembic migration** — `env.py:52-59` wraps both modes in `context.begin_transaction()`. A HNSW or GIN build over a populated table therefore holds a lock for the whole build. Task 4 decides and records whether the index is created in the migration (locking, simple) or by `usher index --create-indexes` outside one.
- `test_migration_creates_the_updated_at_triggers` asserts the trigger set **exactly**. A new M6 table with an `updated_at` trigger must be added to that literal set in the same commit.
- `bulk.py`'s `_SUSPENDABLE_INDEXES` drops and rebuilds `ix_titles_sort_name` and `ix_titles_name_lower_year` around a bootstrap. **Every new index on `titles` must decide whether it joins that dict**, and record why.
- Every CHECK needs an explicit `name=` — `base.py:26-38`'s naming convention deliberately has no `"ck"` key.

**Jobs, handlers, wiring:**
- `JobKind` has three members; `INDEX = "index"` is one line plus rewriting the docstring at `domain/jobs.py:22-29`, which currently *asserts the member's absence*. `tests/unit/test_domain_jobs.py:86` pins the set and moves with it.
- `Handler = Callable[[Job], Awaitable[None]]` (`services/jobs.py:48`); the registry is a plain `dict[JobKind, Handler]` on `JobWorker`; handlers are factory functions returning a closure (`services/handlers.py:68`). `_title_id(job)` (`:163`) already converts a `Job.key` to a `uuid.UUID`, raising `PortDataMalformed` — an `index` job keyed on `str(title_id)` reuses it unchanged.
- **A loaded model is a once-per-process resource and `build_worker` runs once per worker *pass*.** The precedent is `composition.metadata_provider` (`:353`), and the cost of getting it wrong is on record: ~17,280 log lines a day. The `Embedder` is built by an `embedder(settings)` factory of exactly that shape, and `worker.register(JobKind.INDEX, …)` is guarded the way `ENRICH` is guarded on `provider is not None` (`composition.py:346-349`) — a worker never claims work it cannot run.
- `enqueue`'s upsert already carries `WHERE jobs.status <> 'parked' AND jobs.priority < excluded.priority`, so re-running the backfill costs one index probe per row and **zero writes**, and a `BACKFILL`-priority sweep cannot demote a `DEMAND` promotion.
- **No keyset cursor exists on `TitleRepository`** — it has no listing method at all. `list_unmatched`'s `OFFSET` pagination is measured at 43.7 ms at offset 0 and **388.9 ms at offset 1,126,574**, linear per page and quadratic to drain. M6's backfill is exactly the thing CLAUDE.md says needs a keyset cursor.

**Ports, contracts, fakes:**
- A contract suite is a plain class **not** starting with `Test`, holding `async def test_*` methods that take a fixture the subclass supplies; drivers are `Test<Impl>(<Port>Contract)` subclasses declaring that fixture. Unit driver in `tests/unit/test_<port>_contract.py`; integration driver at the **bottom** of `tests/integration/test_<port>.py`.
- Capability that an implementation cannot express is a **class-attribute flag plus `pytest.skip`** (`job_queue_contract.py:92-110`), never a silently-passing assertion. Arrangement a real backend constrains is a **hook method raising `NotImplementedError`** (`credential_store_contract.py:35-44`).
- Port DTOs are `@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)`. **`ports/search.py`'s existing two are missing `slots=True`** — fix while settling the 🔶.
- New port ABCs must be added by hand to `ALL_PORTS` in `tests/unit/test_ports.py:28-37`; nothing enforces it.
- Every fake subclasses the ABC and carries a counted **"Where this is more forgiving than the real thing, on purpose."** docstring section.
- `adapters/` naming is settled in PRD 01 (`01-architecture.md:35-45`): capability-named directories are correct **even with one implementation each**, because neither `sentence-transformers` nor `litellm` is a nameable single upstream. So `adapters/search/postgres.py` and `adapters/embedding/sentence_transformers.py`. `adapters/postgres/` does not exist and must not be created.
- **Import-linter contract 6 constrains only `usher.adapters.emby`.** A `SearchIndex` implementation in `adapters/search/` is not covered by any existing contract. Task 24 decides whether to add a seventh contract; if so it needs `allow_indirect_imports = true` for the same reason contract 6 does.

**Guards a new milestone trips by accident:**
- `test_every_setting_is_read_by_something` — every new `Settings` field must be *read* in `src/` in the same commit.
- `test_every_usher_variable_in_env_example_is_a_setting_or_compose_reserved` and `test_env_example_ships_the_defaults` — `.env.example` documents every setting at its own default.
- `test_no_dataset_row_is_committed_anywhere` scans **the whole repository including `docs/`**, so **this plan document is scanned**. A previous plan prescribed real IMDb rows and tripped it. Every example title in this plan and in every fixture is synthetic; IMDb-shaped ids must match `^(tt|nm)99\d{6}$` and provider ids must be ≥ 90,000,000.

**Embedding stack** — measured 2026-08-02 on this host (Python 3.13.14, `uv` 0.11.31, Ryzen 7 5800X3D, RTX 4090). Probe scripts at `/tmp/m6-embed-probe/q*.py`; all corpora synthetic.

- **`sentence-transformers` cannot be a dependency of this project.** 59 packages, **2.62 GiB downloaded**, **4.8 GiB installed**, 104 s cold install — against a current `usher` image of **332 MB**. It resolves `torch==2.13.0+cu130`, and **~4.5 GiB of the 4.8 GiB is GPU runtime** (`nvidia/` 2.7 G, `torch/` 1.1 G, `triton/` 689 M) pulled unconditionally on a host that may never use a GPU. PRD 05 names sentence-transformers; **PRD 05 is corrected.**
- **`fastembed` is the shipped implementation, and it is better on every measured axis.** 28 packages, **167 MiB installed** (29× smaller), **1.2 s** cold install, **no torch**, 65 MB model artefact, **252.9 texts/s vs 229.5** on identical input (+10%), peak RSS 1,067 MiB vs 1,381 MiB. Vector agreement with sentence-transformers over 205 documents: **min cosine 0.99999619**, **top-1 identical 205/205**, top-10 mean overlap 1.0000.
- **Two caveats that go in the ADR, not in a comment.** (1) fastembed serves an optimised ONNX conversion from a *third-party* repo (`qdrant/bge-small-en-v1.5-onnx-q`), not BAAI's own weights — a supply-chain fact; BAAI's repo does ship its own `onnx/model.onnx` as the fallback path. (2) The ST↔fastembed vector difference (max pairwise-similarity delta **1.41e-03**) is **6× larger than the halfvec quantisation error**, so the two are not interchangeable without a re-embed. **This is why `model_name` records the runtime as well as the checkpoint** (`fastembed:BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5`): swapping implementations then invalidates every vector through the stale predicate automatically, which is the fingerprint scheme doing its job rather than a migration.
- **The embedder is optional, and a deployment without it still has search.** The dependency lives behind an extra; `worker.register(JobKind.INDEX, …)` is guarded exactly as `ENRICH` is guarded on `provider is not None`. Full-text and trigram — PRD 05's "catalog lookup", the tier serving 1.27M titles — need no model at all.
- **Model artefact and offline behaviour.** `SentenceTransformer(...)`/fastembed pull **~129–134 MB / 12 blobs**; cold load 4.84 s, warm 0.13 s. **Do not use `snapshot_download`** — it fetches 401 MB / 14 files, i.e. three redundant copies of the same weights (`.bin`, `.safetensors`, `.onnx`). **A warm cache is not sufficient for offline operation:** with the cache populated but no network and `HF_HUB_OFFLINE` unset, the load **fails** with `RuntimeError: Cannot send a request, as the client has been closed` — huggingface_hub 1.26.0 reuses a closed client on the retry path instead of falling back to the cache, and the message mentions neither the network nor the cache. Reproduced two independent ways. **CI and the container must set `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` explicitly**; it is also the only setting under which a genuine cache miss produces a comprehensible `OSError`.
- **Throughput is linear in *tokens*, not texts — quote the invariant, not the rate.** CPU holds **~8,000–10,700 tokens/s** across the range (412.7 texts/s at 19 tokens; 83.5 at 100; 18.7 at 516). A realistic `name + overview + genres + keywords` document is **~100–130 tokens**, i.e. **~83 texts/s**. Over all 1,271,138 titles that is **4–6 hours**; over the enriched tier that boundary call 4 actually embeds (2k–10k) it is **~25 seconds to 2 minutes**. Best CPU batch size is **16** (229.5 texts/s at 38 tokens); the curve is flat from 16–64 and degrades at 128.
- **GPU throughput is NOT measured, deliberately.** The 4090 had **210 MiB free of 24,564** — the user's live `vllm` container (Alfred's System 1) holds 21,764 MiB, and creating a CUDA context alone needs more than was free. The probe declined to disturb a running service. Re-run `q5_throughput.py cuda` when the GPU is free. **No decision in this plan rests on a GPU number**, and the CPU figures above are what the backfill is sized against.
- **`halfvec(384)` is correct and effectively free.** Round-trip error over 1,000 real vectors: max cosine error **1.21e-04**, mean 3.03e-05 — three orders of magnitude below the useful signal. **Top-1 and top-5 ordering identical in 42/42 queries**; divergence appears only at top-20+ where scores are already within 2e-4. Storage at 1,271,138 titles: **1.83 GiB → 0.92 GiB**.
- **The deterministic fake is `blake2b → Box-Muller → L2-normalise`, and its non-vacuity is measured.** Over 15,996,000 off-diagonal pairs: cosine mean −0.00001, **sd 0.05102 against a theoretical 1/√384 = 0.05103** (ratio 1.000), max +0.2549, **zero pairs above 0.5**. Near-identical inputs stay orthogonal (`"The Quiet Vacuum"` vs the same with a trailing space: −0.033). Norm error 1.11e-16; 54,017 texts/s vectorised. **Use `hashlib`, never `hash()`** — the obvious `np.random.default_rng(abs(hash(text)))` variant passes *every* contract check and fails only across processes, because `str.__hash__` is `PYTHONHASHSEED`-salted; the cross-process case must be pinned. For tests needing a *known* similarity, plant the angle (`v = cos θ·a + sin θ·b` with `a ⊥ b`) — exact to 2.22e-16 — rather than hoping a hash produces one. A hashing-trick TF-IDF fake was also built and **rejected on evidence**: off-diagonal cosine floor **+0.723**, and it collapses case and punctuation to 1.00000, which is the vacuous-pass failure mode itself.

**The generated column — measured 2026-08-02, and PRD 05's design does not compile as written.**

- **`GENERATED ALWAYS AS (…) STORED` rejects PRD 05's expression** with `ERROR: generation expression is not immutable`. The cause is exactly one function: **`array_to_string(anyarray, text)` is `STABLE`**, not immutable — it takes `anyarray`, so PostgreSQL cannot assume the element type's output function is immutable (for `timestamptz` it genuinely is not). Verified from `pg_proc`: `to_tsvector(regconfig, text)` is `IMMUTABLE` (so the explicit `'english'` is load-bearing and a bare `to_tsvector(text)` would **not** work), and `setweight` is `IMMUTABLE`. **PRD 05's "stored generated tsvector" is a sentence nobody had run.**
- **`array_to_tsvector` is immutable, and it is a trap.** It is the obvious core-function fix and it is *wrong for this purpose*: it emits array elements as raw, unlexized, case-preserving lexemes. Measured on `ARRAY['Sci-Fi','Film-Noir','Drama']` it produces `'Drama' 'Film-Noir' 'Sci-Fi'`, which **fails to match even `websearch_to_tsquery('english','drama')`** — a genre search would silently return nothing. Rejected on evidence; recorded so it is not "fixed" back in later.
- **The working formulation is a custom `IMMUTABLE` SQL wrapper**, which lexizes correctly and does match (`'sci-fi':1 'sci':2 'fi':3 'film-noir':4 …` against `websearch_to_tsquery('english','sci-fi')` → true):

  ```sql
  -- IMMUTABLE is a promise the planner takes on trust. It is honest here and
  -- only here: array_to_string is STABLE because `anyarray` admits element
  -- types whose output depends on a GUC (timestamptz and TimeZone), and text
  -- has no such dependency. Narrowing the signature to text[] is what makes
  -- the promise true -- do NOT widen it to anyarray to "reuse" it.
  CREATE FUNCTION usher_array_text(text[]) RETURNS text
      LANGUAGE sql IMMUTABLE PARALLEL SAFE STRICT
      AS $$ SELECT array_to_string($1, ' ') $$;
  ```

  ```sql
  ALTER TABLE titles ADD COLUMN search_document tsvector
  GENERATED ALWAYS AS (
        setweight(to_tsvector('english', coalesce(name,          '')), 'A')
     || setweight(to_tsvector('english', coalesce(original_name, '')), 'A')
     -- Weight B is reserved for cast and crew and is deliberately absent
     -- rather than filled with something else (boundary call 2). When M7
     -- lands Credit, filling it is a migration plus one forced rewrite.
     || setweight(to_tsvector('english', coalesce(overview,      '')), 'C')
     || setweight(to_tsvector('english', coalesce(tagline,       '')), 'C')
     || setweight(to_tsvector('english', usher_array_text(genres)),    'D')
     || setweight(to_tsvector('english', usher_array_text(keywords)),  'D')
  ) STORED;
  ```

  Verified populated and weighted: a row named `Iron` with genres `{autumn,winter}` stores `'autumn':2 'iron':1A 'winter':3`. **Weight `D` is the tsvector default and is not printed** — worth knowing before someone reads its absence as a bug.
- **Cost: 4.06× on the bootstrap's write path, +33% relation size — about +9.5 s and +80 MB over the whole 1.27M-row catalog.** Confirmed acceptable; see "the one thing" above.
- **`CREATE OR REPLACE` of the wrapper does not recompute stored values**, and a later row update does — producing a silently mixed-state table. Verified. The two mitigations in "the one thing" are mandatory, not advisory.

**Indexes, HNSW and fusion** — measured 2026-08-02 against `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` (PG 17.10, vector 0.8.6, pg_trgm 1.6, fuzzystrmatch 1.2), 300,000 synthetic names and 50,000 `halfvec(384)` vectors.

- **GIN `fastupdate = off` needs no raw `op.execute`.** `op.create_index(name, table, ["search_document"], postgresql_using="gin", postgresql_with={"fastupdate": "off"})` compiles to the right DDL — verified against the installed Alembic 1.18.5 / SQLAlchemy 2.0.51 API rather than assumed, and it composes with `postgresql_ops`. Confirmed via `pg_class.reloptions`, and confirmed *behaviourally* with `pageinspect`: after 5,000 inserts, `fastupdate=off` held `n_pending_pages=0 / n_pending_tuples=0` against `50 / 5000` for the default. That pending list is the p99 spike ADR-0002 names.
- **Trigram: use GIN, not GiST. PRD 05 specifies GiST and the measurement contradicts it on every axis.** Build **579 ms vs 1,965 ms**, size **7,968 kB vs 22 MB**, p50 **9.01 ms vs 21.1 ms** on the autocomplete query shape. GiST's one genuine advantage is index-ordered KNN via `<->` — 0.983 ms on a popular prefix, no sort node — but across 50 varied prefixes that is p50 14.8 ms, still slower than GIN, and bimodal (rare prefixes force deep graph traversal). GIN's exposure is that it must materialise every matching row before the top-N sort; **the capped-candidate pattern neutralises exactly that**, so GIN + cap is the coherent combination and GiST buys tail protection already being bought twice.
- **The candidate cap is provable, not just plausible.** For a misspelled two-word query the CTE returned 1,774 rows and the re-rank filter reported `417 kept / 1,357 removed` — summing to exactly the CTE's row count. **1,774 `levenshtein` calls against a 300,000-row table: a 169× reduction**, and `Rows Removed by Filter` is the line a test asserts on rather than wall clock. `fuzzystrmatch`'s `levenshtein` caps inputs at 255 characters.
- **`pg_trgm.similarity_threshold` has a cliff, and the default sits on the right side of it.** Candidates admitted for one query: 0.1 → 8,020; 0.2 → 5,611; **0.3 (default) → 1,774**; 0.4 → 1,057; **0.5 → 23**. Above ~0.45 fuzzy recall is destroyed. Below 0.3 the cap starts truncating and results become threshold-dependent. Set it with `SET LOCAL` inside the transaction — `SET` leaks to the next checkout of a pooled connection.
- **PRD 05's HNSW recall warning reproduces, and it is worse than "recall collapse" suggests.** At 2% filter selectivity, recall@10 over 25 query vectors:

  | `hnsw.iterative_scan` | `ef_search` | recall@10 | **rows returned of 10** |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `off` (default) | 40 | 0.068 | **0.88** |
  | `off` | 200 | 0.284 | 4.24 |
  | `strict_order` | 40 | 0.100 | 10.00 |
  | **`relaxed_order`** | 40 | **0.508** | **10.00** |
  | `relaxed_order` | 100 (`max_scan_tuples` 200k) | 0.560 | 10.00 |

  **The headline is the row count, not the recall.** With the GUC off, a request for ten results returns **0.88 rows on average — frequently zero**. That is an empty endpoint, not a degraded ranking, and `EXPLAIN` says why: `rows=1, Rows Removed by Filter: 39` — HNSW visited `ef_search` = 40 candidates, the filter killed 39, and the scan was already over. Three non-obvious consequences: **`ef_search` is the wrong lever** (40 → 200 with the GUC off still yields 4.24/10 for five times the work); **`relaxed_order` beats `strict_order` on recall, 0.508 vs 0.100**, because strict ordering terminates earlier to pay for a guarantee RRF re-ranking does not need; and **`max_scan_tuples` (default 20,000) is the real ceiling**, worth 0.508 → 0.560 where `ef_search` 200 → 400 was noise. **Caveat, stated rather than buried:** the probe used uniform-random 384-dim vectors, the worst case for any ANN index, so absolute recall is a pessimistic floor — real sentence embeddings will score higher. **0.56 must never be quoted as expected production recall.** What transfers is the relative ordering and the row-count failure, which are properties of the filter/early-termination interaction, not of the data.
- **Two GUC traps.** `SET LOCAL` cannot take bind parameters (it is a utility statement) — interpolate from a validated allow-list, never from user input. And `pg_settings` returns **zero** `hnsw.%` rows on a fresh connection, one after any query touching a vector operator, because the library loads lazily per backend — setting the GUC on a cold connection still succeeds, so **never feature-detect via `pg_settings` or `current_setting`**; it reports absent when cold and present when warm, which is a flaky-test generator.
- **RRF works as one statement, and all three of its traps were reproduced.** A `FULL OUTER JOIN` of two `row_number()` CTEs at k=60. **(1)** Omit `COALESCE` on a term and a single-lane row scores `NULL`; Postgres defaults to `NULLS FIRST` under `ORDER BY … DESC`, so **every single-lane row sorts above every correctly-scored row and the one id both lanes agreed on lands last** — the single most damaging thing that can silently ship here. `COALESCE(ft.id, vec.id)` is equally mandatory or single-lane rows surface a NULL id. **(2)** `INNER JOIN` reduces hybrid search to what both lanes already agreed on — 1 row against 5 — which is the set you did not need hybrid search to find. **(3)** **Ties are pervasive, not occasional:** two disjoint 50-row lanes produced **100 fused rows with 50 distinct scores — every score a two-way tie.** Without a deterministic tiebreaker, pagination duplicates and drops rows between pages, so `ORDER BY rrf_score DESC, id ASC` is required, and `id` must break ties inside each lane's `row_number()` window too, since `ts_rank_cd` ties are common.
- **HNSW build: 50,000 × `halfvec(384)` at `m=16, ef_construction=64` → 4.109 s, 56 MB (1,170 B/row).** Inferred: 10k → ~11.7 MB / ~0.7 s; 1.27M → ~1.39 GiB / ~136 s. **At 1.27M the graph exceeds a 1 GB `maintenance_work_mem` and pgvector falls back to an on-disk build, 3–10× slower — 7–20 minutes.** **This does not bind on M6:** boundary call 4 embeds the enriched tier, so the shipped index is the ~11.7 MB / ~0.7 s case. The 1.27M figures are recorded as what a future whole-catalog embedding would cost, and as the reason that decision needs `maintenance_work_mem ≥ 2 GB` if it is ever taken.

**⏳ Still open, and owned by the tasks that need them:** FTS latency at 1.3M with ADR-0002's match-set-cardinality claim tested (Task 16), recall@5 per typo class against the real catalog (Task 26, the gate), brute-force cosine at 10k (Task 21), and the staging-lock contention demonstration (Task 22).

---

## AMENDMENT — the full-scale run, and what it overrides

*Added after the plan was written, from a 1,320,000-row / 2,079,982-name / 200,000-vector run on `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` (`shared_buffers=4GB`, `work_mem=256MB`, `maintenance_work_mem=2GB`). **Where this section and a task body disagree, this section wins** — the task bodies were written against a 300k-row probe. Corrections: the image ships **pgvector 0.8.6**, not 0.8.5.*

**⚠️ 1. ADR-0002's gate measures the wrong dimension, and the right one fails. (Tasks 17, 26, 27.)**

A synthetic dry run of Task 26's gate — 604 single-edit typo cases over 34 genuinely short real titles planted in a 2.08M-name corpus with real collisions:

| strategy | recall@5 | transposition | p50 | p95 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PRD 05 as literally written** | **66.2%** | **34.5%** | 181 ms | 241 ms |
| GIN `%` @0.1 | **93.5%** | 82.8% | 582 ms | 1,893 ms |
| **GiST KNN `ORDER BY name <-> q`** | **93.5%** | 82.8% | 281 ms | **342 ms** |
| btree `lower(name) text_pattern_ops` prefix | — (no typo tolerance) | — | **0.12–1.30 ms** | 0.14–18.85 ms |

Recall by title length under the best tuning: **2–4 chars 79.9%**, 5–6 chars 97.5%, 7+ chars 100%.

**Recall is arguable; latency is not.** Every configuration reaching 93.5% has a p50 of 281–582 ms and a p95 up to 1,893 ms. As-you-type needs sub-50 ms. **PRD 05 and ADR-0002 define the gate purely as recall@5 — and recall is the half that passes.** That omission is itself the finding, and Task 26 must measure both. Task 27 records it in ADR-0002's Uncertainty section.

**PRD 05's `LIMIT 3000` with no `ORDER BY` is an active bug, not a simplification.** An unordered cap truncates arbitrarily, so *lowering the similarity threshold makes recall worse*: **66.2% @0.3 → 48.5% @0.1 → 2.6% @0.05**. Any cap must be `ORDER BY similarity(name, q) DESC` (GIN) or `ORDER BY name <-> q` (GiST). Capping smaller does not help — GiST KNN costs 272 ms at `LIMIT 200` against 283 ms at `LIMIT 3000`; the cost is the traversal.

Also measured: PRD 05's own worked examples sit **below the 0.3 default threshold** — `similarity('dune','dnue') = 0.111`, `('her','hor') = 0.143`, `('up','uo') = 0.200`. So `name % 'dnue'` matches *nothing*. "Transpositions are close to a blind spot" is exact.

**⚠️ 2. GIN vs GiST is two questions, and the earlier answer was only right about one. (Task 17.)** At 2.08M names: on the `%` threshold path **GIN is ~110× faster** (1.671 ms / 205 buffers vs 182.5 ms / 31,174), builds in 7.5 s vs 23.1 s, and is 69 MB vs 244 MB. **But GIN has no KNN operator class** — `ORDER BY name <-> q` under GIN degrades to a Seq Scan at **3,989.9 ms**, and GiST KNN is the configuration with the best recall/p95 pair in the table above. Task 17 must decide between them *knowing both*, not treat GIN as a settled win.

**⚠️ 3. The FTS collapse is caused by ranking, not matching. (Task 16.)** ADR-0002's cardinality claim holds and is sharper than stated — at a constant 1.32M corpus, latency spans **0.12 ms → 556.76 ms, a 4,600× range**, driven entirely by match-set size. But `LIMIT 20` *without* `ORDER BY` is **flat at ~0.13 ms at every cardinality**, because the planner early-exits a seq scan. Ranked, the same 650,000-match query is 601.9 ms — of which the index scan is 42 ms and the other 560 ms is fetching 650,000 heap tuples so `ts_rank_cd` can score them. **Ranking has no `LIMIT` pushdown; there is no way to rank without materialising the whole match set.** ADR-0002's own recorded mitigation — cap candidates — is therefore mandatory in the implementation, not optional.

**⚠️ 4. RRF has a fifth trap, and it is silent and total. (Task 19.)** `row_number()` returns **`bigint`**, so `1 / (60 + rank)` is **integer division**: every score becomes `0.0`, the result set comes back in `id` order, and **nothing errors**. It must be `1.0 / (60 + rank)` or an explicit `::numeric`. Also measured: among the top 500 `ts_rank_cd` values for one query the **largest tie group is 498**, so the deterministic tiebreak is needed in *both* the window's `ORDER BY` and the CTE's. And SQL-vs-Python is a non-question — both produced **byte-identical top-20 order on 7/7 query pairs**, with Python marginally *faster* (0.81–0.96×); choose SQL for the single round trip and the 20-row payload, not for speed.

**⚠️ 5. The HNSW numbers in "Facts" came from uniform-random vectors, which is a broken harness. (Task 18.)** In 384 uniform-random dimensions all distances concentrate — nearest neighbour at cosine distance 0.790 against a median of 1.000 — so there is nothing for an ANN index to find and unfiltered recall@10 measured **4.7%**, which is nonsense as a statement about pgvector. Re-run over a 2,000-centroid mixture with a realistic neighbour distribution (nearest 0.110, median 1.000):

| config | no filter | 10% | 1% | 0.1% |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `off`, `ef_search=40` (defaults) | 89.3% n=10.0 | 31.7% n=3.7 | 3.3% n=0.3 | **0.3% n=0.0** |
| `off`, `ef_search=1000` | 99.3% | 97.7% | 82.0% | 8.7% n=0.9 |
| **`relaxed_order`, `ef_search=40`** | 89.3% | 90.7% | 82.7% | **75.7% n=10.0** |
| `strict_order`, `ef_search=40` | 89.3% | 87.0% | 66.3% | 61.0% |

**The conclusion is unchanged and stronger** — at 0.1% selectivity the default returns **0 rows**, and `relaxed_order` returns all 10 at 75.7% recall. `ef_search` cannot substitute (40→1000 reaches only 8.7% at 0.1%). **If Task 18's harness uses uniform-random vectors it will measure noise; use a clustered mixture.**

**And a cheaper answer exists that the plan did not consider: a plain btree on the filter column.** With `ix_embeddings_filter (kind)` the planner abandons HNSW for the selective case and answers by btree + exact re-sort in **0.180 ms at 100% recall** — 113× faster than the iterative HNSW scan *and* exact. **The ANN question only exists inside a selectivity band; below it, index the filter column.** Task 18 must build that index and decide the band deliberately.

**`SET hnsw.iterative_scan` leaks across the pool — verified.** A bare `SET` in one session was still readable from a brand-new session on the same engine after the connection was returned. Use `SET LOCAL` inside the request transaction, or `server_settings` at engine construction. Never a bare `SET`. (Same rule already stated for `pg_trgm.similarity_threshold`, now measured for both.)

**⚠️ 6. `usher.db.staging`'s fix is better than the one Task 22 prescribes. (Task 22, and it reaches Task 9.)** The contention is confirmed and worse than recorded: two concurrent sources serialise **for the length of the other's whole transaction**, not the length of a DDL (measured 846 ms waits, in lockstep, indefinitely). And with **no** leftover table the failure is not a wait at all — two concurrent `CREATE UNLOGGED TABLE` race on `pg_type` and raise `UniqueViolationError`, which surfaces as **`sqlalchemy.exc.IntegrityError`: indistinguishable, to a repository's `except IntegrityError`, from a genuine data conflict.** A healthy batch gets reported as a constraint violation.

**`CREATE TEMP TABLE … ON COMMIT DROP` fixes it in one line per DDL constant**, and was verified against every property the module actually relies on: `asyncpg.copy_records_to_table` resolves it unqualified via `search_path` (proved against a deliberate `public`/`pg_temp` shadow — the COPY landed 1 row in `pg_temp`, 0 in `public`); it works inside `session.begin_nested()`; `inspect(conn).get_table_names()` does not see it, which **deletes the migration-drift failure and the `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS stg_*` cleanup that seven test files carry today**; and `ON COMMIT DROP` is what makes it pool-safe. Two notes: **`CREATE TEMP UNLOGGED TABLE` is a syntax error** (`TEMP` replaces `UNLOGGED`, and temp tables are already WAL-free), and the migration must **`DROP TABLE IF EXISTS public.stg_*` for all six names** or an existing leftover keeps resolving first. The per-call-unique-name alternative also removes contention but leaks one table per committing route, forever.

**⚠️ 7. Two smaller corrections.** PRD 05's "brute-force exact cosine is sub-millisecond" is **true in numpy and false in Postgres at 10k** — measured 1.820 ms (Postgres, seq scan + top-N) against 0.088 ms (numpy float32). The *conclusion* survives comfortably: 1.8 ms exact beats an approximate filtered HNSW scan. But **numpy `float16` is 140× slower than `float32`** (12.275 ms vs 0.088 ms at 10k — no SIMD GEMM path for half precision), so store `halfvec` and **convert to float32 before any numpy dot product** (Task 21). And `fastupdate=off`'s real argument is the read side, now measured: a 1.6 MB pending list cost **231 buffers against 30 — 7.7× read amplification** on the index stage, invisible in `EXPLAIN` unless you look at buffers. That is the "mysterious p99 spike" mechanism (Task 6).

---

## Four traps that silently produce a green suite against wrong code

The first three have bitten this project and are restated because every milestone re-encounters them. The fourth is new and belongs to M6 specifically.

**Stale `.pyc`.** Before every mutation sweep:

```bash
find /home/anirudhlath/code/usher/src /home/anirudhlath/code/usher/tests -name __pycache__ -type d -exec rm -rf {} +
export PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1
```

**The venv shebang trap.** `cp -r` to `/tmp` copies `.venv/bin/*`, whose shebangs point back into the original venv, so a mutation in the copy is never imported and the suite runs green proving nothing. Mutate **in place**, revert with `cp` backups rather than `git checkout --`. **If every mutation in a sweep survives, that is this trap, not a finding.**

**A harness must refuse to classify a run that did not run.** A mutation whose pytest invocation collected zero tests is `DID-NOT-RUN`, never `KILLED`.

**A relevance assertion that any ordering satisfies is not a relevance test.** This is M6's own, and it is the easiest way to ship a search that does not work. `assert title_id in {hit.title_id for hit in hits}` passes against an implementation that returns the whole table in physical order. So does `assert len(hits) > 0`. Three rules for every retrieval case in this milestone:

1. **Assert on position, not membership** — `hits[0].title_id == expected`, or `rank(expected) < rank(distractor)`.
2. **Seed a distractor that a broken implementation would rank first** — a title containing the query term in a *lower* weight class, or a more popular title that does not match at all. A case with one seeded row cannot distinguish ranking from returning.
3. **Name the wrong implementation in the docstring.** A ranking case that cannot say what it fails is a case that will be "fixed" by loosening it the first time it goes red.

The same applies to fusion: an RRF test over two candidate lists that agree proves nothing about fusion. Every RRF case seeds lists that **disagree**, and asserts the fused order is one neither input produced.

---

## What the new contract cases rule out

Each case names the wrong implementation it fails, because a contract suite that passes against a deliberately-wrong implementation is hollow.

### `SearchIndexContract` — new file, run against `FakeSearchIndex` and `PostgresSearchIndex`

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_an_indexed_document_is_findable_by_its_name` | The empty implementation. Weakest case in the suite and the only membership assertion allowed. |
| `test_a_name_match_outranks_an_overview_match` | **The milestone's central retrieval claim.** An implementation that concatenates every field into one unweighted document — which is what you get by forgetting `setweight`, and which no membership assertion can see. Seeds two titles: one whose *name* is the query term, one whose *overview* contains it. Asserts position. |
| `test_a_filter_the_backend_cannot_express_raises` | An implementation that silently ignores an unknown filter and returns a **larger** result set — which reads as working, and is how a two-backend vocabulary drifts. |
| `test_indexing_the_same_document_twice_is_one_document` | A non-idempotent `index_many` that appends, so a re-run doubles every result. Redelivery is safe by construction is a PRD 08 rule, and the job queue *will* redeliver. |
| `test_a_removed_document_is_not_returned` | A `remove` that deletes the vector but leaves the candidate row, so a deleted title keeps appearing with a stale score. |
| `test_semantic_search_uses_the_supplied_query_vector` | An implementation that re-embeds the query itself, which is the whole reason `query_vector` moved onto the request — a backend doing its own embedding is a backend with its own model, its own prefix convention, and its own drift. Skipped via `supports_semantic` where unexpressible. |
| `test_a_title_with_no_vector_is_absent_from_semantic_results_not_last` | The trap in point 3 of "the one thing": an implementation that treats a missing vector as a zero vector, which makes every unembedded title a mediocre match for every query instead of a non-candidate. |
| `test_fusion_produces_an_order_neither_input_produced` | An implementation whose `FUSED` mode returns one list and ignores the other — indistinguishable from working unless the seeded lists disagree. |
| `test_fusion_does_not_add_scores_from_different_scales` | Weighted score addition wearing RRF's name. Seeds a vector hit with a large cosine distance and a full-text hit with a small `ts_rank`, where addition and RRF give opposite orders. ADR-0002: "never by adding scores from incompatible scales." |

### `SuggestIndexContract` — new file, run against `FakeSuggestIndex` and `PostgresSuggestIndex`

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_a_prefix_returns_the_title_that_starts_with_it` | The empty implementation. |
| `test_a_single_character_typo_still_finds_a_short_title` | **ADR-0002's known genuine weakness, asserted rather than assumed.** A pure `LIKE 'prefix%'` implementation, and a pure trigram implementation with no `levenshtein` re-rank. Uses synthetic short titles; the real-catalog version is Task 26's gate. |
| `test_a_transposition_still_finds_a_short_title` | Trigram overlap's near-blind spot, named explicitly in ADR-0002. |
| `test_results_are_ordered_by_popularity_within_equal_distance` | An implementation that returns candidates in physical order, so the type-ahead box's first row is arbitrary among equally-good matches. |
| `test_the_candidate_set_is_capped_before_the_rerank` | An implementation running `levenshtein` over the whole table — the exact latency cliff PRD 05 says the narrow path exists to avoid. Asserted by statement shape / measured work, not by wall clock. |

### `EmbedderContract` — new file, run against `FakeEmbedder` and (marked, opt-in) the real model

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_every_vector_is_unit_normalised` | **The port's own stated contract, which nothing has ever checked.** PRD 05's "brute-force exact cosine" is only equal to a dot product when this holds. |
| `test_dimension_matches_the_declared_dimension` | A model swap that silently changes width and writes vectors a `halfvec(384)` column rejects — or worse, accepts. |
| `test_a_batch_returns_one_vector_per_input_in_order` | An implementation that dedupes or reorders internally, so title *n*'s vector lands on title *m*. The most damaging possible bug in this milestone and completely invisible to a per-vector assertion. |
| `test_the_same_text_embeds_identically_twice` | Non-determinism that would make `source_fingerprint` useless and the backfill never drain. |
| `test_an_empty_batch_is_an_empty_result_and_not_a_call` | An implementation that round-trips a model for zero inputs, which on a GPU-resident model is the difference between a no-op and a stall. |

### `JobQueueContract` — one new case

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_a_small_batch_takes_no_table_level_lock` | Task 22's fix, asserted rather than hoped: the small-batch path must not `DROP`/`CREATE` a shared staging table. Measured through `pg_locks` on a real backend, skipped by the fake via a capability flag. |

## Where each new fake is more forgiving than the real thing

Every fake M6 adds carries this statement in its own module docstring. M3's live run found 40 contract assertions passing against a write-back that had never once worked, because the fake faithfully implemented the adapter's own guess.

| Fake | Where it is more forgiving than the real thing | What closes the gap |
|---|---|---|
| `FakeSearchIndex` | **It has no text analysis at all.** No stemming, no stop words, no `tsquery` parsing, no weight classes beyond the ones it simulates by hand, no `ts_rank` — so "search for *running* finds *run*", "`the` matches nothing", and every real ranking property are invisible from it. It also has no dimension check against a column, no index build cost, and cannot fail. | The identical `SearchIndexContract` runs against `PostgresSearchIndex` on real Postgres (`tests/integration/test_adapters_search_postgres.py`), where the analyzer, the weights and `ts_rank` are the real ones. The residual gap is real-catalog *distribution*, which only Task 26's gate closes. |
| `FakeSuggestIndex` | It computes edit distance in Python over its whole dict, so **the one property the real path exists for — capping candidates before the re-rank — is structurally absent**. Its typo tolerance is therefore *better* than the real one, which is the dangerous direction. | The contract's cap case is skipped against the fake by capability flag rather than passed, and asserted against Postgres through `pg_locks`/statement shape. Task 26 measures real recall on real titles. |
| `FakeEmbedder` | It is a hash. Nothing it returns has semantic structure, so **no test using it can distinguish a working semantic search from one that returns nothing** — similarity between two related titles is noise. It also never downloads, never loads a model, never uses a GPU, has no batch ceiling, and its `aclose()` cannot fail. | Deliberate: unit tests use it for *plumbing* (order, dimension, normalisation, determinism, fingerprinting) and never for relevance. Relevance is asserted only where a real model runs — the opt-in `EmbedderContract` driver and Task 26. Any unit test asserting semantic relevance against `FakeEmbedder` is a defect in the test. |
| `title_embeddings` in unit tests | An in-memory dict has no `halfvec` quantisation, so a vector round-trips exactly where Postgres loses float16 precision. | The measured quantisation error is recorded in Task 3 and the integration suite asserts ranking is stable across the real `halfvec` round-trip. |

Things one task defines and another consumes. Collected here because a dependency recorded only inside the task that happens to mention it is one the next implementer re-invents with a different spelling. **Where a name below differs from what actually shipped, adapt the call site — never the behaviour.**

| Task 9 | 12, 15, 24 | `TitleEmbeddingRepository` (`get`/`upsert`/`count_without_vector`) and the two module-level predicates: `STALE_EMBEDDING_PREDICATE` and the separate refused/`embedding IS NULL` one. **Imported, never restated** — two copies of a predicate is how a backfill stops draining. |
| Task 9 | 11 | `_FINGERPRINT_SQL`. The fingerprint is spelled in SQL because the assembly is per-title and cannot be a bound parameter; **Task 11's Python composer is held to it by a cross-check test**, because these two drifting apart is failure mode (a) of the whole scheme. |
| Task 11 | 12, 20, 21 | `EmbeddingDocument` — deliberately **not** the port's `SearchDocument`. One is a weighted retrieval document for `index_many`; the other is one string for one model plus its hash. Sharing the type invites computing the fingerprint over the weighted form. |
| Task 1 | 16–20 | `SearchDocument`, `SearchFilters`, `SearchOutcome`, `SearchRequest.query_vector`, `index_many`. `search` returns `SearchOutcome`, not `list[SearchHit]` — coverage is only computable in the layer that knows which candidates had vectors. |
| Task 1 | — | `SearchRequest.__post_init__` refuses `SEMANTIC`/`FUSED` with no vector, which **breaks the existing `test_search_mode_fused_is_reachable`**; Task 1 carries the fix. |
| Task 23 | 14, 16–21 | The `search_*`/`embedding_*` settings. **This inverts the obvious order:** `test_every_setting_is_read_by_something` means a setting cannot land before its reader, so **group F runs after C and D**, not before. |
| Task 22 | 9 | The small-batch escape belongs in **`usher.db.staging`**, not only in `PostgresJobQueue` — put it there and `stg_title_embeddings` inherits it for free. |
| Task 9 | 22 | `_UPSERT`'s staging DDL needs an `ordinal` column for last-wins dedup within a batch. |
| — | 12 | `JobWorker.registered_kinds`, a read-only property, so tests stop reaching into `_handlers`. **No task owns this; whichever runs first adds it.** |
| — | 14 | Hoist `composition._nothing` to module scope so both degradation paths share one object. Same rule: first task there adds it. |
| Task 16 | **amends Task 4** | Two contract-file changes no real backend can do without. `given_title_row` widens from a `title_id` to a whole `SearchDocument`, because the indexed text is a fact about the `titles` row and a real index cannot invent it. And a new `owns_document_lifecycle` flag lets Postgres assert `remove`'s vector half through the port while its full-text half comes from `ON DELETE CASCADE` — **a search index that deleted `titles` rows would turn a reindex bug into data loss.** Task 4 writes the suites; Task 16 is where the amendment lands. |
| Task 16 → 18 → 19 | — | The Postgres contract driver ships `supports_semantic = False` in Task 16 (four cases skip), Task 18 Step 1 flips it and watches those four go red, and Task 19 ends the group with **zero skips**. A driver that reaches Task 19 still skipping is a driver that never ran the milestone's most delicate logic. |
| Task 1 | 20, 25 | `SearchRequest.__post_init__`'s refusal means **only the embedder's owner can construct a non-full-text request** — so `SearchService` builds it and the CLI does not. Caught as a real collision between Tasks 1 and 25: a `_search` that passes a `SearchRequest` straight in raises on `--mode fused`. |

**One acknowledged exception to the milestone's central rule.** Everything else here is fresh by construction or carries a per-row fingerprint. `title_neighbors` is neither: it is a batch artefact over a population, and there is no per-row predicate that says a neighbour list is stale — a title's neighbours change when *some other title* gets an embedding. It gets an oldest-row `computed_at()` instead, where `None` means never computed. That is a weaker guarantee than the rest of the milestone and it is written down as weaker rather than dressed up, because the alternative — a freshness predicate that looks like the others and does not mean the same thing — is worse than an honest gap.

*Tasks follow. Each is written to be executed by one implementer working TDD, and reviewed by a reviewer told to mutation-test.*

## Task 1: `SearchIndex` stops being a Postgres wrapper and becomes a candidate generator

`ports/search.py:39` carries a 🔶 naming four defects and telling this milestone to settle them. All four are real, none is cosmetic, and the shape they add up to is the same one: **the port is written from the inside of one implementation outward.** `index(title_id)` is what you write when you already know the index can reach the database; `filters: dict[str, Any]` is what you write when there is exactly one reader of the keys; the missing bulk method is what you get when the only caller is a per-title job; and the missing query vector is what you get when the engine is the thing that holds the model.

The fix is one idea applied four times: **the port takes the facts, never the means of fetching them.** A `SearchDocument` is assembled by the service out of a `Title` it already holds, so no implementation ever fetches back — which is the difference between one statement and 1.3M round-trips on a rebuild. A `SearchFilters` is a closed vocabulary, so two backends cannot invent different key spellings. A `query_vector` rides on the request, so the *caller* owns the model — which is what simultaneously settles who applies the query prefix (🔶 3, Task 3) and what keeps a second engine from acquiring its own model, its own prefix convention and its own drift.

**And the thing that must not be built is `rebuild`.** It is the obvious fourth method and it is a liability: a second path to the same state, exercised only by an operator, whose only test would be its own test. The predicate-driven backfill already rebuilds from scratch by construction — `index_many` over everything the stale predicate returns *is* a rebuild, drained by the same code path production uses every night. A method whose failure mode is "the operator ran the rare path and it had rotted" is a method that should not exist.

**One rule carries more weight than the rest and is worth stating on its own: a backend that cannot express a filter raises, it does not ignore.** An ignored filter returns *more* rows than it should, and more rows reads as working — nothing is missing, nothing errors, the page is full. That is exactly how a two-backend vocabulary drifts into two different meanings for `owned_only` with no failing test anywhere. `FilterNotSupported` is a `UsherPortError` declared next to the port that raises it, the same place `SourceNotSupported` lives relative to `SourceAdapter`.

**Two of the six filter fields name facts a document does not carry, and that is the point rather than an oversight.** `owned_only` is a fact about `media_items` and `min_enrichment` is a fact about `titles.enrichment_state`; neither is in a `SearchDocument` and neither can be, without the document growing into a copy of the row. So *any* engine that stores only documents is structurally unable to express them, and has to say so. That is ADR-0002's whole argument — "Postgres already holds the join" — arriving in the type system instead of in prose.
