## Task 11: The document composer, its fingerprint, and its refusal

A pure function turning a `Title` into the text an embedder receives, plus the
`md5` of exactly that text. Two properties are load-bearing enough that getting
either wrong produces a system that looks healthy and is not.

**(a) The fingerprint is over the same assembled string the embedder receives,
character for character.** If the two ever diverge — the composer strips
trailing whitespace and the fingerprint does not, one joins on `"\n"` and the
other on `" "`, the fingerprint is taken over `title.name` alone "because that
is what changes" — then `source_fingerprint` stops being a statement about the
vector, and the stale predicate never converges: the backfill re-claims the same
rows every pass, the gauge never reaches zero, and nothing raises. So the
composer returns *one object carrying both*, and a caller cannot get one without
the other.

**(b) The composer refuses a degenerate document.** Measured: every
whitespace-only input embeds to the **identical** vector — cos(`""`, `" "`) =
cos(`""`, `"\n"`) = **1.0000, exactly**. Not a bad result; a perfect unit vector
satisfying every clause of the port contract, at cosine 1.0 from every other
empty-document title. An unbounded cluster pinned to the top of every "more like
this" result, invisible to any assertion about norms, dimensions or determinism.

**The threshold is about *empty*, not *thin*.** Name-only skeletons are not
degenerate: measured pairwise cosine between unrelated ones is **0.5867**
(sd 0.055), and a skeleton retrieves its own enriched form at **0.7638** against
a **0.4751** cross-title mean. Crowded, but ordered. So the rule is
`not text.strip()` and nothing more elaborate — a minimum word count would
refuse documents the measurements say are fine, and each refusal is a title
permanently absent from semantic results. It is reachable: `Title.name` is
`Field(min_length=1)`, which admits `" "`.

**The refusal is a written outcome, not a skipped one, and that is the second
trap.** A refused title with no `title_embeddings` row keeps matching the stale
predicate forever — re-claimed every pass, counted every scrape, churning the
queue on rows that can never succeed. **This project has shipped exactly that
bug once**: the watch-history repair carrying the walk's `observed_at` was
refused by the very row it existed to repair and left it matching `played AND
play_count = 0` for good. So a refused title gets a row with a `NULL` embedding
and **the fingerprint of the degenerate text**. Task 9 owns the nullable column
and the predicate pair; Task 12 owns the write; this task owns the two
properties that make the write possible — a degenerate document still *has* a
fingerprint, and that fingerprint *moves* the moment enrichment arrives.

**Why `EmbeddingDocument` and not the port's `SearchDocument`.** That one is a
retrieval document with weight classes, assembled for `index_many`. This is one
string for one model plus the hash that makes its vector's freshness a query.
Sharing a type would invite the fingerprint being computed over the weighted
form, which is (a) failing.

**Files:** new `src/usher/services/search.py`,
`tests/unit/test_services_search_document.py`. (`SearchService`'s own cases live
in `tests/unit/test_services_search.py`; the composer is a pure function and its
suite is separate so a failure names the function rather than the service.)

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_search_document.py  (new)
# `_title(**rest)` builds a synthetic enriched movie -- "The Quiet Vacuum",
# 2019 -- with `rest` overriding. Every title in this file is invented;
# `test_no_dataset_row_is_committed_anywhere` scans it.


def test_the_fingerprint_is_the_md5_of_the_text_that_gets_embedded() -> None:
    """The milestone's central mechanism, asserted against the *string*
    rather than against itself.

    Fails: a fingerprint over anything but `document.text` -- `title.name`, a
    `model_dump()`, the text before a `strip()`, the parts before they were
    joined. All are stable and deterministic, and every one silently
    decouples "the vector is current" from "the text is current", so the
    stale predicate stops converging with nothing raising.

    Spelled as an independent `md5` of `document.text`, never as
    `compose(x).fingerprint == compose(x).fingerprint`, which any pure
    function satisfies.
    """
    document = compose_document(_title(overview="A caretaker inventories a house."))
    assert document.fingerprint == hashlib.md5(
        document.text.encode("utf-8"), usedforsecurity=False
    ).hexdigest()


def test_two_titles_differing_only_in_overview_get_different_fingerprints() -> None:
    """The half that makes re-enrichment re-index. An implementation
    fingerprinting only identity fields passes the case above and fails this
    one -- and in production it means a title enriched from a skeleton keeps
    the skeleton's vector for good.
    """
    assert compose_document(_title()).fingerprint != compose_document(
        _title(overview="Two sisters share one inherited grudge.")
    ).fingerprint


def test_a_whitespace_only_document_is_refused_and_still_carries_a_fingerprint() -> None:
    """The degenerate-document trap and the second trap it creates, together.

    Measured: every whitespace-only input embeds to the identical vector, cos
    = 1.0000 exactly, so a catalog of them is an unbounded cluster at the top
    of every similarity result -- and no assertion about norms, dimensions or
    determinism can see it.

    Two wrong implementations, the second worse. One embeds it anyway and
    ships the cluster. One *refuses by returning `None`*, leaving the caller
    nothing to write, so the title matches the stale predicate forever:
    re-claimed every pass, counted every scrape, permanently. This repository
    has shipped that shape once, one lane over.

    `name=" "` is reachable -- `Title.name` is `Field(min_length=1)`.
    """
    document = compose_document(_title(name=" ", sort_name=" ", year=None))
    assert document.is_degenerate is True
    assert document.fingerprint  # a refusal is writable, so it can stop matching
    assert not document.text.strip()


def test_a_refused_title_gets_a_new_fingerprint_the_moment_it_has_content() -> None:
    """Convergence, asserted rather than hoped for. An implementation
    fingerprinting every refusal to one constant (`md5("")`, a literal)
    satisfies the case above, writes a row, and then never re-claims the
    title however much enrichment gives it.
    """
    refused = compose_document(_title(name=" ", sort_name=" ", year=None))
    repaired = compose_document(_title(overview="A caretaker counts the rooms."))
    assert repaired.is_degenerate is False
    assert repaired.fingerprint != refused.fingerprint


def test_a_name_only_skeleton_is_not_degenerate() -> None:
    """The threshold is about *empty*, not *thin*, and this is what stops it
    drifting. Measured: unrelated name-only documents sit at 0.5867 pairwise
    and a skeleton retrieves its own enriched form at 0.7638 against a 0.4751
    cross-title mean.

    Fails: a minimum word count or minimum length -- the obvious
    "improvement" the first time someone reads the refusal. In production it
    makes every thin title permanently absent from semantic results while the
    gauge reads zero stale.
    """
    document = compose_document(_title(name="Ledgerhand", sort_name="ledgerhand", year=None))
    assert document.is_degenerate is False


def test_the_document_is_deterministic_and_ordered_by_the_provider() -> None:
    """`genres` and `keywords` are tuples in a provider's order, not ours. An
    implementation iterating a `set` produces a different string -- and a
    different fingerprint -- per process, which is `PYTHONHASHSEED` making the
    backfill never drain. Same family as the `hash()` trap the fake embedder
    documents. Four elements, not two: a two-element `set` round-trips.
    """
    keywords = ("house", "ledger", "attic", "inventory")
    first = compose_document(_title(keywords=keywords))
    assert first.text == compose_document(_title(keywords=keywords)).text
    assert first.text.index("house") < first.text.index("attic")


def test_credits_are_accepted_and_are_empty_in_m6() -> None:
    """Boundary call 2: weight class B ships empty because no `Person` or
    `Credit` table exists in `src/` at all. The argument is present so M7
    fills a caller rather than rewriting this, and this case exists so the
    parameter is not deleted as unused before M7 arrives.
    """
    assert "Wren Halloway" in compose_document(_title(), credits=("Wren Halloway",)).text
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the composer**

```python
# src/usher/services/search.py  (new)

"""PRD 03 stage 4, application side: what gets embedded, and its fingerprint.

`services/` may import only `domain/` and `ports/` (ADR-0009), which is exactly
right here: composing a document out of a `Title` is a decision about *meaning*
and must not be able to reach a `tsvector`, a `halfvec`, or a model.

**The fingerprint is over the assembled string and nothing else.** It is what
turns "is this vector stale?" into one SQL predicate, and that predicate has
three consumers -- the backfill's cursor, the `usher.search.embeddings.stale`
gauge, and the test that proves the enqueue-on-enrichment path closes. Hash
anything but the exact bytes handed to `Embedder.embed` and all three go on
answering, wrongly.

**Changing the assembly invalidates every stored vector, on purpose.** Add a
field, change a separator, reorder a section -- every fingerprint moves, every
row matches the stale predicate, and the backfill re-embeds the enriched tier in
the 25 s to 2 min it costs (~8,000-10,700 tokens/s on CPU, ~100-130 tokens a
document). That is the scheme working, not a migration to write. The same
mechanism covers a model swap, which is why `model_name` records the runtime as
well as the checkpoint (`fastembed:BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5`): the measured
ST-vs-fastembed difference is 6x the halfvec quantisation error, so the two are
not interchangeable without a re-embed.
"""

_SECTION = "\n"
_ITEM = ", "


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class EmbeddingDocument:
    """One title as an embedder sees it, plus the hash of exactly that.

    **Deliberately not `ports.search.SearchDocument`**, which is a retrieval
    document with weight classes aimed at `index_many`. Sharing a type would
    invite the fingerprint being computed over the weighted form, which is
    the one way to get this wrong that nothing downstream can detect.

    `is_degenerate` is a flag on a fully-formed document, **never an
    absence**. A refused title still gets a `title_embeddings` row (Task 12)
    carrying this `fingerprint` and a `NULL` embedding, so it stops matching
    the stale predicate and starts matching the `embedding IS NULL` one a
    diagnostic counts. Returning `None` here would leave the caller nothing
    to write and the title re-claimed by every backfill pass forever -- the
    failure this repository has already shipped once, one lane over.
    """

    text: str
    fingerprint: str
    is_degenerate: bool


def compose_document(title: Title, *, credits: Sequence[str] = ()) -> EmbeddingDocument:
    """The text this title embeds as, and the `md5` of that text.

    Pure: same `Title` in, same bytes out, in any process. Determinism is not
    a nicety -- a non-deterministic assembly makes `source_fingerprint`
    meaningless and the backfill non-terminating. Everything below iterates a
    tuple in the order a provider supplied it; nothing iterates a `set`.

    `credits` is always `()` in M6 and is a parameter anyway (boundary call
    2): no `Person`/`Credit`/`Collection`/`Image` table, model or port exists
    in `src/` -- `ports/metadata.py:68-82` defers all four by name -- and the
    only place credits physically exist is `raw_payloads.payload`, so
    assembling them here would put a *provider's* JSON shape in `services/`.
    """
    sections: list[str] = [title.name]
    # Only when it differs: a title whose `original_name` echoes its `name`
    # would embed the same words twice, shifting its vector toward its own
    # title text and away from its description -- the over-weighting
    # `setweight` exists to control on the full-text side.
    if title.original_name and title.original_name != title.name:
        sections.append(title.original_name)
    if title.year is not None:
        sections.append(str(title.year))
    if title.tagline:
        sections.append(title.tagline)
    if title.overview:
        sections.append(title.overview)
    for values in (title.genres, title.keywords, tuple(credits)):
        if values:
            sections.append(_ITEM.join(values))
    text = _SECTION.join(sections)
    return EmbeddingDocument(
        text=text,
        # `usedforsecurity=False` is required, not decorative: ruff's `S`
        # rules flag `hashlib.md5` as S324, and the flag is the honest
        # statement -- this is a content hash for change detection and nothing
        # about it is a security boundary. `md5` because PRD 05's predicate
        # spells `md5(...)` in SQL, the column is sized for 32 hex
        # characters, and a collision costs one un-refreshed vector.
        fingerprint=hashlib.md5(text.encode("utf-8"), usedforsecurity=False).hexdigest(),
        # `not text.strip()`, and nothing more elaborate. Every whitespace-only
        # input embeds to the identical vector (cos = 1.0000 exactly).
        # Measured just as directly: a name-only skeleton is fine -- 0.5867
        # pairwise, 0.7638 self-retrieval against a 0.4751 cross-title mean.
        # The rule is about *empty*, not *thin*; a minimum word count would
        # exclude the majority tier from semantic results while every gauge
        # read zero.
        is_degenerate=not text.strip(),
    )
```

## Task 12: `IndexService` and `index_handler`

The service that turns one `title_id` into one row of `title_embeddings`, and
the handler that is the only thing between it and `JobWorker`.

The handler is a **factory function returning a closure**, matching
`enrich_handler` (`services/handlers.py:68`) exactly — the established shape,
and what keeps `usher.services.jobs` a generic claim/run/park loop rather than a
switch statement over the pipeline. It reuses `_title_id(job)` (`:163`)
unchanged: an `index` job keys on `str(title_id)` exactly as `enrich` does, and
`_title_id` is already the one place a `ValueError` from `uuid.UUID` becomes a
`PortDataMalformed` — without which one corrupted key takes the worker process
down instead of parking its own job, because `JobWorker` deliberately lets
anything that is not a `UsherPortError` propagate.

**Idempotence is mandatory.** PRD 08 says redelivery is safe by construction and
the queue *will* redeliver: `JobWorker.startup()` requeues everything left
`running`, and a process killed between the handler returning and `complete`
committing leaves exactly such a row. Two things make a second run free rather
than merely harmless — the write is an upsert keyed on `title_id`, so the stored
row is byte-identical; and the service reads the stored row first and **does not
call the embedder at all** when `model_name` and `source_fingerprint` both
match. The second is the one a test can distinguish, and it is the difference
between a redelivered job costing one indexed read and costing an inference.

**What parks and what does not.**

| Situation | Disposition | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `job.key` is not a UUID | `PortDataMalformed` → park | `_title_id`, unchanged. No retry parses it. |
| The title id names no row | complete quietly | `handlers.py`'s own rule: work that has become impossible completes, because parking fills the review list with things that are simply gone. **Diverges from `EnrichService`, which parks, deliberately**: an `index` job's other producer is a sweep over the whole enriched tier, so a title deleted between sweep and claim is routine rather than a corrupted key. |
| Wrong vector count, or wrong width | `PortDataMalformed` → park | The upstream answered and the answer was wrong. A 512-wide vector into `halfvec(384)` surfaces one statement later as a constraint error naming a *column*, on a job whose real problem is a model swap. |
| Embedder unreachable, model file gone, database down | the `UsherPortError` propagates → back off | Retrying can genuinely fix all three. |

**The refusal path writes.** A degenerate document (Task 11) produces a row with
a `NULL` embedding, the current `model_name`, and the fingerprint of the
degenerate text, with no embed call. That is the half that makes the backfill
drain, and it is asserted here rather than inferred from Task 15's loop
terminating.

`TitleEmbeddingRepository` is Task 9's port; this task uses `get` and `upsert`.
If Task 9 spelled them differently, adapt the call sites, not the behaviour.

**Files:** new `src/usher/services/index.py`, `tests/unit/test_services_index.py`,
`tests/fakes/title_embedding_repository.py`; modify
`src/usher/services/handlers.py`.

# tests/unit/test_services_index.py  (new)
# Fixtures follow `test_services_enrich.py`: a `FakeTitleRepository`, a
# `FakeTitleEmbeddingRepository`, a `FakeEmbedder`, a recording `commit`, and
# `_given(titles, **rest)` seeding a synthetic enriched movie.
#
# `FakeEmbedder` is a hash, so **no case here asserts relevance** -- that is
# its documented divergence and a test ignoring it is a defect in the test.
# What is asserted is plumbing: what gets written, what gets called, twice.


async def test_indexing_writes_the_vector_the_model_and_the_fingerprint(...) -> None:
    """All three columns, because the vector alone cannot be checked for
    staleness.

    Fails: an implementation storing the embedding and leaving
    `model_name`/`source_fingerprint` at their defaults. Every search still
    works, every vector is still a vector, and the stale predicate matches
    every row forever -- so the backfill never drains and the gauge never
    reaches zero, with nothing raising.
    """
    title = await _given(titles)
    await service.index(title.id)
    stored = await embeddings.get(title.id)
    assert stored is not None
    assert stored.model_name == embedder.model_name
    assert stored.source_fingerprint == compose_document(title).fingerprint
    assert stored.embedding is not None and len(stored.embedding) == embedder.dimension


async def test_indexing_the_same_title_twice_writes_the_same_row_and_embeds_once(...) -> None:
    """PRD 08's "redelivery is safe by construction", plus the half that
    makes it free. `JobWorker.startup()` requeues everything left `running`,
    so a process killed between the handler returning and `complete`
    committing produces exactly this.

    Fails: an implementation that re-embeds on every delivery. It is
    *correct*, and at ~83 texts/s a requeued backfill would re-run the whole
    enriched tier. Asserted on `embedder.embed_calls`, never on wall time.
    """
    ...
    assert await embeddings.get(title.id) == first
    assert embedder.embed_calls == calls, "a redelivered index job re-embedded"


async def test_a_changed_overview_is_re_embedded(...) -> None:
    """The mirror, and why the skip is a fingerprint comparison rather than
    `if stored is not None: return`. That version passes every idempotence
    case and then never updates a vector again -- the milestone's own failure
    mode, a stale index that does not raise, it answers.
    """
    ...
    assert after.source_fingerprint != before.source_fingerprint
    assert after.embedding != before.embedding


async def test_a_degenerate_title_is_written_with_a_null_embedding_rather_than_skipped(
    ...,
) -> None:
    """**The case that catches the non-draining backfill.**

    This project has shipped that bug once: the watch-history repair carried
    the walk's `observed_at`, was refused by the very row it existed to
    repair, wrote nothing, and left that row matching `played AND play_count
    = 0` for good.

    Here the same shape is one `return` away. An implementation that sees
    `document.is_degenerate` and returns without writing leaves the title
    matching the stale predicate forever -- re-claimed by every backfill
    pass, counted by `usher.search.embeddings.stale` on every scrape, with a
    handler that completes successfully every single time. Nothing raises,
    nothing parks, and the queue churns permanently on rows that can never
    succeed.

    Three assertions, and the third is the one the bug slips past: a row
    exists, its embedding is NULL, and its fingerprint is the fingerprint of
    the degenerate text -- so it *stops matching the predicate* rather than
    merely being present.
    """
    title = await _given(titles, name=" ", sort_name=" ", year=None, overview=None)
    await service.index(title.id)
    stored = await embeddings.get(title.id)
    assert stored is not None, "a refused title was skipped, not written"
    assert stored.embedding is None
    assert stored.source_fingerprint == compose_document(title).fingerprint
    assert embedder.embed_calls == 0, "a degenerate document was sent to the model"


async def test_a_refused_title_is_re_indexed_once_when_it_gains_content(...) -> None:
    """The other half of the drain: refused is not permanent. An
    implementation writing a constant fingerprint for every refusal passes
    the case above and then never re-claims the title however much
    enrichment gives it.
    """


async def test_a_title_that_no_longer_exists_completes_rather_than_parks(...) -> None:
    """`handlers.py`'s stated rule, and the deliberate divergence from
    `EnrichService`, which raises `PortDataMalformed` here. An `index` job's
    other producer is a sweep over the whole enriched tier, so a title
    deleted between sweep and claim is routine -- and a park per deleted
    title fills the review list with tombstones.
    """


async def test_a_wrong_width_vector_parks(...) -> None:
    """A model swap that silently changes width writes vectors a
    `halfvec(384)` column rejects, and the rejection arrives one statement
    later naming a column, not a model. `PortDataMalformed` rather than
    retryable: no backoff makes a 512-wide model return 384 floats.
    """


async def test_the_handler_converts_the_key_and_parks_an_unparseable_one(...) -> None:
    """`_title_id`, reused. `uuid.UUID("not-a-uuid")` raises `ValueError`,
    which is not a `UsherPortError`, and `JobWorker` lets those propagate on
    purpose -- so an unconverted key takes the worker process down instead of
    parking one job.
    """

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the service**

```python
# src/usher/services/index.py  (new)

"""PRD 03 stage 4's queued half: one title, one vector, one fingerprint.

**Only half of stage 4 is here, and the asymmetry is the milestone's central
decision.** The full-text document is a `GENERATED ALWAYS AS (...) STORED`
column on `titles`, so no code path -- including a bulk `COPY`, a hand-written
`UPDATE` or a future migration -- can write a title and skip its document. The
embedding needs a model, so it is a job; and jobs can fail, park, or never be
enqueued. Rebuilding the document here alongside the embedding is the obvious
symmetry and is wrong: it makes the cheap, always-correct half depend on the
expensive, fallible one, and a parked embedding job would then also mean a stale
full-text document with the two failures indistinguishable.

So this service does not trust the queue. Every row it writes records *what* was
embedded (`source_fingerprint`) and *by what* (`model_name`), which makes
staleness a predicate rather than an inference.

`commit` is injected because `services/` may depend only on `domain/` and
`ports/` (ADR-0009), and a session is neither.
"""


class IndexService:
    def __init__(
        self,
        titles: TitleRepository,
        embeddings: TitleEmbeddingRepository,
        embedder: Embedder,
        commit: Callable[[], Awaitable[None]],
    ) -> None:
        self._titles = titles
        self._embeddings = embeddings
        # Held, never built. A loaded model is a process-lifetime resource and
        # this service is constructed once per worker *pass*
        # (`composition.build_worker`); building one here would load 65 MB of
        # ONNX every five seconds. Task 14 makes that structural.
        self._embedder = embedder
        self._commit = commit

    async def index(self, title_id: uuid.UUID) -> None:
        """Bring one title's embedding up to date. Raises `UsherPortError`.

        **Safe to call twice with no observable difference**, and cheap the
        second time: the stored row is compared against the current
        `(model_name, fingerprint)` before the model is asked for anything.
        Redelivery is not hypothetical -- `JobWorker.startup()` requeues
        everything left `running` -- and at ~83 texts/s a requeued backfill
        that re-embedded would re-run the whole enriched tier.

        Re-raises rather than absorbing: `JobWorker` is the only thing that
        knows `PortDataMalformed` parks immediately and every other port error
        backs off, and it learns which by catching the exception.
        """
        with _tracer.start_as_current_span("index.title") as span:
            span.set_attribute("usher.title_id", str(title_id))
            title = await self._titles.get(title_id)
            if title is None:
                # Completed, not parked. `handlers.py`: "a job for work that
                # has since become impossible completes rather than parks".
                # The backfill enqueues over the whole enriched tier, so a
                # title deleted between the sweep and the claim is ordinary.
                # `EnrichService` parks here and is right to -- its keys come
                # from a walk that just saw the item.
                logger.debug("index job names a title that no longer exists: {id}", id=title_id)
                return

            document = compose_document(title)
            stored = await self._embeddings.get(title_id)
            if (
                stored is not None
                and stored.model_name == self._embedder.model_name
                and stored.source_fingerprint == document.fingerprint
            ):
                # Fingerprint, never existence. A skip on existence alone
                # passes every idempotence case and then never updates a
                # vector again -- a stale index that does not raise, it
                # answers.
                span.set_attribute("usher.index.skipped", True)
                return

            # **A refusal is a written outcome, not a skipped one.** Returning
            # here instead would leave this title matching the stale predicate
            # forever: re-claimed every backfill pass, counted on every
            # scrape, with a handler that completes successfully each time.
            # The watch-history repair shipped exactly that shape once. The
            # row below carries the fingerprint of the degenerate text, so the
            # title stops matching the stale predicate and starts matching the
            # `embedding IS NULL` one a diagnostic counts -- and the moment
            # enrichment gives it an overview the fingerprint moves and it is
            # re-claimed exactly once.
            vector = None if document.is_degenerate else await self._embed(document.text)
            await self._embeddings.upsert(
                title_id=title.id,
                model_name=self._embedder.model_name,
                source_fingerprint=document.fingerprint,
                embedding=vector,
            )
            await self._commit()
            span.set_attribute("usher.index.degenerate", document.is_degenerate)

    async def _embed(self, text: str) -> list[float]:
        """One vector, checked before it reaches a `halfvec(384)` column.

        Both checks are `PortDataMalformed` rather than retryable: a model
        that returns two vectors for one text, or 512 floats where 384 were
        declared, answers the same way however many times it is asked. Left to
        the database, the second surfaces one statement later as a constraint
        error naming a *column*, on a job whose real problem is a model swap.
        """
        vectors = await self._embedder.embed([text])
        if len(vectors) != 1:
            raise PortDataMalformed(
                "embedder returned the wrong number of vectors", detail=str(len(vectors))
            )
        if len(vectors[0]) != self._embedder.dimension:
            raise PortDataMalformed(
                f"embedder returned a {len(vectors[0])}-wide vector",
                detail=f"{self._embedder.model_name} declares {self._embedder.dimension}",
            )
        return vectors[0]
```

The fake carries the counted **"Where this is more forgiving than the real
thing, on purpose."** section: (1) no `halfvec` quantisation, so a vector
round-trips exactly where Postgres loses float16 precision (max cosine error
1.21e-04); (2) no `halfvec(384)` width constraint, which is why the dimension
check is tested directly rather than through a write; (3) no stale predicate at
all, which is why Task 15's drain is an integration case.

- [ ] **Step 4: Register the handler**

```python
# src/usher/services/handlers.py  (modify)


def index_handler(service: IndexService) -> Handler:
    """`index` jobs key on a `Title.id`, exactly as `enrich` does.

    Deliberately not a variation on `enrich_handler`: `_title_id` is shared,
    so the `ValueError` -> `PortDataMalformed` conversion happens in one place
    for both kinds.

    **A worker holds this handler only if an embedder was built.**
    `composition.build_worker` registers `JobKind.INDEX` under `embedder is
    not None`, the way it registers `ENRICH` under `provider is not None`, and
    `run_once` claims only the kinds it has handlers for -- so a deployment
    without the embedding extra leaves index jobs for one that can run them
    rather than parking work whose only problem is that it was offered to the
    wrong process.
    """

    async def handle(job: Job) -> None:
        await service.index(_title_id(job))

    return handle
```

Add the import and `"index_handler"` to the sorted `__all__`.

## Task 13: A finished enrichment enqueues exactly one index job

One `enqueue` call, in one place, and the whole argument is *which* place.

`EnrichService._apply` (`services/enrich.py:191-222`) already ends with the
shape this needs: update the title, store the hierarchy, **commit**, then
publish `title.updated`. The enqueue goes after that commit, beside the publish,
on the success path only.

**Why after the commit.** The worker that claims the index job reads `titles` in
a *different* transaction, so a job enqueued before the commit can be claimed
and run against the pre-enrichment row: it would fingerprint the *old* text,
store a vector of the old text, and — because the fingerprint agrees with what
it embedded — **stop matching the stale predicate**. A permanently stale vector
the backfill will never re-claim, produced by the enqueue that exists to keep it
fresh. This is the one ordering here with a wrong answer and no error attached
to it.

**Why the success path only.** A failure records `enrichment_error` and leaves
the tier where it was (ADR-0008). The text did not change, so the fingerprint
did not change, so the job would find `(model_name, fingerprint)` already
current and complete without embedding — one claim and one staging round trip
per attempt of a backoff schedule, for nothing.

**Why `JobPriority.BACKFILL`.** Nothing a client renders depends on a search
document, so an index job must never sit in front of a `match` or a
demand-promoted `enrich`. It is also the priority Task 15's sweep uses, and that
is deliberate: `enqueue`'s upsert is `WHERE jobs.status <> 'parked' AND
jobs.priority < excluded.priority`, so two producers at the same priority means
the second writes nothing rather than rewriting the row.

**Per boundary call 5, no new client event is published, and the reasoning goes
in a comment so a later reader does not "fix" it.** PRD 09 says M6 "publishes
`title.updated` through the `EventPublisher` port M5 built rather than inventing
a channel" — and the port is *already* used right here, three lines up. Nothing
a client renders depends on the search document or the embedding, so a second
event on index completion would be an event with no consumer, which
`ports/events.py:40-47` establishes as the thing this project does not do ("no
member nothing emits"). M6 satisfies the roadmap's intent by not inventing a
channel; Task 27 rewords the sentence.

**Why here and not `IngestService._enqueue_followups`.** Its neighbour's
docstring, `_titles_needing_enrichment` (`services/ingest.py:316-340`), makes the
argument: "A nightly walk sees all 1,126,674 items every night. Enqueueing
enrichment for each one makes the queue permanently the size of the library and
starves every demand-promoted job behind it — and the `(kind, key)` uniqueness
would collapse them to one per title, which merely turns '1.1M inserts' into
'1.1M no-op upserts' every night." Every word transfers. **Enrichment completion
is the smaller population and the correct one**: the enriched tier (2k–10k,
boundary call 4) rather than the catalog, firing exactly when the embedded text
changed, which is what PRD 03's stage ordering — match, ingest, enrich, index —
implies. A skeleton title needs no index job at all; its full-text document is a
generated column, so it is already fully indexed.

**`EnrichService` currently holds no `JobQueue`** — a constructor change plus
one wiring change in `composition.build_enrich_service` (`composition.py:303-314`),
where `pipeline.queue` is already in scope.

**The enqueue flushes and does not commit**, per `JobQueue`'s contract, so the
row lands in the transaction `JobWorker._run` closes with `complete(job.id)`:
"this enrich job is done" and "an index job exists" commit together. A third
`self._commit()` here would close the window microseconds earlier and buy
nothing.

**An enqueue failure propagates.** After a successful commit the session is
healthy, so this is close to unreachable — but catching and logging would be a
silently lost index job, this milestone's failure mode in miniature. Propagating
costs one `enrichment_error` on an already-enriched title (the tier is
untouched) that the next attempt clears, and the retry is cheap because
`_payload_for` reads the cache.

`tests/unit/test_services_enrich.py`, `tests/unit/test_composition.py`,
`tests/integration/test_services_enrich.py`, `docs/prd/03-sources-and-sync.md`.

## Task 14: The embedder is built once per process, and the worker never claims work it cannot run

An `embedder(settings) -> tuple[Embedder | None, Callable[[], Awaitable[None]]]`
factory in `composition.py`, shaped **exactly** like `metadata_provider`
(`:353`) — same signature shape, same `(None, no-op)` degradation, same
once-per-process warning, same "the thing and the callable that closes it" pair.

**This is not a style choice and the cost of getting it wrong is on record in
this repository.** `build_worker` runs once per worker *pass*:
`usher.api.lanes._run_worker` rebuilds it on each turn of a loop whose floor is
`IDLE_SLEEP_SECONDS = 5.0`. A `logger.warning` in that function was measured at
**~17,280 identical lines a day** in the default no-key deployment, at exact 5 s
intervals — and that was one string. A model load is 65 MB of ONNX at **4.84 s
cold / 0.13 s warm**; at one per pass the lane would spend more time loading the
model than running jobs, forever, with nothing in the logs saying so.
`metadata_provider` already states the general rule for the `TmdbClient`'s token
bucket ("one client per *process*, not per job or per pass"); a loaded model is
the same kind of resource and gets the same treatment.

**`worker.register(JobKind.INDEX, …)` is guarded on `embedder is not None`, the
way `ENRICH` is guarded on `provider is not None` (`composition.py:346-349`).**
`JobWorker.run_once` claims `list(self._handlers)` and its docstring says why:
"Claiming everything and discovering the gap afterwards would either crash on
the lookup or park work whose only problem is that it was offered to the wrong
process — and a job parked that way needs a human to release it." A deployment
without the extra leaves index jobs pending for one that has it. **Full-text and
trigram — PRD 05's catalog-lookup tier, serving 1.27M titles — need no model at
all**, so that deployment still has search.

**The `fastembed` import is lazy, inside the factory, exactly as `websockets` is
imported inside `connect_websocket` (`adapters/emby/push.py`).** That function's
own comment states the rule: the module is imported on every composition-root
build, and the dependency is one only a single lane needs. Identical here —
`usher.composition` is imported by every entry point including
`usher bootstrap-status`, and this dependency lives behind an extra (167 MiB, 28
packages, no torch; against sentence-transformers' 4.8 GiB and 59 packages, ~4.5
GiB of it GPU runtime pulled unconditionally). A deployment that runs no index
lane must not pay for it and must not *fail to start* without it.

**`HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` must be set, and this is the only place that can guarantee
it.** Measured: warm cache, no network, flag unset → the load **fails** with
`RuntimeError: Cannot send a request, as the client has been closed` —
huggingface_hub 1.26.0 reusing a closed client on the retry path, in a message
that mentions neither the network nor the cache. Reproduced two independent
ways. It is also the only setting under which a genuine cache miss produces a
comprehensible `OSError`. Set with `os.environ.setdefault` **before** the
import, so a one-time cache warm and the container's own value both win.

**The implementation asserts unit norm on its first batch rather than trusting
the model card.** Norms are 1.0 to within 5.96e-08 and `normalize_embeddings=
False` returns bit-identical vectors, because normalisation is baked into the
*checkpoint* as a third module (`Transformer → Pooling → Normalize`). The same
backbone with `2_Normalize` removed returns norms **8.99–9.46**. So it is a
property of this checkpoint, not of embedders: a swap that drops that module
makes every dot-product score ~85× too large, and `EmbedderContract` cannot see
it because it runs against the model the deployment shipped with.

**Settings are Task 23's.** This task *reads* `embedding_enabled`,
`embedding_model`, `embedding_batch_size` and `embedding_offline`; Task 23
defines them and documents each at its default in `.env.example`, which is what
keeps `test_every_setting_is_read_by_something` green. Do not define them here.

**Files:** modify `src/usher/composition.py`,
`src/usher/adapters/embedding/fastembed.py`, `src/usher/cli.py`,
`src/usher/api/lanes.py`, `tests/unit/test_composition.py`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```python
# tests/unit/test_composition.py  (append)


async def test_no_embedder_configured_degrades_rather_than_raising() -> None:
    """The same shape `metadata_provider` has, for the same reason: a worker
    refusing to start without a model would take three working lanes down
    with the fourth. PRD 05's catalog-lookup tier -- full-text plus trigram
    over 1.27M titles -- needs no model at all, so "no embedder" is a
    *narrowed* deployment rather than a broken one.
    """
    built, aclose = await embedder(_settings(embedding_enabled=False))
    assert built is None
    await aclose()  # the no-op half of the pair, callable unconditionally


def test_a_worker_without_an_embedder_registers_no_index_handler() -> None:
    """`run_once` claims `list(self._handlers)`, and its docstring says why:
    claiming a kind you cannot run either crashes on the lookup or parks work
    whose only problem is that it was offered to the wrong process -- and a
    job parked that way needs a human to release it.

    Fails: registering `INDEX` unconditionally and letting `IndexService` hold
    `None`. Nothing raises until a job arrives, at which point it parks, and
    the review list fills with work that is perfectly runnable elsewhere.

    The `ENRICH` half is asserted alongside it, so the two guards cannot drift
    into "one guarded, one not".
    """
    worker = build_worker(
        _pipeline(), _settings(), provider=None, embedder=None, resolve=_resolve, user_id=uuid4()
    )
    assert JobKind.INDEX not in worker.registered_kinds
    assert JobKind.ENRICH not in worker.registered_kinds
    assert JobKind.MATCH in worker.registered_kinds


def test_a_worker_with_an_embedder_registers_the_index_handler() -> None:
    """The control that makes the case above evidence rather than a
    tautology: without it, an implementation registering *nothing* passes."""
    worker = build_worker(
        _pipeline(), _settings(), provider=None, embedder=FakeEmbedder(),
        resolve=_resolve, user_id=uuid4(),
    )
    assert JobKind.INDEX in worker.registered_kinds


def test_the_model_is_loaded_once_across_three_worker_passes() -> None:
    """**The measured failure this factory exists to prevent.**

    `build_worker` runs once per worker *pass* -- `lanes._run_worker` rebuilds
    it every turn of a loop whose floor is 5.0 s. A per-pass `logger.warning`
    there was measured at ~17,280 lines a day; a per-pass *model load* is
    4.84 s cold / 0.13 s warm and 65 MB of ONNX.

    Three passes, not one: a single pass cannot tell "once" from "per pass" --
    the same shape
    `test_the_worker_lane_requeues_abandoned_claims_once_not_every_pass`
    needed. Counted through a loading embedder, not read off the source.
    """


async def test_the_factory_sets_hf_hub_offline_before_importing(monkeypatch) -> None:
    """Measured: warm cache, no network, flag unset -> `RuntimeError: Cannot
    send a request, as the client has been closed`, from huggingface_hub
    1.26.0 reusing a closed client on the retry path. The message names
    neither the network nor the cache. Reproduced two independent ways.

    `setdefault`, not assignment: an operator doing a one-time warm sets
    `USHER_EMBEDDING_OFFLINE=false`, and the container's own value must win.
    """
    monkeypatch.delenv("HF_HUB_OFFLINE", raising=False)
    built, aclose = await embedder(_settings(embedding_enabled=True))
    try:
        assert os.environ["HF_HUB_OFFLINE"] == "1"
    finally:
        await aclose()


async def test_an_embedder_that_cannot_load_degrades_rather_than_crashing(monkeypatch) -> None:
    """A missing extra, a missing model file, a cache miss under
    `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`: all three are `ImportError`/`OSError` at *build* time
    in a process whose other three lanes are fine.

    Fails: letting it propagate out of `create_app`'s lifespan, which turns
    "the embedding extra is not installed" into a server that will not boot --
    the degradation-into-outage trade PRD 08 forbids.
    """
    monkeypatch.setattr("usher.composition._load_embedder", _raising(ImportError("no fastembed")))
    built, aclose = await embedder(_settings(embedding_enabled=True))
    assert built is None
    await aclose()
```

`JobWorker` has no `registered_kinds` today — add it as a read-only property
over `self._handlers`. One line, and it is the difference between asserting on
wiring and reaching into a private dict from a test.

- [ ] **Step 3: The factory**

```python
# src/usher/composition.py  (modify)


async def embedder(settings: Settings) -> tuple[Embedder | None, Callable[[], Awaitable[None]]]:
    """The embedding model and the callable that releases it.

    **Deliberately the same shape as `metadata_provider` above**, down to the
    return type, for the same three reasons.

    *One per process, never per pass.* `build_worker` is called once per
    worker pass -- `usher.api.lanes._run_worker` rebuilds it every turn of a
    loop whose floor is `IDLE_SLEEP_SECONDS = 5.0`. A 65 MB ONNX load there is
    4.84 s cold and 0.13 s warm, every five seconds, forever, with nothing in
    the logs saying so. The precedent is on record here at ~17,280 log lines a
    day for a *string*; a model is not a string.

    *`(None, no-op)` rather than a raise.* `match`, `enrich` and
    `watch_history` need no model, and PRD 05's catalog-lookup tier needs none
    either. A worker refusing to start without one would take three working
    lanes down with the fourth, and a `create_app` that did would turn a
    missing extra into a server that will not boot.

    *This is where the degradation is reported, once.* Not `build_worker`, for
    the reason its own docstring gives: an operator whose index queue never
    drains has to be able to see why, and a per-pass warning is how an
    operator learns to ignore warnings.

    **The `fastembed` import is local**, the way `connect_websocket` imports
    `websockets`: `usher.composition` is imported by every entry point
    including `usher bootstrap-status`, and this dependency lives behind an
    extra (167 MiB, 28 packages, no torch -- against sentence-transformers'
    4.8 GiB and 59, ~4.5 GiB of it GPU runtime pulled unconditionally on a
    host that may never have a GPU). ADR-0022 carries the measurement.

    **`HF_HUB_OFFLINE` is set before that import and it is not optional.**
    Measured: warm cache, no network, flag unset -> `RuntimeError: Cannot send
    a request, as the client has been closed`, naming neither the network nor
    the cache. It is also the only setting under which a genuine cache miss
    produces a comprehensible `OSError`. `setdefault`, so an operator warming
    the cache once can say otherwise.
    """
    if not settings.embedding_enabled:
        logger.warning("no embedding model configured; index jobs will not be claimed")
        return None, _nothing

    # Before the import, never after: huggingface_hub reads it at client
    # construction, and the failure it prevents names neither the network nor
    # the cache.
    os.environ.setdefault("HF_HUB_OFFLINE", "1" if settings.embedding_offline else "0")
    try:
        built = _load_embedder(settings)
    except (ImportError, OSError) as exc:
        # A missing extra, a missing model file, a cache miss under
        # HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1. All three are a *narrowed* deployment, not a
        # broken one, and all three must be legible -- `str(exc)` for the
        # offline case is the OSError this setting exists to produce.
        logger.warning("embedding model unavailable; index jobs will not be claimed: {e}", e=exc)
        return None, _nothing
    return built, built.aclose


def _load_embedder(settings: Settings) -> Embedder:
    """The one line that touches `fastembed`, isolated so a test can replace it.

    Absolute import, so the sibling-named module
    `usher.adapters.embedding.fastembed` does not shadow the third-party
    `fastembed` -- Python 3 absolute imports make that correct, and the
    adapter's own docstring records that it was *verified* rather than
    assumed.
    """
    from usher.adapters.embedding.fastembed import FastEmbedEmbedder

    return FastEmbedEmbedder(settings.embedding_model, batch_size=settings.embedding_batch_size)
```

`metadata_provider` defines its own inner `_nothing`; hoist it to module scope
and have both use it, so the two degradation paths cannot drift. Add `os`, the
`Embedder` import, and `"embedder"` to `__all__`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Guard the registration and thread the argument through**

```python
# src/usher/composition.py  (modify) -- build_worker gains `embedder: Embedder | None`

    # Guarded exactly as ENRICH is, and the symmetry is the point: `run_once`
    # claims `list(self._handlers)`, so a worker with no model leaves index
    # jobs pending for a worker that has one rather than parking them. A
    # deployment without the extra still has full-text and trigram over all
    # 1.27M titles -- narrowed, not broken.
    if embedder is not None:
        worker.register(JobKind.INDEX, index_handler(build_index_service(pipeline, embedder)))


def build_index_service(pipeline: Pipeline, embedder: Embedder) -> IndexService:
    """One session's repositories plus the process's model.

    The asymmetry in the arguments is the whole design: everything on
    `pipeline` is rebuilt per pass, and the `embedder` is not.
    """
    return IndexService(pipeline.titles, pipeline.embeddings, embedder, pipeline.commit)
```

`Pipeline` gains `embeddings: TitleEmbeddingRepository`, constructed in
`build_pipeline` — the front matter's file table assigns the search ports to
`composition.py`, and this is the field `build_index_service` needs. A no-op if
Task 9 already added it.

Both `build_worker` callers pass `embedder=`, build it once beside the provider,
and close it in the same `finally`: `usher.cli._work` (`cli.py:339`), and
`LaneSupervisor`, which already carries `provider` from `create_app`/`_run_lanes`
and gains `embedder` alongside it — `_run_worker`'s per-pass `build_worker` call
passes `embedder=self._embedder`.

- [ ] **Step 5: The runtime norm check, in the adapter**

```python
# src/usher/adapters/embedding/fastembed.py  (modify) -- inside `embed`

        if not self._norm_checked:
            self._norm_checked = True
            # **Asserted, not taken from the model card**, and the reason is
            # mechanical rather than defensive. Normalisation is baked into
            # this *checkpoint* as a third module (Transformer -> Pooling ->
            # Normalize), not applied by the library: `normalize_embeddings=
            # False` returns bit-identical vectors and norms are 1.0 to within
            # 5.96e-08, while the same backbone with `2_Normalize` removed
            # returns norms 8.99-9.46. So a swap that drops that module
            # silently makes every dot-product score ~85x too large, and
            # `EmbedderContract` cannot see it -- it runs against the model
            # this deployment shipped with, not the one it is running now.
            #
            # **Before the halfvec cast, never after**: post-cast norm drift
            # is 1.21e-04 against 1.19e-07, a 1000x change, so the same check
            # over a stored vector fails on a healthy model.
            norm = math.sqrt(sum(value * value for value in vectors[0]))
            if abs(norm - 1.0) > _NORM_TOLERANCE:
                raise PortDataMalformed(
                    f"{self.model_name} returned a vector of norm {norm:.4f}, not 1.0",
                    detail="this checkpoint's Normalize module is missing or was replaced",
                )
```

`_NORM_TOLERANCE = 1e-4` — comfortably above the measured 5.96e-08 and
comfortably below the 8.99 a missing module produces. Worth recording alongside
it: the contract is only load-bearing under the inner-product operator anyway.
Verified against real pgvector, `<=>` is normalisation-*invariant* while `<#>`
is not, so with the `halfvec_cosine_ops`/`<=>` index PRD 05 specifies,
normalisation buys speed rather than correctness.

## Task 15: `usher index`, and a backfill that drains

The operator-facing half. `usher index` reports what is stale;
`usher index --backfill` enqueues one `index` job per stale title until there
are none left.

It follows `cli.py`'s established shape exactly, because every deviation is a
place where the two composition roots drift: a `sub.add_parser` in
`build_parser`, an `elif` arm in `main`, an `async def _index(settings, …)`
using `_session_for` + `build_pipeline`, and reporting through `print()` and
never `logger` — `_status` says why ("this is a report an operator asked for,
and routing it through the JSON log sink would make it unreadable at a
terminal").

**The population is `enrichment_state <> 'skeleton'`, per boundary call 4**, and
`ix_titles_enrichment_state` is *already exactly that partial index*
(`db/models/title.py:181-185`). This is PRD 05's own two-workload split taken
seriously: catalog lookup is full-text plus trigram over everything, and the
semantic tier is the library experience at 2k–10k. A skeleton needs no index job
at all — its document is a generated column, so it is already fully searchable —
and embedding it would produce a vector of its name, which full-text does better
and cheaper.

**A keyset cursor, not `OFFSET`.** `list_unmatched`'s `OFFSET` pagination is
measured in this repository at **43.7 ms at offset 0 and 388.9 ms at offset
1,126,574** — linear per page and therefore quadratic to drain. CLAUDE.md
already records the conclusion ("a keyset cursor is the fix when something needs
one"); this backfill is the something. The cursor is `titles.id`, a UUIDv7
minted at insert time: unique, so no page boundary drops or repeats a row, and
monotonic in insert order, so the pages walk roughly in heap order.

**The cursor advances on `id`, never on the predicate, and that is what makes it
drain.** A loop spelled "re-read the stale set, enqueue it, repeat until empty"
does not terminate against a row the predicate cannot clear. This repository has
shipped exactly that non-convergence once, in the watch-history repair. A keyset
cursor cannot loop, because each pass starts strictly after the last id it saw,
whatever the predicate did.

**Re-running writes zero rows, and that is proved rather than asserted.**
`enqueue`'s upsert carries `WHERE jobs.status <> 'parked' AND jobs.priority <
excluded.priority`, so a second sweep at `BACKFILL` over jobs already at
`BACKFILL` costs one index probe per row and no writes — the same clause that
stops a nightly walk rewriting the whole queue for no state change. **The test
must be an integration test**: `FakeJobQueue.enqueue` counts a no-op re-enqueue
as a row written and Postgres answers 0, recorded in CLAUDE.md as the fake's
seventh divergence. A unit case would assert the opposite of the truth and pass.

**Sizing, quoted as the invariant rather than as a rate.** Throughput is linear
in *tokens*, not texts: CPU holds **~8,000–10,700 tokens/s** across the range
(412.7 texts/s at 19 tokens; 83.5 at 100; 18.7 at 516). A realistic
`name + overview + genres + keywords` document is **~100–130 tokens**. So the
enriched tier boundary call 4 embeds (2k–10k titles) is **~25 seconds to 2
minutes**. Over all 1,271,138 titles it would be 4–6 hours, which is the number
boundary call 4 exists to avoid paying. Quote tokens/s in the help text and the
docstring, never a texts/s rate: a document twice as long costs twice as much,
and a rate hides that. **GPU throughput is deliberately not measured** — the
probe found 210 MiB free of 24,564 and declined to disturb a running service.

**Which repository.** The front-matter file table puts the keyset cursor on
`db/repositories/title.py`, and that is right: the driving table and the cursor
key are both `titles`. The stale predicate itself is Task 9's — **imported from
`usher.db.repositories.search` rather than restated**, because two copies of a
staleness rule is how the backfill and the `usher.search.embeddings.stale` gauge
come to disagree about what they are counting.

**Files:** modify `src/usher/cli.py`, `src/usher/ports/repository.py`,
`src/usher/db/repositories/title.py`, `tests/fakes/title_repository.py`,
`tests/unit/test_cli.py`, `README.md`,
`docs/prd/05-search-and-similarity.md`; new
`tests/integration/test_index_backfill.py`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```python
# tests/integration/test_index_backfill.py  (new)
# Two things live here and nowhere else. `FakeJobQueue.enqueue` counts a
# no-op re-enqueue as a row written while `_ENQUEUE`'s `WHERE jobs.priority <
# excluded.priority` makes Postgres answer 0 -- the fake's seventh divergence
# -- so the zero-rows property is only observable here. And the stale
# predicate is a join over `title_embeddings`, which a dict cannot answer.


async def test_the_backfill_enqueues_one_index_job_per_stale_enriched_title(...) -> None:
    """Boundary call 4, asserted on the *population* rather than on a count.

    Seeds three titles: enriched with no embedding (stale), enriched with a
    current embedding (not stale), and a skeleton (out of the population
    entirely -- its full-text document is a generated column, so it is
    already fully indexed).

    Fails: an implementation sweeping every title. On the measured catalog
    that is 1,271,138 jobs against ~10,000, and 4-6 hours against 25 s-2 min.
    """


async def test_re_running_the_backfill_writes_zero_rows(...) -> None:
    """`enqueue`'s upsert already carries `WHERE jobs.status <> 'parked' AND
    jobs.priority < excluded.priority`, so a second sweep at BACKFILL over
    jobs already at BACKFILL costs one index probe per row and no writes.

    **This cannot be written against `FakeJobQueue`**, which counts a no-op
    re-enqueue as a row written -- it would pass and assert the opposite of
    the truth. Same reason `test_services_titles.py`'s promotion case needed
    a real backend.

    Without this the backfill is not re-runnable: nightly it would produce
    ~10,000 dead-weight row versions plus the WAL and the vacuum, on a table
    whose entire purpose is to stay small.
    """
    first = await _index_backfill(session, settings)
    second = await _index_backfill(session, settings)
    assert first > 0
    assert second == 0, "a re-run wrote rows; the upsert predicate is not doing its job"


async def test_the_backfill_drains_across_pages_and_terminates(...) -> None:
    """Seven stale titles, three per page: empty in exactly three passes.

    **The case that catches the non-draining backfill.** An implementation
    re-reading the predicate each pass instead of advancing a cursor is
    indistinguishable from this one while every row leaves the predicate --
    and loops forever the moment one does not. This repository has shipped
    that: the watch-history repair carrying the walk's instant was refused by
    the row it existed to repair and matched `played AND play_count = 0`
    forever.

    Bounded with `asyncio.wait_for`, so a non-converging loop fails the case
    rather than hanging the suite -- the same bound
    `test_the_bounded_backfill_terminates` uses one lane over, and the reason
    `pytest-timeout` is deliberately not a dependency.
    """


async def test_the_cursor_is_a_keyset_and_not_an_offset(...) -> None:
    """Asserted on the statement, not the clock. OFFSET pagination is 43.7 ms
    at offset 0 and 388.9 ms at offset 1,126,574 -- linear per page,
    quadratic to drain -- and a timing assertion at fixture scale cannot tell
    the two apart. Captured off `before_cursor_execute` (never transcribed --
    a hand-copied lookalike drifts and then reads like coverage) and asserted
    to carry `id > ` and no `OFFSET`.
    """


async def test_a_refused_title_leaves_the_backfill_after_one_pass(...) -> None:
    """Task 12's refusal case through the real predicate: a degenerate title
    is claimed once, written with a NULL embedding and the fingerprint of its
    degenerate text, and the *next* sweep does not see it. The failing
    implementation is Task 12's skip-without-writing, and here it shows up as
    a sweep enqueueing the same title every night forever while
    `usher.search.embeddings.stale` never reaches zero.
    """
```

```python
# src/usher/ports/repository.py  (modify, on TitleRepository)

    @abstractmethod
    async def list_stale_for_index(
        self, model_name: str, *, after: uuid.UUID | None = None, limit: int = 1000
    ) -> list[uuid.UUID]:
        """Ids of enriched titles whose embedding is missing or out of date,
        in `id` order, strictly after `after`.

        **A keyset cursor, never an `OFFSET`.** `list_unmatched` pages with
        `OFFSET` and is measured at 43.7 ms at offset 0 against 388.9 ms at
        offset 1,126,574 -- linear per page, quadratic to drain. Acceptable
        for an operator reading the first pages of a review queue; wrong for
        anything that walks a whole population.

        `after` is exclusive and the result is ordered, so a caller drains by
        passing back the last id it saw. **The cursor advances on `id`, not on
        the predicate**: a caller re-reading "everything stale" each pass
        would not terminate against a row the predicate cannot clear, which is
        the failure the watch-history repair shipped.

        **Ids, not `Title`s.** The only consumer enqueues `str(title_id)`; a
        10,000-row sweep returning whole titles would pull 31 columns per row
        for one of them.

        The population is `enrichment_state <> 'skeleton'` (boundary call 4;
        `ix_titles_enrichment_state` is already exactly that partial index). A
        skeleton's search document is a generated column, so it is already
        fully indexed and needs no job.

        `model_name` is an argument rather than a constant because it is half
        the staleness question: a swap from sentence-transformers to fastembed
        changes vectors by 1.41e-03 max pairwise delta -- 6x the halfvec
        quantisation error -- so the two are not interchangeable, and
        recording the runtime alongside the checkpoint is what makes the swap
        invalidate every vector automatically instead of by migration.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def count_stale_for_index(self, model_name: str) -> int:
        """How many rows `list_stale_for_index` would eventually yield.

        *The same predicate*, imported rather than spelled again -- a
        diagnostic that disagreed with the sweep it reports on is worse than
        no diagnostic.
        """
```

```python
# src/usher/db/repositories/title.py  (modify)

# The stale predicate, imported from where Task 9 defines it rather than
# spelled again. Two copies of a staleness rule is how the backfill and the
# `usher.search.embeddings.stale` gauge come to disagree about what they are
# counting -- and its fingerprint half must stay byte-identical to
# `usher.services.search.compose_document`'s assembly or nothing converges.
from usher.db.repositories.search import STALE_EMBEDDING_PREDICATE

_STALE_FOR_INDEX = f"""
SELECT t.id
FROM titles AS t
LEFT JOIN title_embeddings AS e ON e.title_id = t.id
-- Boundary call 4, and `ix_titles_enrichment_state` is already exactly this
-- partial index -- so this clause is the index's own predicate rather than a
-- filter applied after a scan.
WHERE t.enrichment_state <> 'skeleton'
  AND ({STALE_EMBEDDING_PREDICATE})
  -- The keyset. `CAST(... AS uuid)`, never the colon-colon spelling:
  -- SQLAlchemy's bind-parameter regex treats a name immediately followed by a
  -- double colon as a Postgres cast and skips the bind entirely, and asyncpg
  -- then answers `PostgresSyntaxError: syntax error at or near ":"`.
  AND (:after IS NULL OR t.id > CAST(:after AS uuid))
ORDER BY t.id
LIMIT :limit
"""
```

**Do not write a colon-prefixed name inside a comment in this statement.** That
same regex scans SQL comments, so a comment quoting a parameter spelling
declares a real bind parameter and every call raises `InvalidRequestError: A
value is required for bind parameter` — with the offending token visible only
inside a comment nobody reads when debugging a bind error. The comments above
use unprefixed spellings for exactly that reason.

Add both methods to `FakeTitleRepository`, plus a counted line in its "Where
this is more forgiving than the real thing, on purpose." section: **it has no
`title_embeddings` join and no `ORDER BY` the database chose**, so its page
boundaries are Python's and the zero-rows-on-rerun property is not expressible
against it at all.

- [ ] **Step 4: The command**

```python
# src/usher/cli.py  (modify)


async def _index(settings: Settings, *, backfill: bool, limit: int, page_size: int) -> None:
    """Report the search index's freshness, or enqueue the work that fixes it.

    **The bare form only reads**, so it is safe on a production box while
    diagnosing something. `--backfill` is the writing form and it is one
    `enqueue` per stale title, never an inline embed: the worker owns the
    model (`composition.embedder`), and a CLI that embedded would load 65 MB
    of ONNX in a process whose job is to print two numbers.

    **Sized in tokens, because throughput is linear in tokens and not in
    texts.** CPU holds ~8,000-10,700 tokens/s across the whole range and a
    realistic `name + overview + genres + keywords` document is ~100-130
    tokens, so the enriched tier boundary call 4 embeds (2k-10k titles) is
    ~25 seconds to 2 minutes of worker time. Over all 1,271,138 titles it
    would be 4-6 hours, which is the number boundary call 4 avoids paying.

    **Re-running is free.** `enqueue`'s upsert carries `WHERE jobs.status <>
    'parked' AND jobs.priority < excluded.priority`, so a second sweep over
    jobs already at BACKFILL costs one index probe per row and writes nothing.
    The reported count is rows *written*, which is the honest number and is 0
    on a second run.
    """
    async with _session_for(settings) as session:
        pipeline = build_pipeline(session, settings)
        # The model is not loaded here, and this line is what says so:
        # staleness is a question about a *name*, which is exactly what
        # recording `model_name` on the row bought.
        model = settings.embedding_model
        stale = await pipeline.titles.count_stale_for_index(model)
        if not backfill:
            refused = await pipeline.embeddings.count_without_vector(model)
            print(f"model: {model}")
            print(f"stale embeddings: {stale}")
            print(f"refused (no content to embed): {refused}")
            # ~115 tokens a document at ~8,000-10,700 tokens/s on CPU. A range
            # derived from the invariant rather than a texts/s rate: a
            # document twice as long costs twice as much, and a rate hides it.
            print(f"estimated worker time: {stale * 115 / 10700:.0f}-{stale * 115 / 8000:.0f}s")
            return

        written = seen = 0
        after: uuid.UUID | None = None
        while True:
            page = await pipeline.titles.list_stale_for_index(model, after=after, limit=page_size)
            if not page:
                break
            written += await pipeline.queue.enqueue(
                [
                    JobRequest(kind=JobKind.INDEX, key=str(one), priority=JobPriority.BACKFILL)
                    for one in page
                ]
            )
            await session.commit()
            seen += len(page)
            # **The cursor advances on the last id of the page, always** --
            # never on "how many were still stale afterwards". A loop that
            # re-asked the predicate would not terminate against a row the
            # predicate cannot clear, and this repository has shipped exactly
            # that non-convergence once, in the watch-history repair.
            after = page[-1]
            if limit and seen >= limit:
                break
        print(f"{seen} stale titles swept, {written} index jobs written")
```

```python
# src/usher/cli.py  (modify) -- build_parser, then an `elif` arm in `main`

    index = sub.add_parser("index", help="report search-index freshness, or enqueue the work")
    index.add_argument(
        "--backfill",
        action="store_true",
        help="enqueue one index job per stale title (the bare form only reads)",
    )
    index.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=0, help="stop after N titles; 0 drains")
    index.add_argument("--page-size", type=int, default=1000)
```

`pipeline.embeddings.count_without_vector(model)` is Task 9's — the
`embedding IS NULL` half of the predicate pair its own step defines. If it
spelled the method differently, adapt this call site.

| drop `AND t.enrichment_state <> 'skeleton'` | `test_the_backfill_enqueues_one_index_job_per_stale_enriched_title` |
| `priority=JobPriority.NEW` | `test_re_running_the_backfill_writes_zero_rows` — at NEW the second sweep *promotes* the row and writes |
| `ORDER BY t.updated_at` | page boundaries stop being unique, so rows repeat and drop; the drain case sees the wrong count |
| delete the in-loop `session.commit()` | *survives the unit suite*; the integration drain case sees one transaction hold every claimed row until the sweep ends |
| print `seen` where `written` is printed | the real-run check in Step 5; add a CLI-output assertion if that is not enough |

## Task 16: `PostgresSearchIndex`, and a rank that is not a coin flip

Group A settled what crosses the port and built the doubles; group B built the column and the tables. This task is the first implementation of `SearchIndex` that a household would actually search, and it lands PRD 05's **catalog-lookup tier** — the one that serves all 1,271,138 titles, needs no model, and is what a deployment with `USHER_INDEX_ENABLED=false` still has.

**The write half is almost nothing, and that is the generated column being paid for rather than merely described.** `index_many` on this backend writes *vectors*. It does not write text, cannot write text, and must not try: `titles.search_document` is `GENERATED ALWAYS AS (…) STORED`, recomputed by PostgreSQL inside the same statement that writes `name` or `overview`. An `index_many` whose text half is a no-op is normally the bug you are hunting; here it is the guarantee, and this task pins it with a case that **renames a title and searches for the new name without indexing anything** — which fails against any implementation that has quietly started maintaining its own copy.

**The one thing this write half must not do is claim freshness it cannot verify.** `SearchDocument` carries text and a vector; it does not carry `source_fingerprint`, because the port has no staleness concept and no business acquiring one. So a row written through `index_many` gets `model_name = ''` and `source_fingerprint = ''` — sentinels that are `IS DISTINCT FROM` every real model name, so the row **matches the stale predicate on purpose** and the backfill re-claims it exactly once. The alternative is worse in the way this milestone is about: writing the current `model_name` and an `md5` of text nobody embedded produces a row that *asserts* it is current, a permanently wrong vector, and a stale predicate that will never look at it again. The port's generic path can only ever add work; it can never hide staleness. `IndexService` (Task 12) remains the only writer that knows what text produced a vector, and is therefore the only writer allowed to say so.

**The central retrieval claim of the milestone lives in the read half, and no membership assertion can see it.** `assert title_id in {hit.title_id for hit in hits}` passes against an implementation that returns the whole table in physical order. What separates a search from a table scan is that **a name match outranks an overview match**, and that property is one `setweight` call and one weight array away from being silently absent. `ts_rank_cd(ARRAY[0.1, 0.2, 0.4, 1.0], …)` — D, C, B, A, PostgreSQL's own order — is spelled out rather than defaulted, so a later tuning is a diff instead of a discovery. Weight class B is `0.4` and contributes exactly nothing today because it is reserved and empty (boundary call 2); leaving the slot in the array is what makes M7's `Credit` landing a migration rather than a rewrite.

**The filter vocabulary is expressed in full — which moves `FilterNotSupported` to where the real hazard is.** This backend can express all six members, and `owned_only` and `min_enrichment` are exactly the two the port's docstring says a document-only engine cannot: ADR-0002's "Postgres already holds the join", arriving as an `EXISTS` over `media_items` and a predicate on `titles.enrichment_state`. So the contract's raise-half skips here, by its own design, and the raise is instead wired to the failure that is actually reachable: **a member added to `SearchFilters` in a later milestone that this backend was never taught about.** The translation is a table keyed by field name and driven by `dataclasses.fields(SearchFilters)`, so an untranslated member raises rather than being dropped — because a dropped filter returns *more* rows, and more rows reads as working.

Two translations are traps rather than transcriptions:

- **`min_enrichment` must not be a string comparison.** `EnrichmentState` is a `StrEnum` and `"enriched" < "skeleton" < "stub"`, so `enrichment_state >= 'stub'` in SQL returns *skeletons and stubs and not enriched titles* — the exact inversion `ENRICHMENT_RANK` exists to prevent, already recorded against `EnrichService` where it silently promoted nothing. The translator expands the ladder in Python to the set of states at or above the requested rung and emits `= ANY(...)`, so the ordering question never reaches SQL at all.
- **`owned_only` is an `EXISTS`, never a `JOIN`.** `media_items.title_id` carries the *series'* id on every episode row, and one measured series has 20,000 of them: `list_for_title`'s own numbers are 1 row / 0.251 ms / 21 buffers with `episode_id IS NULL` against 20,001 rows / 22.901 ms / 402 buffers without it. A join here would multiply every owned series by its episode count before the `LIMIT` ever ran. An `EXISTS` stops at the first row and cannot.

**And `remove` is where this backend's shape genuinely diverges from the port's, so the divergence is declared rather than smoothed over.** The port says "drop a title from the index, text and vector together", and names the failure it exists to prevent: a `remove` that clears the vector and leaves the candidate row. On Postgres the vector is a `title_embeddings` row this adapter owns, and the candidate row *is the `titles` row*, which it does not own and must not delete — a search index that deletes catalog rows turns a reindex bug into data loss. So `SearchIndexContract` grows one capability flag, `owns_document_lifecycle`, this driver sets it `False`, and the property does not go untested: the full-text half is asserted on the real backend through the mechanism that actually owns it, `ON DELETE CASCADE` from `titles`, plus a Postgres-only case pinning that `remove` drops the vector row and leaves the catalog exactly where it was.

**One contract-file amendment is load-bearing and belongs here rather than in a follow-up.** `SearchIndexContract.given_title_row(title_id)` takes only an id, and no real backend can satisfy it: on Postgres the document's *text* is a fact about the `titles` row, and a hook handed only an id cannot land it. The hook widens to take the whole `SearchDocument`. The fake's driver loses one line and gains nothing, which is the correct asymmetry — a hook exists for the constraint a real backend has.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/adapters/search/__init__.py`
- Create: `src/usher/adapters/search/postgres.py`
- Create: `tests/integration/test_adapters_search_postgres.py`
- Modify: `tests/contract/search_index_contract.py`
- Modify: `tests/unit/test_search_index_contract.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Amend the contract for the hook and the lifecycle split**

```python
# tests/contract/search_index_contract.py  (modify)

class SearchIndexContract:
    supports_semantic: bool = False
    unsupported_filter: str | None = None

    # Whether this backend owns the *document's* lifecycle as well as the
    # vector's. `FakeSearchIndex` and any document store do: the document is
    # a row they wrote and can delete. `PostgresSearchIndex` does not --
    # `titles.search_document` is a generated column of a table the catalog
    # owns, so a title that exists is full-text indexed by construction, and
    # an index that deleted `titles` rows to satisfy `remove` would turn a
    # reindex bug into data loss.
    #
    # Declared `True` by default so the *stronger* assertion is what a new
    # driver gets for free and weakening it is a visible line in a subclass.
    # The Postgres driver pays for the exemption with
    # `test_deleting_the_title_removes_it_from_full_text`, which asserts the
    # same property through the mechanism that really owns it.
    owns_document_lifecycle: bool = True

    async def given_title_row(self, document: SearchDocument) -> None:
        """Arrangement a real index constrains and a dict does not.

        **Takes the whole document, not just its id.** A hook handed an id
        alone is one no real backend can satisfy: on Postgres the document's
        text is a fact about the `titles` row -- `search_document` is
        `GENERATED ALWAYS AS (...) STORED` over `name`, `overview`, `genres`
        and `keywords` -- so a driver that could only insert an id would seed
        an empty document and every retrieval case in this file would assert
        against blank text.

        `FakeSearchIndex` is a dict with no foreign keys and needs nothing.
        """
        raise NotImplementedError

    async def index_all(self, index: SearchIndex, documents: Sequence[SearchDocument]) -> None:
        for document in documents:
            await self.given_title_row(document)
        await index.index_many(documents)
```

and the removal case, which now names both halves and which half each driver is asserting:

```python
    async def test_a_removed_document_is_not_returned(self, index: SearchIndex) -> None:
        """A `remove` that drops one half of a title's index state and leaves
        the other, so a deleted title keeps appearing with a stale score --
        which is why `remove` is one method rather than two.

        **Which half is asserted depends on which half the backend owns**,
        and neither driver is let off. A document store owns both, so this
        searches `FULL_TEXT` on purpose: a `remove` that cleared only the
        vector passes a semantic search (the title is gone from that lane
        anyway) and fails here, which is the asymmetry that hides the bug.
        Postgres owns the vector only -- its document is a generated column
        of a table this port does not own -- so it asserts the semantic half
        here and the full-text half through `ON DELETE CASCADE` in
        `tests/integration/test_adapters_search_postgres.py`.

        Both documents carry a vector so the semantic branch has two
        candidates to distinguish; the survivor assertion rules out the other
        direction, a `remove` that empties the index.
        """
        removed = _document("The Quiet Vacuum", vector=(1.0, 0.0))
        survivor = _document("Vacuum Chamber", vector=(0.6, 0.8))
        await self.index_all(index, [removed, survivor])
        await index.remove(removed.title_id)

        if self.owns_document_lifecycle:
            outcome = await index.search(SearchRequest(query="vacuum", limit=50))
        elif self.supports_semantic:
            outcome = await index.search(
                SearchRequest(
                    query="vacuum",
                    mode=SearchMode.SEMANTIC,
                    query_vector=(1.0, 0.0),
                    limit=50,
                )
            )
        else:
            pytest.skip("this driver owns neither lane's lifecycle yet; see Task 18")
        found = {hit.title_id for hit in outcome.hits}
        assert removed.title_id not in found
        assert survivor.title_id in found
```
