## Task 20: `SearchService` — retrieval is not ranking, and a degraded search says so

PRD 05 separates the two deliberately: **retrieve** candidates (full-text, vector, or fused), then **rank in application code** — relevance, popularity, owned-vs-not, watch state, recency, taste-centroid proximity. ADR-0002 makes the same split from the other direction: "neither Meilisearch nor Typesense can express `0.6·semantic + 0.2·log(popularity) + 0.2·recency`", which is what makes the engine a *candidate generator* and therefore swappable behind a port. Tasks 16–19 built the generator. This task builds the half above it, and its first job is to say plainly which of PRD 05's six ranking terms M6 actually ships.

**Three ship, three are deferred, and none ships without data behind it.**

| PRD 05 ranking term | M6 | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **relevance** | ✅ | `SearchOutcome.hits` is an *ordering*. Enters the blend as a rank, never as a raw score — see below. |
| **popularity** | ✅ | `titles.popularity` is a real column and `Title.popularity` is `float \| None, ge=0`. Nullable on the skeleton tier, which is what makes the absence rule below load-bearing rather than decorative. |
| **owned-vs-not** | ✅ | `media_items` is local and complete. PRD 05 is explicit that this is a *boost*, not a filter, and the wrong implementation has a test. |
| **watch state** | ❌ → **M7** | `SearchRequest` carries no user, and `SearchFilters` is a closed vocabulary that deliberately does not have one (🔶 1's settlement — a filter vocabulary that grows per caller is the `dict[str, Any]` it replaced). M7 is the first milestone whose calls hold a user identity by construction: PRD 06's rows are per-user, and `ContinueWatchingRow` reads exactly this table. Shipping it here would mean inventing a user seam for one term. |
| **recency** | ❌ → **M7** | The data exists (`year` on essentially the whole catalog, `release_date` on the enriched tier) and that is not the obstacle: **the term needs a decay constant and M6 has nothing to choose one against.** Task 26's gate measures the *suggest* path's recall@5, not ranking, so a half-life picked here would be `DEFAULT_STALE_AFTER_SECONDS = 90.0` again — a constant that reads like a measurement and is a guess. There is also a double-counting argument worth recording *as an argument and not as a measurement*: TMDb's `popularity` is a rolling engagement figure rather than a static quality score, so it already leans recent, and an explicit recency term on top of it counts one fact twice. |
| **taste-centroid proximity** | ❌ → **M7** | There is no centroid. PRD 06 owns the taste model; nothing in `src/` computes one. |

**Relevance enters the blend as a rank, not as a score, and that is the whole of ADR-0002's prohibition arriving one layer up.** `ts_rank` lands around 0.06, an RRF score around 0.016–0.033, a cosine similarity in [-1, 1]. A popularity term in [0, 1) added to any of those is *scores from incompatible scales* — the exact thing ADR-0002 forbids for fusion, committed in application code instead of in SQL, where nothing in this milestone would catch it. So the service reads `SearchOutcome.hits` as an **ordering** and derives `1 / (k + rank)` from the position, with the dense rank so equal index scores stay equal.

Two consequences fall out of that, and both are worth having on purpose:

- **`k` is 1, not `search_rrf_k`'s 60**, and the difference is not cosmetic. At k = 60 the entire relevance term spans 0.0167 down to 0.0092 while popularity spans [0, 1) — the popularity term would outweigh relevance by two orders of magnitude, and the resulting list is popularity-ordered wearing search's name. k = 1 puts relevance on the same [0, 1] scale as every other term, which is what makes the declared weights mean what they say. RRF's k has a different job (flattening two *candidate lists* against each other, Task 19) and borrowing the constant across the two would be a number that looks shared and is not.
- **The service becomes indifferent to whether a backend's score is a similarity or a distance.** Nothing here compares scores across hits except for equality, so a semantic implementation that returned `v <=> q` rather than `1 - (v <=> q)` cannot invert this ranking. That is one fewer cross-backend convention to get right, and it is a reason to prefer the rank-derived term over the obvious score-derived one, not just an accident of it.

**Absence is not zero, and the blend is a weighted mean over the signals that are actually present.** `Title.popularity` is `None` for every title TMDb has never described — which on the measured catalog is most of 1,271,138 rows. `popularity or 0.0` buries the entire un-enriched catalog beneath the enriched tier while looking like arithmetic, and it is ADR-0014's rule broken in a new place: a title whose popularity nobody has measured is not a title measured as unpopular. So a `None` signal contributes to **neither** the numerator nor the denominator, and the score is renormalised by the weights that applied. The observable consequence, which is also the test: at equal relevance, a title with unknown popularity ranks **above** one known to have popularity 0.0.

That shape is also what makes boundary call 8's promise true one task later — `SimilarityService`'s blend (Task 21) is the same skeleton with a different signal list, so landing MovieLens or collection membership is adding a term and a weight in both places rather than rewriting two scorers.

**Owned titles are boosted, not exclusive, and the arithmetic says so rather than a comment.** PRD 05: "searching should surface things you don't have, clearly marked, because that feeds discovery." A ranking that filters to owned is the wrong implementation, and it is an *attractive* wrong implementation because every result it returns is correct — nothing is wrong on the screen, the catalog is simply smaller than it should be. It needs two cases, not one: an unowned title that matched strongly must still be present **and still first** (which fails a filter, and fails a boost large enough to invert the top of the list), and an owned title must outrank an unowned one **at equal relevance** (which fails a boost of zero, i.e. a term that was declared and never applied). With `1/(1 + rank)` at weight 0.70 and a flat 0.15 owned term, an owned title cannot displace the rank-0 hit — 0.70 against 0.35 + 0.15 = 0.50 — and can move roughly five positions in the middle of the list. That bound is stated because a boost nobody can bound is a boost nobody can defend.

**Ranking reorders the candidate window; it does not widen it.** An owned title the index did not return at all cannot be rescued here. Over-fetching (retrieve 2× the limit, rank, truncate) was considered and declined: it makes the result set depend on a multiple nobody measured, and the boost's job is to reorder things that all matched, not to import things that matched less. `owned_only` is a *filter* on the port for the case where the narrowing is what the caller wanted.

**The service owns the `Embedder`, and Task 1's DTO makes that structural rather than conventional.** `SearchRequest.__post_init__` refuses a `SEMANTIC` or `FUSED` request with no `query_vector`, so **the only object that can legally construct one is the object holding the model.** That is worth stating because it forces one correction to a sibling task: Task 25's draft `_search` passes a `SearchRequest(query=…, mode=SearchMode.FUSED, …)` *into* this service, and that call raises `ValueError` before it arrives. The port DTO is this service's **output** to the index, never its input from a caller — so `SearchService.search` takes primitives and builds the request itself, and Task 25's `_search` calls `pipeline.search.search(query, mode=…, limit=…, filters=…)`.

**And nobody applies a model prefix — the 🔶 3 settlement, asserted here because this is the only place a caller could.** Measured over 210 paired observations: the documented BGE query prefix moves MRR by **−0.0028**, CI `[−0.0259, +0.0203]`, and applying it to *both* sides is **−0.0663**, CI `[−0.1013, −0.0330]`, against a power control of −0.2497 at P(>0) = 0.000. `compose_document` (Task 11) applies none on the document side, so a prefix added here is the asymmetric case and a prefix added to both is the −0.066 one. The case asserts the embedder received the query **exactly as typed**.

**A blank query is refused before it reaches the model, and the reason is this milestone's own trap on the query side.** Every whitespace-only input embeds to the *identical* vector, cos = 1.0000 exactly. That vector is a perfectly valid unit vector, so a blank semantic query returns a confident ranked list of whatever happens to sit nearest a degenerate point — no error, no empty result, nothing that says the query was empty. Task 11 refuses a degenerate *document*; this refuses a degenerate *query*. It returns an empty answer rather than raising, because a blank query is what a search box sends between keystrokes and erroring on it makes a client handle a failure for a non-event.

**Degradation is asymmetric on purpose: `SEMANTIC` raises, `FUSED` narrows and reports.** The embedder is optional (ADR-0022) and a deployment without it still has full-text and trigram over all 1,271,138 titles. PRD 08's rule is "a degraded subsystem narrows functionality; it never fails a request local state can answer" — and `FUSED` minus its semantic lane is still full-text, which is a narrower but honest answer. `SEMANTIC` minus its semantic lane is nothing; narrowing it to zero is not narrowing, it is failing while pretending, and the caller gets a plausible full-text list for a query whose entire point was that full-text could not answer it. So one raises and one degrades, and the degradation is *carried in the answer* — `requested_mode` beside `mode` — because a degradation nobody can see is the milestone's headline failure mode wearing a different hat.

**`semantic_coverage` is passed through, never recomputed.** It is the fraction of the *filtered population* that had a vector, which only the index can know. Computed here from the returned hits it would be 1.0 whenever every hit had a vector — and the two agree exactly in the case a green test is most likely to seed, which is how that mutation survives.

**Suggest is retrieval only, and that asymmetry is the reason the ports are split.** `SearchService.suggest` delegates to `SuggestIndex` and hydrates; it applies **no ranking blend at all**. Task 17's path already ordered by edit distance and then popularity *inside the capped candidate set*, and re-ranking that in application code would undo the ordering the narrow path exists to produce — a popularity term applied twice, once inside the cap and once outside it.

**Two batch reads are added to existing ports, and both replace an N+1 that is measured rather than feared.** Hydrating 50 hits through `TitleRepository.get` is 50 statements per search — the same shape `index(title_id)` was removed from the search port for. And ownership through `MediaItemRepository.list_for_title` is worse than N+1: CLAUDE.md records that a read on `media_items.title_id` alone is a read of the whole show — **20,001 rows, 22.901 ms, 402 buffers** for one 20,000-episode series against **1 row, 0.251 ms, 21 buffers** with `AND episode_id IS NULL`. So `owned_title_ids` carries that clause, the same one `resolve_external_ids`' title branch already carries for the same reason, and its bound is named: a library that reported episodes but never their series row reads as not-owned for that series.

**One definition of "owned", cited from both sides.** A copy the nightly sweep retracted (`available = false`, PRD 02's soft delete) still counts. A ranking that flips because a source went down moves search results for a reason that has nothing to do with the query, and M5's whole lesson is that a channel can look healthy while delivering nothing. The `owned_only` *filter* in `PostgresSearchIndex` (Task 16) must use the identical predicate — two definitions of owned is how a filtered list and a boosted list stop agreeing.

- Create: `src/usher/domain/search.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/services/search.py` (Task 11 created it for the composer)
- Modify: `src/usher/ports/repository.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/db/repositories/title.py`, `src/usher/db/repositories/media_item.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/composition.py`
- Modify: `tests/fakes/title_repository.py`, `tests/fakes/media_item_repository.py`

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_search.py  (new file)

"""`SearchService`'s ranking, its degradation, and who embeds.

**Retrieval is held fixed here and only ranking varies**, which is why these
cases drive a scripted `SearchIndex` rather than `FakeSearchIndex`. That fake
is the contract-tested double for the *port* and its own docstring says it has
no text analysis at all -- no stemming, no `tsquery`, no weight classes -- so
a ranking assertion driven through its matching would be an assertion about a
tokenizer nobody shipped. `SearchIndexContract` covers the port; this file
covers what the service does with what the port returned.

Every title below is invented; `test_no_dataset_row_is_committed_anywhere`
scans this file.
"""

import uuid
from collections.abc import Sequence

import pytest

from tests.fakes.embedding import FakeEmbedder
from tests.fakes.media_item_repository import FakeMediaItemRepository
from tests.fakes.title_repository import FakeTitleRepository
from usher.domain.enums import TitleKind
from usher.domain.title import Title
from usher.ports.search import (
    SearchDocument,
    SearchFilters,
    SearchHit,
    SearchIndex,
    SearchMode,
    SearchOutcome,
    SearchRequest,
)
from usher.services.search import SearchService, SemanticSearchUnavailable


class _ScriptedIndex(SearchIndex):
    """A `SearchIndex` that returns exactly what a case tells it to.

    Records the request it was handed, so the "who embeds" cases can assert on
    the vector that crossed the port rather than on the vector the service
    happened to compute.
    """

    def __init__(self, outcome: SearchOutcome) -> None:
        self.outcome = outcome
        self.requests: list[SearchRequest] = []

    async def index_many(self, documents: Sequence[SearchDocument]) -> None:
        return None

    async def remove(self, title_id: uuid.UUID) -> None:
        return None

    async def search(self, request: SearchRequest) -> SearchOutcome:
        self.requests.append(request)
        return self.outcome


# --- who embeds, and with what --------------------------------------------


async def test_the_service_embeds_the_query_and_the_index_never_does() -> None:
    """Defect 4 of 🔶 1, from the caller's side. Fails: an index that embeds
    for itself -- a backend with its own model has its own prefix convention,
    its own checkpoint and its own drift, and nothing above it can see any of
    the three."""
    embedder = FakeEmbedder()
    index = _ScriptedIndex(SearchOutcome())
    await _service(index, embedder=embedder).search("an empty room", mode=SearchMode.SEMANTIC)
    assert index.requests[0].query_vector == tuple((await embedder.embed(["an empty room"]))[0])


async def test_the_query_is_embedded_exactly_as_typed_with_no_instruction_prefix() -> None:
    """**The 🔶 3 settlement, asserted where a caller could break it.**
    `ports/embedding.py` used to say "callers are responsible for any
    query-side instruction prefix" and this service is the caller it meant.
    The documented BGE prefix moves MRR -0.0028, CI [-0.0259, +0.0203]; applied
    to both sides -- which one symmetric loop plus that instruction produces --
    it is -0.0663, CI [-0.1013, -0.0330]. `compose_document` applies none on
    the document side, so anything added here is one of those two conditions
    and nothing else in this repository could detect it."""
    embedder = FakeEmbedder()
    await _service(_ScriptedIndex(SearchOutcome()), embedder=embedder).search(
        "an empty room", mode=SearchMode.SEMANTIC
    )
    assert embedder.calls == [["an empty room"]]


async def test_a_blank_query_never_reaches_the_model() -> None:
    """The degenerate-document trap, on the query side. Every whitespace-only
    input embeds to the *identical* vector -- cos = 1.0000 exactly -- and that
    vector is perfectly valid, so a blank semantic query returns a confident
    ranked list of whatever sits nearest a degenerate point, with no error and
    no empty result to say the query was empty. Task 11 refuses a degenerate
    document; this refuses a degenerate query. Empty rather than a raise: a
    search box sends this between keystrokes."""
    embedder = FakeEmbedder()
    index = _ScriptedIndex(SearchOutcome(hits=(SearchHit(title_id=uuid.uuid4(), score=1.0),)))
    answer = await _service(index, embedder=embedder).search("   ", mode=SearchMode.SEMANTIC)
    assert answer.results == ()
    assert embedder.calls == []
    assert index.requests == [], "a blank query reached the index"


# --- degradation -----------------------------------------------------------


async def test_semantic_with_no_embedder_is_an_error_not_a_full_text_fallback() -> None:
    """Fails: a silent fallback to full-text, which returns a plausible ranked
    list for a query whose whole point was that full-text could not answer it
    ("movies about isolation in space" against a `tsquery` for four words).
    PRD 08's rule is that a degraded subsystem *narrows*; SEMANTIC minus its
    only lane is not narrower, it is a different question answered without
    saying so."""
    with pytest.raises(SemanticSearchUnavailable):
        await _service(_ScriptedIndex(SearchOutcome()), embedder=None).search(
            "an empty room", mode=SearchMode.SEMANTIC
        )


async def test_fused_with_no_embedder_narrows_to_full_text_and_says_which() -> None:
    """The other half of the asymmetry, and the half that must not raise --
    FUSED minus its semantic lane is still full-text, an honest answer local
    state can give. Two wrong implementations; the second is the dangerous
    one. Raising fails a request local state can answer. **Degrading silently**
    returns the right rows under the label the caller asked for, so an operator
    running `--mode fused` without the embedding extra sees a working hybrid
    search forever."""
    index = _ScriptedIndex(SearchOutcome(hits=(SearchHit(title_id=_QUIET, score=1.0),)))
    answer = await _service(index, embedder=None).search("vacuum", mode=SearchMode.FUSED)
    assert answer.requested_mode is SearchMode.FUSED
    assert answer.mode is SearchMode.FULL_TEXT
    assert answer.degraded is True
    assert [result.title_id for result in answer.results] == [_QUIET]
    assert index.requests[0].mode is SearchMode.FULL_TEXT
    assert index.requests[0].query_vector is None


async def test_semantic_coverage_is_passed_through_rather_than_recomputed() -> None:
    """Point 3 of "the one thing this milestone must not get wrong". Fails: a
    service computing coverage from its own hits, which reads 1.0 whenever
    every returned hit had a vector -- precisely the case a green test seeds --
    while the real question is what fraction of the *filtered population* had
    one. The two agree exactly in the easy case."""
    index = _ScriptedIndex(
        SearchOutcome(hits=(SearchHit(title_id=_QUIET, score=1.0),), semantic_coverage=0.25)
    )
    answer = await _service(index).search("vacuum", mode=SearchMode.FULL_TEXT)
    assert answer.semantic_coverage == 0.25


# --- ranking ---------------------------------------------------------------


async def test_an_unowned_match_still_appears_and_still_outranks_an_owned_one() -> None:
    """**PRD 05's "boosted but not exclusive", and the wrong implementation is
    an attractive one.** A ranking that filters to owned returns nothing
    incorrect -- every row on the screen is right, the catalog is just quietly
    smaller. The unowned title is the *stronger* match (index rank 0), so this
    fails a filter **and** a boost big enough to invert the top of the list.
    Position, not membership: `_UNOWNED in ids` passes against an
    implementation that returns the whole table in physical order.
    """
    index = _ScriptedIndex(
        SearchOutcome(
            hits=(SearchHit(title_id=_UNOWNED, score=0.9), SearchHit(title_id=_OWNED, score=0.4))
        )
    )
    answer = await _service(index, owned={_OWNED}).search("vacuum")
    assert [result.title_id for result in answer.results] == [_UNOWNED, _OWNED]
    assert [result.owned for result in answer.results] == [False, True]


async def test_an_owned_title_outranks_an_unowned_one_at_equal_relevance() -> None:
    """The mirror, and without it a boost of **zero** passes the case above --
    a term declared in the weight table and never applied to anything.

    Equal *scores* from the index, so both hits take the same dense rank and
    the relevance term cancels exactly. That is why the relevance term groups
    ties rather than using raw position: with a strict positional rank no two
    candidates ever tie, and this property would be unassertable.
    """
    index = _ScriptedIndex(
        SearchOutcome(
            hits=(SearchHit(title_id=_UNOWNED, score=0.5), SearchHit(title_id=_OWNED, score=0.5))
        )
    )
    answer = await _service(index, owned={_OWNED}).search("vacuum")
    assert [result.title_id for result in answer.results] == [_OWNED, _UNOWNED]


async def test_a_strong_match_is_not_displaced_by_a_popular_weak_one() -> None:
    """**The incompatible-scale failure, in application code.** Fails: a blend
    adding `hit.score` raw (a `ts_rank` around 0.06) to a popularity term in
    [0, 1), and equally a relevance term spelled `1 / (search_rrf_k + rank)` --
    at k = 60 relevance spans 0.0167 to 0.0092 while popularity spans the unit
    interval, so the list is popularity-ordered wearing RRF's respectable name.
    ADR-0002 forbids exactly this one layer down; nothing catches it one layer
    up except this case.
    """
    index = _ScriptedIndex(
        SearchOutcome(
            hits=(SearchHit(title_id=_QUIET, score=0.9), SearchHit(title_id=_POPULAR, score=0.1))
        )
    )
    answer = await _service(index).search("vacuum")
    assert answer.results[0].title_id == _QUIET


async def test_an_unknown_popularity_is_not_a_popularity_of_zero() -> None:
    """ADR-0014 in a fourth place. `titles.popularity` is `None` for every
    title TMDb has never described -- most of 1,271,138 rows. Fails:
    `popularity or 0.0` and its SQL twin `coalesce(popularity, 0)`, which rank
    a title nobody measured identically to one measured as unpopular and bury
    the un-enriched catalog beneath the enriched tier while looking like
    arithmetic. The two hits are at equal relevance and equal ownership, so the
    only thing that can separate them is whether absence was scored or excluded.
    """
    index = _ScriptedIndex(
        SearchOutcome(
            hits=(SearchHit(title_id=_ZERO_POP, score=0.5), SearchHit(title_id=_NO_POP, score=0.5))
        )
    )
    answer = await _service(index).search("vacuum")
    assert [result.title_id for result in answer.results] == [_NO_POP, _ZERO_POP]


async def test_equal_scores_are_broken_by_id_so_two_searches_agree() -> None:
    """Determinism, which is a pagination property before it is a tidiness
    one. Fails: falling back to whatever order the index returned -- and this
    repository has already measured that `UPDATE ... RETURNING` hands rows back
    in heap order on a small table, so "the order it came in" is not an order.
    """
    hits = (SearchHit(title_id=_HIGH_ID, score=0.5), SearchHit(title_id=_LOW_ID, score=0.5))
    answer = await _service(_ScriptedIndex(SearchOutcome(hits=hits))).search("vacuum")
    assert [result.title_id for result in answer.results] == sorted([_LOW_ID, _HIGH_ID])


async def test_a_hit_whose_title_row_is_gone_is_dropped_rather_than_raising() -> None:
    """A title deleted between the index write and the read. Fails:
    `by_id[hit.title_id]`, which is a `KeyError` -- a 500 on a search because
    one row was removed. The surviving hit is asserted too, so "drop
    everything" is not a pass.
    """


async def test_the_limit_is_clamped_to_the_configured_ceiling() -> None:
    """`search_result_limit` is the most a caller may ask for, not the
    default. Fails: passing `request.limit` straight through, which makes
    `--limit 10000` a scan wearing a search's name -- every candidate is a row
    hydrated into a `SearchResult` in application code.
    """


async def test_suggest_hydrates_and_does_not_re_rank() -> None:
    """`PostgresSuggestIndex` already ordered by edit distance and then by
    popularity *inside* the capped candidate set. Fails: a service applying
    the search blend here, which is popularity counted twice -- once inside the
    cap and once outside it -- and which reorders a type-ahead list away from
    the ordering the narrow path exists to produce.
    """
```

- [ ] **Step 3: The domain-side result shapes**

```python
# src/usher/domain/search.py  (new file)

"""What a search and a "more like this" hand back, once ranked.

`domain/` imports nothing -- not `ports/`, which imports *it*. So these carry
no `SearchMode`, no `SearchHit` and no engine vocabulary: they are what a row
renders and what a CLI prints. The service-side envelope that *does* carry a
`SearchMode` lives in `services/search.py`, beside the service, exactly as
`TitleDetail` lives beside `TitleReadService`.
"""

import uuid

from usher.domain.base import DomainModel
from usher.domain.enums import TitleKind


class SearchResult(DomainModel):
    """One ranked hit, hydrated.

    `score` is the **blended** score, not the index's -- it is comparable
    within one answer and meaningless between two, because the relevance term
    is derived from position within the candidate set that request returned.
    Named `score` rather than `relevance` for exactly that reason.

    `owned` rides along because PRD 05 requires unowned results to be surfaced
    "clearly marked": a client that had to ask a second question to render the
    badge would either ask it per row or not render it.
    """

    title_id: uuid.UUID
    kind: TitleKind
    name: str
    year: int | None = None
    popularity: float | None = None
    owned: bool = False
    score: float = 0.0


class SimilarTitle(DomainModel):
    """One neighbour of a seed title, read out of the precomputed table.

    `score` is `SimilarityService`'s blend at the instant the batch ran, not a
    live computation -- which is the whole point of `title_neighbors` (PRD 05:
    "item vectors are static, so this is a cheap batch artifact that makes
    'more like this' instant and engine-independent").
    """

    title_id: uuid.UUID
    kind: TitleKind
    name: str
    year: int | None = None
    score: float = 0.0
```

```python
# src/usher/ports/repository.py  (modify -- on TitleRepository)

    @abstractmethod
    async def list_by_ids(self, title_ids: Sequence[uuid.UUID]) -> list[Title]:
        """Every title named by `title_ids` that still exists, in any order.

        **A missing id is an omission, never an error.** A title deleted
        between an index write and a search read is ordinary, and the caller
        re-orders by its own ranking anyway -- so returning fewer rows than
        asked for is the contract, and a caller that indexes the result by id
        must tolerate the gap.

        Exists because hydrating a 50-hit result set through `get()` is 50
        statements per search: the same round-trip-per-item shape `index_many`
        was introduced to delete from `SearchIndex`, arriving from the other
        direction.
        """
```

```python
# src/usher/ports/repository.py  (modify -- on MediaItemRepository)

    @abstractmethod
    async def owned_title_ids(self, title_ids: Sequence[uuid.UUID]) -> set[uuid.UUID]:
        """Which of `title_ids` this household has a copy of, on any source.

        **A retracted copy still counts.** PRD 02's availability is a soft
        delete, so `available = false` means "a source is not currently
        reporting it", and a search ranking that flipped when a source went
        down would move results for a reason unconnected to the query.

        **Restricted to a title's own row (`episode_id IS NULL`), and the
        reason is measured.** `IngestService` writes an episode's row with its
        series' `title_id` *and* its own `episode_id`, so an unrestricted read
        of a series is one row per episode file: 20,001 rows / 22.901 ms / 402
        buffers against 1 row / 0.251 ms / 21 buffers, on this project's own
        measurement of `list_for_title`. `resolve_external_ids`' title branch
        carries the identical clause. The bound that buys: a library that
        reported episodes but never their series row reads as not-owned for
        that series.
        """
```

- [ ] **Step 5: The service**

```python
# src/usher/services/search.py  (modify -- appended below `compose_document`)


class SemanticSearchUnavailable(Exception):
    """This deployment has no `Embedder`, so a semantic query cannot be served.

    **Deliberately not a `UsherPortError`**: that family's docstring says
    "every error a port implementation may raise", and nothing failed here --
    the deployment is configured without a model and said so once, at startup.
    Filed there it would land in the `except UsherPortError` arms that mean "an
    upstream is broken", where the response is a retry and no retry can help.
    **And not a `ValueError`**, which a caller wrapping a search would catch
    alongside every argument failure in the call. M9 gives it a `code` in PRD
    07's vocabulary; M6 invents no status code for a route that does not exist.
    """


# `1 / (k + rank)`, and **k is 1 rather than `search_rrf_k`'s 60**. At 60 the
# relevance term spans 0.0167 down to 0.0092 while popularity spans [0, 1), so
# the blend is popularity-ordered wearing RRF's name -- ADR-0002's prohibition
# committed one layer up, where nothing else here would catch it. RRF's k has a
# different job and sharing it would be a number that looks shared and is not.
_RELEVANCE_K = 1.0

# Popularity squashed to [0, 1) by `p / (p + midpoint)`: bounded, monotone, and
# -- unlike a min-max over the candidate set -- independent of which other rows
# came back. `log1p` is unbounded (6.9 at p=1000 against 0.69 at p=1) so it
# would need a set-dependent normaliser, which is the same problem again. 10.0
# is **chosen, not measured**; what makes that tolerable is that the term is
# bounded, so a wrong midpoint moves a score by at most its weight.
_POPULARITY_MIDPOINT = 10.0

# PRD 05's ranking terms M6 has data for. Watch state, recency and taste
# centroid are M7's and are *absent* rather than zeroed -- a term with no data
# is a weight that reads like a signal. Relevance dominates because a search is
# a request for a specific thing; the other two are tie-breakers among things
# that already matched. The 0.15 owned boost is bounded on purpose: against
# `1 / (1 + rank)` at 0.70 it cannot displace the rank-0 hit (0.70 against
# 0.35 + 0.15) and moves a title roughly five positions mid-list -- PRD 05's
# "boosted but not exclusive" as arithmetic rather than as a promise.
_WEIGHTS: dict[str, float] = {"relevance": 0.70, "popularity": 0.15, "owned": 0.15}


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class SearchAnswer:
    """Ranked results, plus what actually ran.

    Lives here rather than in `usher.domain.search` because it carries a
    `SearchMode`, which is a port type, and `domain/` imports nothing. The
    precedent is `TitleDetail` beside `TitleReadService`.

    **`requested_mode` beside `mode` is the degradation made visible.** A
    `FUSED` request on a deployment with no embedder is served as full-text and
    every row of that answer is correct -- so without these two fields the only
    signal is `semantic_coverage == 0.0`, which is *also* what a healthy FUSED
    search over a catalog with no embeddings reports. Two different problems
    with two different fixes (install the extra; run `usher index`) that would
    otherwise present identically.
    """

    results: tuple[SearchResult, ...] = ()
    requested_mode: SearchMode = SearchMode.FULL_TEXT
    mode: SearchMode = SearchMode.FULL_TEXT
    semantic_coverage: float = 0.0

    @property
    def degraded(self) -> bool:
        """The request was served in a narrower mode than it asked for."""
        return self.mode is not self.requested_mode


class SearchService:
    """PRD 05's two stages, in order: retrieve, then rank.

    **Holds the `Embedder`, and Task 1's DTO makes that structural.**
    `SearchRequest.__post_init__` refuses a `SEMANTIC` or `FUSED` request with
    no `query_vector`, so the only object that can construct one is the object
    holding the model. That is why this method takes primitives: the port DTO
    is this service's *output* to the index, never its input from a caller.

    **Applies no instruction prefix, ever** (ADR-0022). This checkpoint needs
    none: the documented BGE query prefix moves MRR -0.0028 and applying it to
    both sides is -0.0663, against a power control of -0.2497.

    `result_limit` rather than a `Settings`: `services/` may import only
    `domain/` and `ports/` (ADR-0009). `composition.build_pipeline` passes
    `settings.search_result_limit`, which is also what satisfies
    `test_every_setting_is_read_by_something`.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        index: SearchIndex,
        suggestions: SuggestIndex,
        titles: TitleRepository,
        media_items: MediaItemRepository,
        *,
        result_limit: int,
        embedder: Embedder | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self._index = index
        self._suggestions = suggestions
        self._titles = titles
        self._media_items = media_items
        self._result_limit = result_limit
        # Optional, and a deployment without it still has search: full-text and
        # trigram are PRD 05's catalog-lookup tier and serve all 1,271,138
        # titles with no model at all.
        self._embedder = embedder

    async def search(
        self,
        query: str,
        *,
        mode: SearchMode = SearchMode.FULL_TEXT,
        limit: int = 20,
        # `SearchFilters()` as a default would be ruff B008 (a call in a
        # default) even though the value is frozen. The sentinel is the
        # spelling, not the reason.
        filters: SearchFilters | None = None,
    ) -> SearchAnswer:
        """Retrieve, then rank. Raises `SemanticSearchUnavailable`."""
        requested = mode
        # Refused before the model, not after. Every whitespace-only input
        # embeds to the identical vector at cos 1.0000 exactly, so a blank
        # semantic query would return a confident list of whatever sits nearest
        # a degenerate point -- Task 11's trap, on the query side. Empty rather
        # than a raise: a search box sends this between keystrokes.
        if not query.strip():
            return SearchAnswer(requested_mode=requested, mode=requested)

        vector: tuple[float, ...] | None = None
        if mode is not SearchMode.FULL_TEXT:
            if self._embedder is None:
                if mode is SearchMode.SEMANTIC:
                    # Narrowing this to full-text is not narrowing. The caller
                    # asked the one question full-text cannot answer and would
                    # get a plausible answer to a different one.
                    raise SemanticSearchUnavailable(
                        "semantic search needs an embedding model; this deployment has none"
                    )
                # FUSED, on the other hand, still has a whole lane left, and
                # PRD 08 says a degraded subsystem narrows rather than fails.
                # The narrowing is carried in the answer, not hidden in it.
                mode = SearchMode.FULL_TEXT
            else:
                vector = tuple((await self._embedder.embed([query]))[0])

        outcome = await self._index.search(
            SearchRequest(
                query=query,
                # A ceiling, not a default: every candidate becomes a hydrated
                # row in application code, so an unclamped limit is a scan.
                limit=min(limit, self._result_limit),
                mode=mode,
                filters=filters or SearchFilters(),
                query_vector=vector,
            )
        )
        return SearchAnswer(
            results=await self._rank(outcome.hits),
            requested_mode=requested,
            mode=mode,
            # Passed through, never recomputed. It is the fraction of the
            # *filtered population* that had a vector; derived from the hits it
            # would read 1.0 whenever every returned hit had one, which is
            # exactly the case a green test seeds.
            semantic_coverage=outcome.semantic_coverage,
        )

    async def suggest(self, prefix: str, limit: int = 10) -> tuple[SearchResult, ...]:
        """Type-ahead candidates, hydrated and **not re-ranked**.

        `PostgresSuggestIndex` already ordered by edit distance and then by
        popularity inside its capped candidate set. Applying the search blend
        here would count popularity twice -- once inside the cap and once
        outside it -- and reorder the box away from the ordering the narrow
        path exists to produce. Which is also the practical half of why
        `SuggestIndex` is its own port.
        """
        if not prefix.strip():
            return ()
        hits = await self._suggestions.suggest(prefix, limit=min(limit, self._result_limit))
        by_id = {title.id: title for title in await self._titles.list_by_ids(
            [hit.title_id for hit in hits]
        )}
        owned = await self._media_items.owned_title_ids(list(by_id))
        return tuple(
            _result(by_id[hit.title_id], owned=hit.title_id in owned, score=hit.score)
            for hit in hits
            if hit.title_id in by_id
        )

    async def _rank(self, hits: Sequence[SearchHit]) -> tuple[SearchResult, ...]:
        """PRD 05 stage 2, over one already-retrieved candidate set.

        Two reads regardless of hit count, which is the whole reason
        `list_by_ids` and `owned_title_ids` exist.
        """
        if not hits:
            return ()
        titles = {title.id: title for title in await self._titles.list_by_ids(
            [hit.title_id for hit in hits]
        )}
        owned = await self._media_items.owned_title_ids(list(titles))
        ranks = _dense_ranks(hits)
        results = [
            _result(
                titles[hit.title_id],
                owned=hit.title_id in owned,
                score=_blend(
                    relevance=_RELEVANCE_K / (_RELEVANCE_K + rank),
                    popularity=_popularity_term(titles[hit.title_id].popularity),
                    owned=1.0 if hit.title_id in owned else 0.0,
                ),
            )
            for hit, rank in zip(hits, ranks, strict=True)
            # Dropped, not raised: a title deleted between the index write and
            # this read is ordinary, and `titles[hit.title_id]` is a KeyError
            # -- a 500 on a search because one row went away.
            if hit.title_id in titles
        ]
        # Ties broken by id. Falling back to the index's own order is not an
        # order: this repository has measured `UPDATE ... RETURNING` handing
        # rows back in heap order on a small table, and a search that reorders
        # equal-scoring rows between two identical calls cannot be paginated.
        results.sort(key=lambda result: (-result.score, result.title_id))
        return tuple(results)


def _dense_ranks(hits: Sequence[SearchHit]) -> list[int]:
    """Positions, with equal index scores sharing a position.

    **Dense rather than strict, and the difference is load-bearing.** Under a
    strict positional rank no two candidates ever tie on relevance, so the
    owned boost could only ever be a tie-break that no case could construct --
    `test_an_owned_title_outranks_an_unowned_one_at_equal_relevance` would be
    unassertable and a boost of zero would pass the suite.

    Scores are compared for **equality only**, never for magnitude, so this is
    indifferent to whether a backend reports a similarity or a distance. One
    fewer cross-backend convention to get right, and a reason to prefer a
    rank-derived relevance term over the obvious score-derived one.
    """
    ranks: list[int] = []
    rank = 0
    previous: float | None = None
    for hit in hits:
        if previous is not None and hit.score != previous:
            rank += 1
        ranks.append(rank)
        previous = hit.score
    return ranks


def _popularity_term(popularity: float | None) -> float | None:
    """`p / (p + midpoint)`, or `None` when nobody has measured it.

    **`None` is not 0.0** -- ADR-0014, in a fourth place. `titles.popularity`
    is null for every title TMDb has never described, which on the measured
    catalog is most of 1,271,138 rows, and `popularity or 0.0` ranks a title
    nobody measured identically to one measured as unpopular. That buries the
    whole un-enriched catalog beneath the enriched tier while looking like
    arithmetic and raising nothing.
    """
    if popularity is None:
        return None
    return popularity / (popularity + _POPULARITY_MIDPOINT)


def _blend(**signals: float | None) -> float:
    """A weighted mean over the signals that are actually present.

    An absent signal leaves **both** the numerator and the denominator, so a
    title with no popularity is scored on what is known about it rather than
    penalised for what is not. The observable consequence: at equal relevance,
    unknown popularity ranks above a measured zero.

    Written as a sum over an explicit signal list -- the same skeleton
    `SimilarityService` uses -- so that landing watch state, recency or a taste
    centroid in M7 is adding a term and a weight in both places rather than
    rewriting two scorers.
    """
    total = 0.0
    applied = 0.0
    for name, value in signals.items():
        if value is None:
            continue
        total += _WEIGHTS[name] * value
        applied += _WEIGHTS[name]
    return total / applied if applied else 0.0


def _result(title: Title, *, owned: bool, score: float) -> SearchResult:
    return SearchResult(
        title_id=title.id,
        kind=title.kind,
        name=title.name,
        year=title.year,
        popularity=title.popularity,
        owned=owned,
        score=score,
    )
```

> **Task 24 adds `usher.search.duration` and `usher.search.results` to this module.** `search()` is written so the timing boundary is one `with` around the whole method rather than around `self._index.search` — a search's latency is what a client waits for, and hydration plus ranking are inside it.

- [ ] **Step 6: Wire it, and correct Task 25's call**

`Pipeline` gains `search: SearchService`, constructed in `build_pipeline`, which gains `embedder: Embedder | None = None` alongside its existing `provider`. Two notes that are decisions rather than mechanics:

- **`build_pipeline` does not build the embedder**, for the reason `metadata_provider` is not built there either: it is a once-per-*process* resource and `build_pipeline` runs once per session. `usher search` builds one with `composition.embedder(settings)` and closes it in the same `finally`.
- **`usher search --mode full_text` and `usher suggest` must not pay a model load** (4.84 s cold). `cli._search` builds the embedder only when the mode is not `FULL_TEXT`; `_suggest` never does, because `SuggestIndex` is trigram-only.

Task 25's `_search` becomes `await pipeline.search.search(query, mode=SearchMode(mode), limit=limit, filters=filters)` — passing a `SearchRequest` in raises `ValueError` from `__post_init__` for every mode but full-text, and that is the DTO working rather than a defect. Its output line gains `requested=` when `answer.degraded`, and its warning gains the second cause:

```python
    if answer.degraded:
        print(
            f"warning: {answer.requested_mode.value} was served as {answer.mode.value} -- "
            "this deployment has no embedding model (install the embedding extra)"
        )
```

## Task 21: Similarity, and a neighbour table that is a batch artefact rather than a queue

`SimilarityService` in `src/usher/services/similar.py`, plus the precompute that fills `title_neighbors` (Task 8's table). PRD 05 gives the design in one sentence — "item vectors are static, so this is a cheap batch artifact that makes 'more like this' instant and engine-independent" — and PRD 06 names the consumer: `SimilarityRow`, M7, TTL "hours".

**Boundary call 8 decides what the blend contains, and the table is worth restating because two of PRD 05's four signals do not exist in `src/` at all.**

| PRD 05 signal | M6 | Checked against |
|---|---|---|
| Embedding cosine | ✅ | `title_embeddings`, built by this milestone |
| Jaccard over **genres, keywords** | ✅ | `ARRAY(Text)`, `NOT NULL`, `'{}'` default |
| Jaccard over **cast, crew** | ❌ | No `Person`/`Credit` table, model or port anywhere; `ports/metadata.py:68-82` defers them to M7/M9 by name |
| MovieLens tag-genome cosine | ❌ | **The importer has never been built** — `PHASES` has no `movielens`, `adapters/bulk/` has no `movielens.py`. Task 27 records the roadmap gap and gives it an owner |
| Collection membership | ❌ | `titles.collection_id` is a bare nullable UUID with no table, and **nothing in `src/` ever writes it** — `services/enrich.py:92` calls it "a FK to a table M7 creates" |

So M6 ships **embedding cosine plus genre and keyword Jaccard**, and the blend is a **sum of weighted terms over an explicit signal list** — the same skeleton `SearchService._blend` uses — so landing a third signal is adding a term and a weight rather than rewriting a scorer. That is not an aspiration: `_WEIGHTS` is a mapping and `_blend` iterates it, so the diff that adds tag-genome cosine is one dict entry, one accessor, and one case.

**Genres and keywords are two terms, not one Jaccard over their union, and the reason is vocabulary size.** Genres are a closed set of roughly nineteen values with two to four per title, so genre Jaccard *saturates*: any two dramas share a genre and score 0.33 or better against each other regardless of subject — high recall, near-zero precision. Keywords are a long tail of hundreds of thousands of values, so an overlap of three is a coincidence rather than a category. Merged into one set, the five-element genre contribution disappears inside a forty-element keyword union and the term nobody weighted does all the work.

**The weights, and they are chosen rather than measured — which is stated here rather than discovered later.**

| Signal | Weight | Why that one |
|---|---|---|
| `cosine` | **0.60** | The only signal computed over the *text* — overview and tagline — which is where "about the same thing" actually lives, and the only one that can tell two horror films apart. It has measured support at this scale: an enriched document retrieves its own skeleton at **0.7638** against a **0.4751** cross-title mean, so the signal is crowded but ordered. |
| `keywords` | **0.25** | Long-tail vocabulary, so overlap is evidence. Its weakness is *coverage* — TMDb keywords are sparse and uneven — which is exactly why absence excludes the term rather than scoring it zero. |
| `genres` | **0.15** | A guard, not a driver: it stops the vector pairing a war documentary with a war film's trailer. Smallest weight because it saturates, per the paragraph above. |

They sum to 1.0, so a fully-covered pair scores in [0, 1] and reads as a fraction — and the renormalisation below preserves that for a partly-covered pair. **Nothing in M6 measures similarity relevance** (Task 26's gate measures the suggest path's recall@5 against synthetic typos, which is a different question entirely), so these are constants with an argument and a docstring, not tuned parameters. What keeps that honest is that changing them is a code change *plus a rebuild* — see the invalidation section — rather than a knob an operator turns and then wonders why yesterday's rows disagree with today's.

**They are deliberately not `Settings` fields, and Task 23's eight-field block is not amended.** A weight is not an operator knob: changing it changes what "similar" *means*, and every row of a 250,000-row precomputed artefact was written under the old meaning. A setting invites exactly the state this milestone exists to eliminate — a table half-computed under one definition and half under another, with nothing to tell them apart. `_NEIGHBORS_PER_TITLE` is a constant for the same reason.

**Jaccard's empty-set case is decided, and the decision is ADR-0014 applied to a set-valued field.** `|A ∩ B| / |A ∪ B|` with both sides empty is 0/0 — a `ZeroDivisionError` inside a batch job, which is the worst place for one: it aborts mid-rebuild and leaves a table that is half old and half new. Returning 0.0 instead is worse than a crash, because it is *silent*: it gives the same answer for "these two share no genres" (evidence of dissimilarity) as for "we do not know either one's genres" (no evidence at all), and the second is a fact about enrichment rather than about the films. So **the term contributes only when both sides are non-empty**; otherwise it leaves the numerator *and* the denominator, and the pair is scored on the signals that have something to say. A thin title's neighbours are then decided by its vector rather than pushed to the bottom for a gap in our metadata.

**NULL-embedding rows are excluded explicitly, on both sides, and this is the trap the front matter names.** Task 11 refuses a degenerate document and Task 12 writes a row with a `NULL` embedding, because a refusal has to be *written* or the backfill re-claims it forever. Those rows must not reach this computation. As a **candidate**, `embedding <=> :seed` is `NULL`, and Postgres sorts NULLs last on an ascending order — so they leak in only when the population is smaller than the requested top-N, at which point they arrive with a `NULL` distance that either raises on the float conversion or, with a careless `coalesce`, lands as distance 0 and pins every refused title to the top of every neighbour list. As a **seed**, there is no vector to search from at all. Both exclusions are `WHERE e.embedding IS NOT NULL` in the repository and both have a case: a refused title has no neighbours of its own and appears in nobody else's.

Remember what makes that failure invisible without the exclusion: every whitespace-only input embeds to the *identical* vector, cos = 1.0000 **exactly**. Had the refusal path stored those vectors instead of NULL, the degenerate set would be a mutually-perfect cluster of unbounded size at the top of every "more like this" row, and no assertion about norms, dimensions or determinism could see it.

**The precompute asks for an exact scan, not the HNSW index, and the distinction between two measurements matters here.** Two facts are easy to conflate and only one of them licenses this:

- **`halfvec` quantisation is free where it matters.** Max cosine error 1.21e-04, mean 3.03e-05; **top-1 and top-5 ordering identical in 42/42 queries**, with divergence only at top-20+ where scores are already within 2e-4. That is a statement about the *storage format*, and it is what puts a top-N of 20–50 inside the safe band.
- **HNSW's recall is a different question and this milestone has not measured it.** Borrowing the halfvec number to justify an approximate index would be laundering one measurement into a claim about another — the thing this plan refuses elsewhere and would be doing here.

Independently of any number, the shapes differ: **recall loss in a live query is per-query and recall loss in a cached artefact is permanent.** A neighbour missed by an approximate scan is missed by every read of that row until the next rebuild, and PRD 05 already says brute-force exact cosine is the right call at this scale — 10k × 384 float32 is 15 MB, and the halfvec form is 7.7 MB, which fits in this host's 96 MB L3. So the precompute runs with the index off (`SET LOCAL enable_indexscan = off` in the rebuild's own transaction, the same idiom this repository already uses to make an ordering key observable), and Task 28's smoke run is where a wall-clock number comes from. This plan does not invent one.

**N is 25, and 20 is where the storage format stops promising an order.** PRD 06's `SimilarityRow` renders roughly ten to twenty items and a consumer that filters — already watched, not owned — needs headroom above what it renders. 25 is deliberately just past the point where halfvec and float32 orderings begin to diverge, and the honest statement is that the last five rows' *internal* order is not to be trusted to better than 2e-4, which is below anything a rendered row can express. Storing 200 would be storing an ordering the storage format cannot honour, at eight times the rows.

**A CLI-driven batch, not a new `JobKind`, and the argument is about the unit of work.**

The standing rule is M4's: *a job kind whose handler is a stub is a queue that grows forever* — which is why no `index` job kind shipped before its handler did. Applied here it cuts a second way. Four reasons, in the order they decide it:

1. **The unit of work is not one title.** Re-embedding title X changes the neighbour lists of every title X is near, and no per-seed job can know which those are without doing the whole computation anyway. A `JobKind.SIMILAR` keyed on a title id would therefore be *wrong* rather than merely partial: it would update the seed's own row and leave every list that should now contain it untouched, producing a table that is never coherent and whose incoherence is undetectable from any single row.
2. **There is no natural enqueue.** Nothing in the pipeline knows when the artefact as a whole has drifted enough to be worth rebuilding, so the enqueue would be a timer — and a timer that enqueues one job is a cron entry with a queue, a handler, a `JobKind` member, a `test_domain_jobs` set update and a park path bolted on.
3. **The cost fits a batch.** The population is the enriched tier (boundary call 4), 2k–10k rows, not 1.27M. The arithmetic is 10k × 10k pairwise distances over a 7.7 MB table, chunked a page of seeds at a time.
4. **It is idempotent and re-runnable**, which is the property that makes a batch acceptable at all: the rebuild deletes and re-inserts per seed inside the page's transaction, so a second run writes the same table and an interrupted run resumes by being run again.

**And the cost of that decision, stated rather than hidden: nothing in M6 re-runs it.** `usher similar --rebuild` is run by an operator or a cron entry, after `usher index --backfill`. PRD 06's "TTL: hours" is therefore a statement about how long M7 may cache what it read, **not** a promise that the table is hours-fresh. An automatic trigger belongs to whichever milestone gains a scheduler — PRD 08 has none — and M7 is the first consumer that would notice. Task 27 is the PRD pass; if it has already landed, this is a one-line addition to PRD 09's M7 section. An obligation recorded only where it was assumed is one nobody plans, which is the failure boundary call 8 caught once already in this milestone.

**What invalidates a neighbour row, and why this artefact does not get the fingerprint treatment.** The milestone's rule is that every derived artefact is either fresh by construction or carries the fingerprint of its input. `title_neighbors` is the one place that rule cannot be honoured, and saying so is better than pretending:

- **A row is stale when either title's embedding moved.** That is a predicate — `title_embeddings.source_fingerprint` or `model_name` differing from what the rebuild saw. And it subsumes the metadata case for free: `compose_document` includes genres and keywords, so a title whose genres changed has a different fingerprint by construction.
- **A row is also stale when some *third* title's embedding moved into its neighbourhood** — and that is *not* decidable without recomputing the row. A new enriched title can belong in the top-25 of a hundred seeds it has never been compared against.
- So per-row freshness is undecidable, and the honest response is a whole-artefact age rather than a per-row fingerprint that would be right about half the question and look right about all of it. `TitleNeighborRepository.computed_at()` returns the **oldest** row's timestamp — the newest would report a whole-table rebuild as fresh the moment the first page committed, which is "looks healthy while describing yesterday" in one accessor.
- **`None` means never computed at all**, which is a different fact from "this title has no neighbours" and is what `usher similar` needs to avoid sending an operator to look at the wrong thing.

**Ordering is deterministic or pagination and re-runs disagree.** Ties break by `neighbor_title_id`. Two candidates at the same blended score are genuinely common here — two titles sharing one genre, no keywords, and near-identical cosines — and "whatever order the candidate query returned" is not an order: this repository has measured `UPDATE … RETURNING` handing rows back in heap order on a small table.

**Per boundary call 1 there is no `GET /titles/{id}/similar`.** M9 owns it, over the service and the table this task finishes. In M6 the read is reached through `usher similar <title_id>` (Task 25's) and the write through `usher similar --rebuild`, which is this task's — one subcommand with a read form and a write form, exactly the shape `usher index` / `usher index --backfill` already has. Two subcommands for one artefact is how those two would have drifted.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/services/similar.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/ports/repository.py` (`TitleNeighborRepository`, and two methods on `TitleEmbeddingRepository`)
- Create: `src/usher/db/repositories/search.py`'s `PostgresTitleNeighborRepository` *(Task 8/9 own the row and the embedding half; if they spelled a column differently, adapt the call sites, not the behaviour)*
- Modify: `src/usher/composition.py`, `src/usher/cli.py`, `README.md`
- Create: `tests/fakes/title_neighbor_repository.py`
- Create: `tests/unit/test_services_similar.py`, `tests/integration/test_services_similar.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_similar.py  (new file)

"""`SimilarityService`'s blend, its exclusions, and its determinism.

**Every cosine in this file is planted, never hoped for.** `FakeEmbedder` is a
hash: similarity between two related titles is noise, so a case that asserted
"these two are similar" against it would pass or fail for reasons unrelated to
the code. `planted_pair(theta)` gives `dot(a, v) == cos(theta)` exactly --
verified to 2.22e-16 -- so a case that needs "these two are 0.9 similar"
states 0.9 and gets 0.9. A threshold a hash has to land on the right side of is
a case that goes red on an unrelated change and gets loosened once,
permanently.

Every title below is invented; `test_no_dataset_row_is_committed_anywhere`
scans this file.
"""

import math

import pytest

from tests.fakes.embedding import planted_pair
from tests.fakes.title_embedding_repository import FakeTitleEmbeddingRepository
from tests.fakes.title_neighbor_repository import FakeTitleNeighborRepository
from usher.services.similar import SimilarityService, _jaccard


# --- the blend -------------------------------------------------------------


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("left", "right", "expected"),
    [
        (("drama", "noir"), ("drama", "noir"), 1.0),
        (("drama", "noir"), ("drama",), 0.5),
        (("drama",), ("comedy",), 0.0),
        ((), (), None),
        (("drama",), (), None),
    ],
)
def test_jaccard_is_none_when_either_side_has_nothing_to_say(
    left: tuple[str, ...], right: tuple[str, ...], expected: float | None
) -> None:
    """**The 0/0 case, decided.** Two wrong implementations, and the second is
    worse.

    `len(a & b) / len(a | b)` raises `ZeroDivisionError` on two empty sets --
    inside a batch job, which is the worst place for one: it aborts mid-rebuild
    and leaves a table half old and half new.

    Returning `0.0` is *silent*, which is worse: it gives the same answer for
    "these two share no genres" (evidence of dissimilarity, the third row here)
    as for "we do not know either one's genres" (no evidence at all). The
    second is a fact about enrichment, not about the films, and scoring it
    pushes every thin title to the bottom of every neighbour list while every
    gauge reads healthy.

    The fifth row is the asymmetric case and it is `None` too: one side being
    empty tells us nothing about whether they overlap.
    """
    assert _jaccard(left, right) == expected


async def test_a_higher_jaccard_wins_at_equal_cosine() -> None:
    """**Half of "a single-signal implementation must not pass".**

    Both candidates sit at exactly the same planted cosine to the seed, so the
    cosine term cancels and the only thing left is tag overlap. Fails a blend
    that is cosine and nothing else -- which is the implementation you get by
    writing the vector half first and never coming back, and which is
    indistinguishable from a working one on any pair whose signals agree.
    """
    seed, at_theta = planted_pair(math.pi / 4)
    service, embeddings = _service()
    embeddings.given(_SEED, seed, genres=("drama", "noir"), keywords=("ledger",))
    embeddings.given(_SHARES_TAGS, at_theta, genres=("drama", "noir"), keywords=("ledger",))
    embeddings.given(_SHARES_NOTHING, at_theta, genres=("comedy",), keywords=("beach",))
    await service.rebuild()
    assert [n.title_id for n in await service.neighbors_of(_SEED, limit=2)] == [
        _SHARES_TAGS,
        _SHARES_NOTHING,
    ]


async def test_a_higher_cosine_wins_at_equal_jaccard() -> None:
    """The mirror, and without it a Jaccard-only implementation passes the case
    above. Identical genre and keyword sets on both candidates, planted cosines
    of cos(0) = 1.0 and cos(pi/3) = 0.5.

    Both cases together are what "the blend uses every term" means: neither
    alone rules out a single-signal scorer, and a case whose two signals agree
    rules out nothing at all.
    """
    seed, far = planted_pair(math.pi / 3)
    service, embeddings = _service()
    embeddings.given(_SEED, seed, genres=("drama",), keywords=("ledger",))
    embeddings.given(_NEAR, seed, genres=("drama",), keywords=("ledger",))
    embeddings.given(_FAR, far, genres=("drama",), keywords=("ledger",))
    await service.rebuild()
    assert [n.title_id for n in await service.neighbors_of(_SEED, limit=2)] == [_NEAR, _FAR]


async def test_a_pair_with_no_tags_is_scored_on_its_vector_alone() -> None:
    """Absence leaves the denominator as well as the numerator, so the pair is
    scored on what is known rather than penalised for what is not.

    Fails: a blend that divides by `sum(_WEIGHTS.values())` unconditionally.
    That version scores an untagged pair at 0.60x its true cosine agreement,
    which puts every skeleton-shaped enriched title below every richly-tagged
    one *regardless of how close the vectors are* -- and boundary call 4's
    whole point is that the embedded population is the tier where the text is
    the good signal.
    """
    seed, near = planted_pair(0.0)
    service, embeddings = _service()
    embeddings.given(_SEED, seed, genres=(), keywords=())
    embeddings.given(_NEAR, near, genres=(), keywords=())
    await service.rebuild()
    assert (await service.neighbors_of(_SEED, limit=1))[0].score == pytest.approx(1.0, abs=1e-9)


# --- exclusions ------------------------------------------------------------


async def test_a_title_is_never_its_own_neighbour() -> None:
    """Cosine of a vector with itself is 1.0, so without an explicit exclusion
    every title's top hit is itself -- and every "more like this" row's first
    item is the film the user is already looking at. Fails an implementation
    that filters the *rendered* list instead of the stored one, too: the stored
    row would then cost every consumer one wasted slot out of 25.
    """


async def test_a_null_embedding_title_is_neither_a_seed_nor_a_candidate() -> None:
    """**The degenerate-cluster trap: the front matter names it as the one that
    either crashes or, worse, forms a cluster.** Task 12 writes a refused title
    as a row with a NULL embedding so it stops matching the backfill, and those
    rows must not reach this computation. As a candidate the distance is NULL,
    which sorts last -- so it leaks in only when the population is smaller than
    the top-N, arriving as a `TypeError` or, with a careless `coalesce`, a
    distance of 0 that pins every refused title to the top of every list. Both
    directions asserted: no neighbours of its own, and in nobody else's.
    """


async def test_the_top_n_is_capped_and_ordered_best_first() -> None:
    """Thirty candidates at thirty distinct planted angles, twenty-five stored,
    nearest first. Fails an implementation that stores everything (250,000 rows
    becomes 10,000,000) and one that stores the first N the candidate query
    returned rather than the N best *after* the blend -- which is the whole
    point of blending in application code.
    """


async def test_equal_scores_are_broken_by_id_so_two_rebuilds_agree() -> None:
    """Determinism, which here is a *pagination and diff* property: M7 reads
    this table repeatedly and a row that reorders between two identical
    rebuilds makes every "more like this" row shuffle for no reason. Two
    candidates at an identical planted angle and identical tag sets -- genuinely
    common, not contrived -- and "whatever the candidate query returned" is not
    an order.
    """


# --- the artefact ----------------------------------------------------------


async def test_a_rebuild_is_idempotent() -> None:
    """Run twice, same rows, same scores, same order. This is the property that
    makes a batch acceptable in place of a job at all: an interrupted rebuild
    is resumed by running it again.
    """


async def test_a_seed_that_lost_every_neighbour_has_its_old_rows_removed() -> None:
    """**The one row shape a rebuild cannot repair if the delete is scoped
    wrongly**, which is why the seed list is passed to `replace` separately
    from the rows rather than derived from them.

    A seed whose neighbours all disappeared -- the other enriched titles were
    deleted, or every candidate became degenerate -- contributes *no rows* to
    the write. An implementation deriving the delete's scope from the rows
    therefore deletes nothing for it and leaves stale neighbours in place
    forever, through every future rebuild.
    """


async def test_computed_at_distinguishes_never_computed_from_no_neighbours() -> None:
    """Two causes for an empty answer, and only one is a fact about the title.
    One message for both sends an operator to look at the wrong thing --
    `usher similar` needs this to say "run `usher similar --rebuild`" rather
    than "this title has nothing like it".

    Also asserts it reports the **oldest** row's timestamp: the newest would
    report a whole-table rebuild as fresh the moment the first page committed,
    which is "looks healthy while describing yesterday" in one accessor.
    """
```

```python
async def test_the_precompute_excludes_null_embedding_rows_in_sql(...) -> None:
    """The unit case above proves the *service* excludes them; this proves the
    statement does. A fake computes cosine in Python and can skip a NULL by
    accident of its own control flow, where Postgres needs
    `WHERE e.embedding IS NOT NULL` written down -- and without it a
    `halfvec <=> NULL` is NULL, which sorts last and then arrives.
    """


async def test_the_candidate_query_is_an_exact_scan_and_not_the_hnsw_index(...) -> None:
    """Asserted on the plan, not the clock. Recall loss in a live query is
    per-query; recall loss in a cached artefact is permanent, and this table is
    read until the next rebuild. PRD 05 says brute-force exact cosine is the
    right call at this scale (10k x 384 halfvec is 7.7 MB).

    quantisation figures to justify an approximate index would be laundering
    one measurement into a claim about another. `EXPLAIN` output captured, not
    transcribed.

async def test_the_top_five_survive_the_halfvec_round_trip(...) -> None:
    """The measured safe band, exercised rather than cited: max cosine error
    1.21e-04, mean 3.03e-05, top-1 and top-5 ordering identical in 42/42
    queries. Planted angles far enough apart that a 1.21e-04 error cannot
    reorder them, so this fails a *storage* mistake -- a `vector` column
    silently narrowed, or a float32 written through a lossy cast -- rather than
    re-measuring quantisation.

async def test_a_page_costs_one_candidate_statement_not_one_per_seed(...) -> None:
```
