```python
# src/usher/ports/repository.py  (modify)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class NeighborSeed:
    """One embedded title, carrying the tag sets the blend needs -- so a page
    read answers the seed half in one statement rather than ids here plus a
    second `list_by_ids` pulling 31 columns per row for two of them."""

    title_id: uuid.UUID
    genres: tuple[str, ...]
    keywords: tuple[str, ...]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class NeighborCandidate:
    """One candidate neighbour and the raw signals it offers.

    **`cosine`, never a distance.** pgvector's `<=>` is a distance and the
    blend wants agreement, so `1 - (a <=> b)` happens once, in the adapter,
    rather than in a scorer that would then have to know which operator
    produced its input. A signal list whose members disagree about direction is
    how a weight silently becomes a penalty.
    """

    title_id: uuid.UUID
    cosine: float
    genres: tuple[str, ...]
    keywords: tuple[str, ...]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ScoredNeighbor:
    """One row of `title_neighbors`, as the service computed it."""

    title_id: uuid.UUID
    neighbor_title_id: uuid.UUID
    score: float
    rank: int
```

```python
# src/usher/ports/repository.py  (modify -- on TitleEmbeddingRepository, Task 9's port)

    @abstractmethod
    async def list_embedded(
        self, *, after: uuid.UUID | None = None, limit: int = 500
    ) -> list[NeighborSeed]:
        """Titles with a **non-NULL** embedding, in `id` order, after `after`.

        A keyset cursor for the reason Task 15's is one: `OFFSET` pagination is
        measured in this repository at 43.7 ms at offset 0 and 388.9 ms at
        offset 1,126,574 -- linear per page, quadratic to drain.

        **NULL embeddings are excluded here rather than by the caller.** Task
        12 writes a refused title as a row with a NULL embedding so it stops
        matching the backfill; a refused title has no vector to search from and
        is not a seed. Excluding it in the caller would mean every future
        caller has to remember.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def nearest_for(
        self, seed_ids: Sequence[uuid.UUID], *, limit: int
    ) -> dict[uuid.UUID, list[NeighborCandidate]]:
        """The `limit` nearest candidates for each seed, nearest first.

        **Excludes the seed itself and every NULL-embedding row**, and both are
        the implementation's job rather than the caller's. Self-exclusion,
        because cosine with itself is 1.0 and every neighbour list would
        otherwise open with the title the reader is already looking at.
        NULL-exclusion, because `embedding <=> :seed` is NULL, NULLs sort last
        on an ascending order, and so they arrive only when the population is
        smaller than `limit` -- at which point they are either a type error or,
        under a careless `coalesce`, a distance of 0 pinning every refused
        title to the top of every list.

        **A page of seeds rather than one**, so a rebuild costs one statement
        per page instead of one per title: the same round-trip-per-item shape
        `index_many` was introduced to delete from `SearchIndex`, at 10,000
        instead of 1.3M and still worth not reintroducing.

        **Exact, not approximate.** PRD 05: brute-force exact cosine at this
        scale, 10k x 384. Recall loss in a live query is per-query; recall loss
        in a precomputed artefact is permanent, and this one is read until the
        next rebuild.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def count_without_embedding(self) -> int:
        """Rows carrying a `NULL` embedding -- Task 12's written refusals.

        The second half of Task 9's predicate pair, and it exists so the
        exclusion above is *observable*: a rebuild that silently skipped a
        growing swathe of the catalog reads exactly like one with nothing to
        skip. `usher similar --rebuild` prints it.
        """


class TitleNeighborRepository(ABC):
    """`title_neighbors` -- the precomputed similarity artefact (PRD 05).

    **The one derived artefact in M6 whose freshness is not a per-row
    predicate**, and the port says so rather than implying otherwise. A row is
    stale when either title's embedding moved -- which is a predicate, and
    which subsumes the metadata case for free because `compose_document`
    includes genres and keywords. But a row is *also* stale when some third
    title's embedding moved into its neighbourhood, and that is not decidable
    without recomputing the row. So the artefact carries an age, not a
    fingerprint, and it is rebuilt rather than repaired.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    async def replace(
        self, seed_ids: Sequence[uuid.UUID], neighbors: Sequence[ScoredNeighbor]
    ) -> int:
        """Replace every stored row for `seed_ids` with `neighbors`.

        **`seed_ids` is passed separately from the rows and that is not
        redundancy.** A seed whose neighbours all disappeared -- the other
        enriched titles were deleted, or every candidate became degenerate --
        contributes no rows at all, so an implementation deriving the delete's
        scope from `neighbors` deletes nothing for it and leaves its stale
        neighbours in place through every future rebuild. It is the one row
        shape a rebuild cannot repair.

        Returns the number of rows written, which is what makes an operator's
        rebuild report a number rather than a reassurance.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def list_for(self, title_id: uuid.UUID, *, limit: int) -> list[ScoredNeighbor]:
        """One seed's stored neighbours, best first, ties broken by id."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def computed_at(self) -> AwareDatetime | None:
        """The **oldest** stored row's timestamp, or `None` if none exists.

        Oldest rather than newest: the newest would report a whole-table
        rebuild as fresh the moment the first page committed, which is this
        milestone's own failure mode ("looks healthy while describing
        yesterday") wearing an accessor.

        `None` means *never computed*, which is a different fact from "this
        title has no neighbours" and is what stops `usher similar` sending an
        operator to look at the wrong thing.
        """
```

- [ ] **Step 4: The service**

```python
# src/usher/services/similar.py  (new file)

"""PRD 05's similarity blend, and the batch that precomputes it.

`services/` may import only `domain/` and `ports/` (ADR-0009), which is exactly
right: what "similar" *means* is a decision about meaning, and it must not be
able to reach a `halfvec`, an operator class or an index. The database computes
distances; this module decides what to do with them.

**Two of PRD 05's four signals do not exist in `src/` and the blend says so by
its shape.** No `Person`/`Credit` table (boundary call 2), and the MovieLens
tag-genome importer has never been built -- `PHASES` has no `movielens` phase
and `adapters/bulk/` has no `movielens.py` (Task 27 records that gap and gives
it an owner). `collection_id` is a bare nullable UUID with no table that
nothing in `src/` writes. So the blend is a **sum of weighted terms over an
explicit signal list**, and landing a third signal is one `_WEIGHTS` entry, one
accessor and one case -- not a rewritten scorer.
"""

import uuid
from collections.abc import Awaitable, Callable, Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass

from opentelemetry import trace
from pydantic import AwareDatetime

from usher.domain.search import SimilarTitle
from usher.ports.repository import (
    NeighborCandidate,
    NeighborSeed,
    ScoredNeighbor,
    TitleEmbeddingRepository,
    TitleNeighborRepository,
    TitleRepository,
)

_tracer = trace.get_tracer("usher.similar")

# Boundary call 8's signal list. **Chosen with an argument, not measured** --
# nothing in M6 measures similarity relevance (Task 26's gate measures the
# suggest path's recall@5, a different question) -- and the argument for each
# weight is in this task's prose rather than restated here. Two terms rather
# than one Jaccard over the union: five genre elements vanish inside a
# forty-element keyword union and the term nobody weighted does all the work.
#
# **Not `Settings` fields, and Task 23's block is deliberately not amended.** A
# weight is not an operator knob: changing it changes what "similar" means, and
# every row of the precomputed artefact was written under the old meaning. A
# setting invites a table half-computed under each definition with nothing to
# tell them apart -- the state this milestone exists to eliminate.
_WEIGHTS: dict[str, float] = {"cosine": 0.60, "keywords": 0.25, "genres": 0.15}

# 20 is where the halfvec ordering starts to diverge from float32, and there
# the scores are already within 2e-4. 25 is deliberately just past it: PRD 06's
# SimilarityRow renders ten to twenty items and a consumer that filters needs
# headroom, while storing 200 would be storing an ordering the storage format
# cannot honour, at eight times the rows.
_NEIGHBORS_PER_TITLE = 25

# Candidates per seed before the blend. Larger than what is stored, because the
# blend reorders: a candidate ranked 40th on cosine alone can enter the stored
# 25 on tag overlap, and a pool equal to the output leaves the tag terms unable
# to promote anything -- decoration on a pure cosine ranking. Same argument
# `search_suggest_candidates` carries one path over.
_CANDIDATE_POOL = 100


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class NeighborRebuild:
    """What one `usher similar --rebuild` did, as an operator reads it."""

    seeds: int
    rows: int
    # Titles carrying a `title_embeddings` row with a NULL embedding: Task 12's
    # written refusals. Reported rather than merely excluded, because the
    # exclusion is otherwise invisible and a number climbing here is how an
    # operator finds out that a swathe of the catalog composes to an empty
    # document.
    without_embedding: int


class SimilarityService:
    """"More like this", as a lookup rather than a computation.

    PRD 05: "item vectors are static, so this is a cheap batch artifact that
    makes 'more like this' instant and engine-independent." PRD 06's
    `SimilarityRow` is the consumer, in M7, with a TTL of hours.

    **Per boundary call 1 there is no HTTP route here.** M9 owns
    `GET /titles/{id}/similar`, over this service and this table.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        embeddings: TitleEmbeddingRepository,
        neighbors: TitleNeighborRepository,
        titles: TitleRepository,
        commit: Callable[[], Awaitable[None]],
    ) -> None:
        self._embeddings = embeddings
        self._neighbors = neighbors
        self._titles = titles
        # Injected because `services/` may depend only on `domain/` and
        # `ports/` (ADR-0009), and a session is neither.
        self._commit = commit

    async def neighbors_of(self, title_id: uuid.UUID, *, limit: int = 10) -> tuple[SimilarTitle, ...]:
        """One seed's precomputed neighbours, hydrated. A lookup, not a scan.

        Empty for a title that has none **and** for a table that has never been
        built. `computed_at()` is what separates the two, and a caller that
        does not ask will tell an operator that a film has nothing like it when
        the truth is that nothing has run.
        """
        stored = await self._neighbors.list_for(title_id, limit=limit)
        rows = {title.id: title for title in await self._titles.list_by_ids(
            [row.neighbor_title_id for row in stored]
        )}
        return tuple(
            SimilarTitle(
                title_id=row.neighbor_title_id,
                kind=rows[row.neighbor_title_id].kind,
                name=rows[row.neighbor_title_id].name,
                year=rows[row.neighbor_title_id].year,
                score=row.score,
            )
            for row in stored
            # A neighbour deleted since the last rebuild is dropped, not raised
            # -- a stale artefact is expected here by construction, and a
            # KeyError would make a deleted film break every row it appeared in.
            if row.neighbor_title_id in rows
        )

    async def computed_at(self) -> AwareDatetime | None:
        """The artefact's age. `None` means it has never been built."""
        return await self._neighbors.computed_at()

    async def rebuild(self, *, page_size: int = 500) -> NeighborRebuild:
        """Recompute `title_neighbors` for the whole embedded population.

        **A batch, and deliberately not a `JobKind`** -- the unit of work is
        not one title, and there is no natural enqueue. Full argument in this
        task's prose; the short form is that a per-seed job updates the seed's
        own row and leaves every list that should now contain it untouched, and
        a job kind whose trigger is a timer is a cron entry with a queue and a
        park path bolted on.

        **Idempotent and resumable by re-running.** Each page deletes and
        re-inserts its own seeds' rows inside one transaction, so a second run
        writes the same table and an interrupted run is fixed by running it
        again. That property is what makes a batch acceptable in place of a job.

        **A keyset cursor, so it drains.** The cursor advances on `id`, never on
        a predicate: a loop spelled "re-read what looks stale, rebuild, repeat"
        does not terminate against a row the predicate cannot clear, which is
        the non-convergence the watch-history repair shipped once.
        """
        with _tracer.start_as_current_span("similar.rebuild") as span:
            after: uuid.UUID | None = None
            seeds = 0
            rows = 0
            while True:
                page = await self._embeddings.list_embedded(after=after, limit=page_size)
                if not page:
                    break
                candidates = await self._embeddings.nearest_for(
                    [seed.title_id for seed in page], limit=_CANDIDATE_POOL
                )
                written = [
                    row
                    for seed in page
                    for row in _neighbors_for(seed, candidates.get(seed.title_id, []))
                ]
                # The seed ids go in separately from the rows: a seed whose
                # neighbours all disappeared contributes none, and a delete
                # scoped to the rows would leave its stale ones forever.
                rows += await self._neighbors.replace([s.title_id for s in page], written)
                seeds += len(page)
                after = page[-1].title_id
                await self._commit()
            span.set_attribute("usher.similar.seeds", seeds)
            return NeighborRebuild(
                seeds=seeds,
                rows=rows,
                without_embedding=await self._embeddings.count_without_embedding(),
            )


def _neighbors_for(
    seed: NeighborSeed, candidates: Sequence[NeighborCandidate]
) -> list[ScoredNeighbor]:
    """Blend, order, cap. The whole of what M6 means by "similar".

    Ties break by `neighbor_title_id`. Two candidates at the same blended score
    are ordinary here -- one shared genre, no keywords, near-identical cosines --
    and "whatever the candidate query returned" is not an order: this repository
    has measured `UPDATE ... RETURNING` handing rows back in heap order on a
    small table. Without the tiebreak, two identical rebuilds disagree and every
    `SimilarityRow` M7 renders shuffles for no reason.
    """
    scored = [
        (
            _blend(
                cosine=candidate.cosine,
                genres=_jaccard(seed.genres, candidate.genres),
                keywords=_jaccard(seed.keywords, candidate.keywords),
            ),
            candidate.title_id,
        )
        for candidate in candidates
        # Belt and braces with the repository's own exclusion, and cheap: a
        # self-match is cosine 1.0 and would open every list with the film the
        # reader is already looking at.
        if candidate.title_id != seed.title_id
    ]
    scored.sort(key=lambda pair: (-pair[0], pair[1]))
    return [
        ScoredNeighbor(
            title_id=seed.title_id, neighbor_title_id=neighbor, score=score, rank=rank
        )
        for rank, (score, neighbor) in enumerate(scored[:_NEIGHBORS_PER_TITLE])
    ]


def _jaccard(left: Sequence[str], right: Sequence[str]) -> float | None:
    """Set overlap, or `None` when one of the sets has nothing to say.

    **`None` rather than 0.0 -- ADR-0014 applied to a set-valued field.** Two
    wrong implementations, the second worse. `len(a & b) / len(a | b)` raises
    `ZeroDivisionError` on two empty sets, inside a batch job, which aborts a
    rebuild mid-page and leaves a table half old and half new. Returning 0.0 is
    silent: it gives the same answer for "these two share no genres" -- real
    evidence -- as for "we do not know either one's genres", which is a fact
    about enrichment rather than about the films, and scoring it pushes every
    thin title to the bottom of every list while every gauge reads healthy.
    One-empty-one-full is `None` too: an empty side says nothing about overlap.
    """
    first, second = set(left), set(right)
    if not first or not second:
        return None
    return len(first & second) / len(first | second)


def _blend(**signals: float | None) -> float:
    """A weighted mean over the signals that are actually present.

    The same skeleton `SearchService._blend` uses, deliberately: an absent
    signal leaves the numerator *and* the denominator. Dividing by
    `sum(_WEIGHTS.values())` unconditionally would score an untagged pair at
    0.60x its true cosine agreement, putting every thinly-tagged enriched title
    below every richly-tagged one however close the vectors are -- against
    boundary call 4's premise that the embedded population is the tier where
    the text is the good signal. Iterating `_WEIGHTS` rather than adding three
    named terms is what makes boundary call 8's promise true: landing
    tag-genome cosine is one entry here and one accessor above.
    """
    total = 0.0
    applied = 0.0
    for name, value in signals.items():
        if value is None:
            continue
        total += _WEIGHTS[name] * value
        applied += _WEIGHTS[name]
    return total / applied if applied else 0.0
```

- [ ] **Step 5: The rebuild's entry point, and the wiring**

`Pipeline` gains `neighbors: TitleNeighborRepository` and `similar: SimilarityService`, constructed in `build_pipeline` beside `embeddings` (Task 14's field). No embedder is needed — the rebuild reads stored vectors and never embeds anything, which is why `usher similar` in either form starts in 0.13 s rather than paying a 4.84 s model load.

`usher similar` gets a read form and a write form on one subcommand, exactly as `usher index` has `--backfill`:

```python
# src/usher/cli.py  (modify -- build_parser)

    similar = sub.add_parser("similar", help="titles like this one")
    # Optional because `--rebuild` is the write form of the same command. Two
    # subcommands for one artefact is how `usher index` and its backfill would
    # have drifted; the cross-argument rule below is what argparse cannot
    # express and `parse_args` therefore states.
    similar.add_argument("title_id", nargs="?")
    similar.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=10)
    similar.add_argument(
        "--rebuild",
        action="store_true",
        help="recompute title_neighbors for the whole embedded population",
    )
```

```python
# src/usher/cli.py  (modify -- parse_args)

    if args.command == "similar":
        if bool(args.title_id) == bool(args.rebuild):
            # `parser.error` exits 2 with usage on stderr like every other
            # argument failure; a raise would exit 1 with a traceback. Both
            # spellings are refused: no arguments is a read of nothing, and
            # both together is a read and a write in one command.
            parser.error("give a title id, or --rebuild, but not both")
```

The rebuild's report distinguishes the two facts an operator needs, and names the command that fixes the second:

```python
    report = await pipeline.similar.rebuild()
    print(f"rebuilt {report.seeds} seeds, wrote {report.rows} neighbour rows")
    if report.without_embedding:
        print(
            f"{report.without_embedding} titles have no embedding and were excluded "
            "-- run `usher index --backfill` if that is unexpected"
        )
```

And the read form distinguishes "no neighbours" from "never computed", which is Task 25's `test_similar_says_whether_the_neighbours_were_ever_computed`:

```python
    if not rows and await pipeline.similar.computed_at() is None:
        print("no neighbours have ever been computed -- run `usher similar --rebuild`")
    elif not rows:
        print("no neighbours for this title")
```

> **`title_neighbors` is not fresh by construction and nothing in M6 re-runs it.** `usher similar --rebuild` is run by an operator or a cron entry after `usher index --backfill`. PRD 06's "TTL: hours" is a statement about how long M7 may cache what it read, not a promise the table is hours old. The automatic trigger belongs to whichever milestone gains a scheduler — PRD 08 has none — and M7 is the first consumer that would notice. **Task 27 records it against M7 in PRD 09**; an obligation recorded only where it was assumed is one nobody plans, which is the failure boundary call 8 already caught once in this milestone.

- [ ] **Step 7: Mutation-test**

Clear `__pycache__` first (`find src tests -name __pycache__ -type d -exec rm -rf {} +`, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`); mutate **in place**, revert with `cp` backups, never `git checkout --`. If every mutation in the sweep survives, that is the venv-shebang trap and not a finding.

| Mutation | Expected failure |
|---|---|
| `_blend` returns `signals["cosine"] or 0.0` | `test_a_higher_jaccard_wins_at_equal_cosine` — **the single-signal implementation, and the case exists because nothing else in the suite distinguishes it** |
| `_blend` ignores `cosine` | `test_a_higher_cosine_wins_at_equal_jaccard` |
| `_jaccard` returns `0.0` for two empty sets | `test_jaccard_is_none_when_either_side_has_nothing_to_say`, `test_a_pair_with_no_tags_is_scored_on_its_vector_alone` |
| `_jaccard` spelled `len(a & b) / len(a \| b)` | `test_jaccard_is_none_...` — `ZeroDivisionError`, which is the crash-in-a-batch-job half |
| `_jaccard` returns `0.0` when exactly one side is empty | `test_jaccard_is_none_...`'s fifth row |
| `_blend` divides by `sum(_WEIGHTS.values())` | `test_a_pair_with_no_tags_is_scored_on_its_vector_alone` |
| One combined Jaccard over `genres + keywords` | `test_a_higher_jaccard_wins_at_equal_cosine` seeded with a long keyword list — **add that seeding if the mutation survives**; two terms collapsed into one is a silent re-weighting, not an error |
| Drop the `candidate.title_id != seed.title_id` guard *and* the repository's | `test_a_title_is_never_its_own_neighbour` |
| Drop only the service-side self-guard | *survives against the fake* if the fake excludes self. Record it as an equivalent mutant **and keep both**: the repository's exclusion is the one that matters and the service's is one line. Do not "strengthen" the fake to kill it — a fake that models the whole predicate is a second implementation, which is the trap M3's live run recorded when 40 contract assertions passed against a write-back that had never worked. |
| Drop `WHERE e.embedding IS NOT NULL` from `nearest_for` | `test_the_precompute_excludes_null_embedding_rows_in_sql` — **and only the integration case**, which is why it exists |
| `coalesce(e.embedding <=> :seed, 0)` | same case — the version that pins every refused title to the top rather than crashing |
| `scored.sort(key=lambda pair: -pair[0])` | `test_equal_scores_are_broken_by_id_so_two_rebuilds_agree` |
| `replace` derives its delete scope from `neighbors` | `test_a_seed_that_lost_every_neighbour_has_its_old_rows_removed` |
| `computed_at` returns the newest row | `test_computed_at_distinguishes_never_computed_from_no_neighbours` |
| `computed_at` returns a timestamp for an empty table | same case — `None` is the "never computed" signal and a default makes it unreachable |
| `_CANDIDATE_POOL = _NEIGHBORS_PER_TITLE` | `test_a_higher_jaccard_wins_at_equal_cosine` with more than 25 candidates seeded — a pool equal to the output makes the tag terms decoration on a pure cosine ranking |
| `_NEIGHBORS_PER_TITLE = 5` | `test_the_top_n_is_capped_and_ordered_best_first` |
| Rebuild re-reads the predicate instead of advancing `after` | `test_a_rebuild_is_idempotent`, bounded with `asyncio.wait_for` so a non-converging loop fails the case rather than hanging the suite |

```bash
table, and the MovieLens importer has never been built. The blend is a sum of
weighted terms over an explicit signal list, so landing a third is one entry
and one accessor.

Genres and keywords are two terms, not one Jaccard over their union: five genre
elements vanish inside a forty-element keyword union, and genre overlap
saturates anyway (any two dramas score 0.33+). Weights are chosen with an
argument and are not measured -- nothing in M6 measures similarity relevance --
and they are constants rather than settings, because changing one changes what
'similar' means and every stored row was written under the old meaning.

Jaccard of two empty sets is None, not 0.0: the naive spelling raises inside a
batch job, and 0.0 silently gives 'we do not know their genres' the same answer
as 'they share no genres'. Absence leaves both sides of the mean.

NULL-embedding rows -- Task 12's written refusals -- are excluded as seeds and
as candidates, in SQL. A halfvec <=> NULL is NULL, NULLs sort last, so they
arrive only when the population is small, and every whitespace-only document
embeds to the identical vector at cos 1.0000 exactly.

A CLI batch, not a JobKind: the unit of work is not one title, there is no
natural enqueue, and a job kind whose trigger is a timer is a cron entry with a
queue bolted on. The cost is stated -- nothing re-runs it, so PRD 06's 'TTL:
hours' is about M7's cache and not about this table."
```

## Task 22: The staging lock, and the small-batch path that avoids it

`usher.db.staging.stage_records` is three statements and the first two are the
problem:

```python
await session.execute(text(f"DROP TABLE IF EXISTS {table}"))
await session.execute(text(ddl))
```

`DROP TABLE` and `CREATE TABLE` each take an **`ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock** — the
strongest Postgres has — and both are held to commit. The table name is a
module constant, `stg_jobs` and `stg_watch_states`, so it is *shared across
every caller in the deployment*: two sessions in `enqueue` at the same instant
do not run concurrently, the second blocks until the first commits.

That was tolerable when the only caller was a walk. It stopped being tolerable
in M5, and this repository already recorded the moment at
`tests/integration/test_titles_route.py:429-444`, measuring one title read at
ten statements of which **five are the promotion** (`SAVEPOINT`, `DROP TABLE IF
EXISTS stg_jobs`, `CREATE UNLOGGED TABLE stg_jobs`, the `INSERT … SELECT`,
`RELEASE SAVEPOINT`) plus a `COPY` on the raw asyncpg connection the counter
cannot see. That comment ends: *"Recorded rather than fixed: the fix is a
small-batch path in a queue this task does not own … `stg_jobs` is a fixed,
shared table name taking an `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock, so a detail-screen open and
a nightly walk's batch serialise against each other."*

**M6 is what makes it hurt**, which is boundary call 9. Task 13 enqueues one
`index` job in `EnrichService._apply`, per enriched title: a single-row `COPY`
through a table-level exclusive lock on the pipeline's hot path, against the
same `stg_jobs` the walk feeding enrichment is using. `merge_from_source` is the
same shape one table over — the live M5 run measured every `UserDataChanged` at
one item.

**The fix: a small-batch path below a row threshold — a plain parameterised
multi-row `INSERT … ON CONFLICT` that never touches a staging table.** Not a
per-session table name and not `CREATE TEMP TABLE`: temp removes the contention
but still costs two DDL statements and a `COPY` per call, and leaks into
`pg_temp` for the session's life. For five rows the answer is the statement
anyone would have written first.

**The threshold is a module constant, not a setting.** `_SMALL_BATCH_MAX_ROWS =
64`, and it separates two known populations rather than sitting at a measured
optimum: every per-request and per-event caller enqueues **one** row, every
walk-shaped caller up to `sync_batch_size`, default **1,000** (`config.py`).
Any value between them routes each population to the path built for it. Not a
`Settings` field because the two paths must be semantically identical, and an
operator-facing knob on a correctness boundary invites the belief that they are
not — which is the belief that lets a divergence survive review. Task 1's
staging-lock demonstration is where a measured crossover would replace it.

**Identical semantics either side is the whole of the risk.** Every property
`_ENQUEUE` carries has to survive:

| Property | In `_ENQUEUE` | In the small-batch path |
|---|---|---|
| Dedup within a batch | `SELECT DISTINCT ON (kind, key) … ORDER BY kind, key, priority DESC` | The `VALUES` list wrapped in the identical `SELECT DISTINCT ON`. Mandatory: one statement may not hit the same conflict target twice, and Postgres answers `CardinalityViolationError`. |
| Promote, never demote | `priority = GREATEST(jobs.priority, excluded.priority)` | Verbatim, including the `GREATEST` M4's sweep proved is an equivalent mutant — `jobs.py`'s comment keeps both clauses because they say different things. |
| No write for a re-seen job | `WHERE jobs.status <> 'parked' AND jobs.priority < excluded.priority` | Verbatim. This is also what makes the return value "rows written", which `TitleReadService._promote` reads. |
| `created_at` untouched | absent from `SET` | absent from `SET` |

The way to keep them identical is to **not have two copies**: extract the `ON
CONFLICT` tail into one module constant both statements interpolate. A
divergence then needs an edit that breaks both.

**`CAST(:name AS type)`, never `:name::type`.** SQLAlchemy's bind-parameter
regex treats a name followed by `::` as a cast and skips the bind, so the
literal string reaches asyncpg and it answers `PostgresSyntaxError` (CLAUDE.md).
The casts are required rather than tidy: a bare `VALUES` row gives Postgres
`unknown`-typed columns and the `DISTINCT ON` over them will not resolve. The
same regex scans `--` comments, so no comment in these statements may quote a
colon-prefixed parameter.

### The recorded secondary hazard, and what this removes

> A test that commits through `usher.db.staging` leaves its staging table
> behind. … It surfaces as `test_migration_matches_the_orm_metadata` reporting
> schema drift in a *later* file, so the queue suite passes alone and takes the
> migration test down in combination. (CLAUDE.md)

Seven integration files carry explicit `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS stg_*` cleanup for
this — `test_job_queue.py:149`, `test_services_jobs.py:68`,
`test_titles_route.py:112-114`, `test_pipeline_spans.py:212-213`,
`test_cli_pipeline.py:93-94`, `test_sse_end_to_end.py:114-116`,
`test_lanes_in_the_server_process.py:119-120`. Every one is a workaround for a
table that should not have existed for a batch that size, and the small-batch
path removes the need for most of them. **Delete the lines that can no longer
fire**: a cleanup that drops nothing is indistinguishable from one still
needed, which is how the next person re-adds the hazard.

**The trap in the other direction must not be lost.** `usher.db.staging`'s
docstring: the staging tables carry **no constraints**, so a value violating the
destination's CHECK survives the `COPY` and fails at the `INSERT … SELECT` —
through SQLAlchemy, therefore an `IntegrityError`, therefore translatable to
`RepositoryConflict`. A direct `INSERT` surfaces `ck_jobs_key_not_empty` at a
*different point*; still SQLAlchemy, still an `IntegrityError`, but that is a
claim about the new path rather than an inherited fact and needs its own case.
The hazard the `COPY` path did not have: asyncpg rejects a wrong-typed value
**client-side** with `TypeError`, whereas a parameterised `INSERT` sends it and
Postgres answers `DataError`, which no `except IntegrityError` catches.
`JobRequest`'s shape cannot produce one today; recorded on the path, as
`staging.py` records its inverse.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/db/repositories/jobs.py`, `src/usher/db/repositories/watch_state.py`, `src/usher/db/staging.py`
- Modify: `tests/contract/job_queue_contract.py`
- New: `tests/integration/test_staging_lock.py`
- Modify: the seven integration files above

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing equivalence test**

Before the lock case, deliberately: a lock assertion is satisfied by a
small-batch path that is *wrong*; an equivalence assertion is not.

```python
# tests/integration/test_staging_lock.py  (new)


async def _rows(session: AsyncSession) -> list[tuple[str, str, int, str]]:
    """Everything about a job that either path is allowed to determine.

    `id`/`created_at`/`updated_at` excluded: a fresh UUIDv7 and two
    `clock_timestamp()` reads, so requiring them equal would fail the case for
    a reason unrelated to the property.
    """
    result = await session.execute(
        text("SELECT kind, key, priority, status FROM jobs ORDER BY kind, key")
    )
    return [(row.kind, row.key, row.priority, row.status) for row in result]


async def test_both_paths_agree_on_the_same_input(
    session: AsyncSession, other_session: AsyncSession
) -> None:
    """The case this task is judged on.

    The wrong implementation: a small-batch path that is *a* correct upsert but
    not *this* one -- no `DISTINCT ON`, or no
    `WHERE jobs.priority < excluded.priority`, or
    `SET priority = excluded.priority`. Each passes a test that only checks the
    rows arrived, and each changes either the return count
    (`TitleReadService._promote` reads it) or PRD 03's no-demotion property
    (the backfill sweep is the demotion the `WHERE` refuses).

    Held input, moved across the threshold on a second backend, so the two
    paths are compared on identical work rather than on batches that merely
    look alike.
    """
    rows = _batch(_SMALL_BATCH_MAX_ROWS, priority=JobPriority.BACKFILL)
    ...  # enqueue `rows` on one session, `[*rows, one more]` on the other
    assert (written_small, written_large) == (len(rows), len(rows) + 1)
    assert await _rows(session) == [r for r in await _rows(other_session) if r[1] != "extra"]


async def test_both_paths_dedupe_a_repeated_key_the_same_way(
    session: AsyncSession, other_session: AsyncSession
) -> None:
    """Two wrong implementations. Without `DISTINCT ON`, Postgres raises
    `CardinalityViolationError` the moment a walk yields the same item twice --
    which `SourceAdapter.list_items`' contract says it will. With `DISTINCT ON`
    but the wrong `ORDER BY`, an arbitrary member of the group survives, so a
    `BACKFILL` duplicate can beat a `DEMAND` one *within a batch*: no-demotion
    failing at a scale nothing looks at.
    """
    duplicated = [
        JobRequest(kind=JobKind.INDEX, key="t99000123", priority=JobPriority.BACKFILL),
        JobRequest(kind=JobKind.INDEX, key="t99000123", priority=JobPriority.DEMAND),
    ]
    ...  # one row at DEMAND on both paths; padded above the threshold on the second


async def test_the_small_batch_path_still_reports_a_conflict_as_a_port_error(
    session: AsyncSession,
) -> None:
    """`usher.db.staging`'s trap, one path over. The staging tables carry no
    constraints, so `ck_jobs_key_not_empty` fires at the `INSERT ... SELECT`; a
    direct `INSERT` surfaces it at a different statement. Without this, a
    violation on the hot path escapes as a raw `sqlalchemy.exc.IntegrityError`
    from a port whose contract says `RepositoryConflict`.
    """
    queue = PostgresJobQueue(session, max_attempts=5, backoff_seconds=30.0)
    with pytest.raises(RepositoryConflict):
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.INDEX, key="", priority=0)])
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the small-batch path to `PostgresJobQueue.enqueue`**

```python
# src/usher/db/repositories/jobs.py  (modify)

# The row count at or below which `enqueue` skips staging entirely.
#
# **It separates two known populations rather than sitting at a measured
# optimum.** Every per-request and per-event caller enqueues exactly one row --
# M5's demand promotion and M6's index enqueue, one job per enriched title --
# and every walk-shaped caller enqueues up to `sync_batch_size`, default 1,000.
# Any threshold between them routes each population to the path built for it.
#
# **A module constant, deliberately not a `Settings` field.** The two paths
# must be semantically identical, and a knob on that boundary invites the
# belief that they are not. `tests/integration/test_staging_lock.py` runs one
# input through both and compares the stored rows.
_SMALL_BATCH_MAX_ROWS = 64

# The conflict clause, extracted so there is *one* of it. Two statements need
# it byte-identical, and two copies that look alike is how the small-batch path
# silently stops promoting, or starts demoting, or starts counting a re-seen
# job as written.
_ON_CONFLICT = """
ON CONFLICT (kind, key) DO UPDATE SET
    priority = GREATEST(jobs.priority, excluded.priority),
    traceparent = COALESCE(excluded.traceparent, jobs.traceparent),
    updated_at = clock_timestamp()
WHERE jobs.status <> 'parked' AND jobs.priority < excluded.priority
"""


def _small_batch_enqueue(count: int) -> str:
    """The same upsert, over an inline `VALUES` list instead of `stg_jobs`.

    `CAST(:x AS t)`, never `:x::t`: SQLAlchemy's bind-parameter regex treats a
    name followed by `::` as a cast and skips the bind, so the literal string
    reaches asyncpg and it answers `PostgresSyntaxError`. Not optional here
    either -- a bare `VALUES` row gives Postgres `unknown`-typed columns and
    the `DISTINCT ON` over them will not resolve. `DISTINCT ON` for the reason
    the staged path has it: one statement may not hit the same conflict target
    twice, and a walk really does yield the same item twice.
    """
    rows = ", ".join(
        "(CAST(:id_{i} AS uuid), CAST(:kind_{i} AS text), CAST(:key_{i} AS text), "
        "CAST(:priority_{i} AS integer), CAST(:traceparent_{i} AS text))".format(i=index)
        for index in range(count)
    )
    return f"""
    INSERT INTO jobs (id, kind, key, priority, status, traceparent, created_at, updated_at)
    SELECT DISTINCT ON (kind, key)
           id, kind, key, priority, 'pending', traceparent,
           clock_timestamp(), clock_timestamp()
    FROM (VALUES {rows}) AS v (id, kind, key, priority, traceparent)
    ORDER BY kind, key, priority DESC
    {_ON_CONFLICT}
    """  # noqa: S608 -- every value is a bind; only the row *count* is interpolated
```

`enqueue` branches once inside the existing `begin_nested()`, keeping the
`SAVEPOINT` on both paths — its reason is about the caller (`IngestService`
commits a batch of jobs with the walk's own checkpoint), not about staging.
Bind `request.kind.value`, not the member: the enum is not what the column
holds, and every other staged write here spells it the same way.

- [ ] **Step 5: Do the same for `merge_from_source`**

`watch_state.py:195-250` has the identical problem and one extra constraint:
its four statements read `stg_watch_states` through `_deduped(target)`, and the
`UPDATE … FROM deduped` exists precisely because `play_count` must still be
genuinely `NULL` there. A `VALUES` list preserves that — `CAST(:pc_0 AS
integer)` bound to `None` is `NULL`, not `0` — but it must be asserted, because
a `NULL` that quietly became `0` is **ADR-0014's failure at the one layer where
it is permanent**, measured in that module's docstring at `play_count = 7 → 0`.

Parameterise `_deduped(target)` on its source relation (`stg_watch_states`, or
`(VALUES …) AS v (…)`) so `_update`/`_insert` build the literally same SQL
either side of the threshold. The case:
`test_the_small_batch_merge_still_cannot_zero_a_play_count`, seeded through a
raw `INSERT` because `trg_watch_states_set_updated_at` is `BEFORE UPDATE` and an
insert is the only way to own `updated_at`. A path that put a `0` where the
`None` belongs, or cast through a defaulting column type, reproduces the
measured bug exactly: a row holding 7 reads back 0, silently, every walk.

- [ ] **Step 6: Add the `JobQueueContract` case, with its capability flag**

The pattern is `job_queue_contract.py:92-110`'s — a class attribute plus
`pytest.skip`, never a silently-passing assertion.

```python
# tests/contract/job_queue_contract.py  (modify)

class JobQueueContract:
    requires_concurrency: bool = False
    # Whether this implementation runs against a backend whose lock state can
    # be read. A dict has no locks and no `pg_locks`, so the case below is
    # *skipped* by the fake rather than passed -- an assertion that trivially
    # holds against an in-memory implementation is the vacuous-pass failure
    # mode this contract already refuses once, for concurrency.
    observes_locks: bool = False

    async def test_a_small_batch_takes_no_table_level_lock(
        self, queue: JobQueue, lock_modes: LockReader | None
    ) -> None:
        """The wrong implementation it fails: `enqueue` unchanged. `DROP TABLE
        IF EXISTS stg_jobs` and `CREATE UNLOGGED TABLE stg_jobs` each take an
        `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock on a fixed, shared name, held to commit -- so
        M6's one-index-job-per-enriched-title enqueue and a nightly walk's
        batch serialise on the hot path.

        On the lock rather than on wall-clock contention, on purpose: a timing
        case passes whenever the machine is idle, and the failure it exists to
        catch only appears when it is not.
        """
        if lock_modes is None:
            pytest.skip("this implementation has no table locks to observe")
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.INDEX, key="t99000456", priority=0)])
        held = await lock_modes()
        assert "stg_jobs" not in held, (
            f"a one-row enqueue locked {held} -- it went through usher.db.staging"
        )
```

The integration driver supplies `lock_modes` over `pg_locks` joined to
`pg_class`, filtered to `pid = pg_backend_pid() AND locktype = 'relation'`.
Assert on the *relation*, not the mode string: `stg_jobs` being absent is the
property, and a future Postgres spelling the mode differently would pass.

## Task 23: Settings, `.env.example`, and compose

Eight new fields, following the preamble every block since M2 has carried and
for the same reason: PRD 08 puts knobs like these in a TOML config layer that
does not exist yet, so until it does they live here rather than as constants
nothing can tune. The form is `push_*`/`sse_*`'s exactly — a `Field(...)` with
**real** bounds, and a comment above each explaining *why the bound is what it
is*. A bound that is not a constraint is worse than no bound, and this block
has both kinds: compliance expressed as a type (`sse_heartbeat_seconds`'
`lt=60.0` is nginx's idle timeout; `enrich_cache_max_age_days`' `le=180` is
TMDb's licensing term) and "a value an operator could set that turns the guard
off while looking configured" (`push_max_items_per_event`' `le=500`).

**Three guards fire here, each failing for a different reason.**

1. **`test_every_setting_is_read_by_something`** (`tests/unit/test_config.py:427`)
   concatenates every `.py` under `src/usher` except `config.py` and asserts
   every field name appears as the literal `.<field_name>`. So **a field added
   here whose reader lands in a later task fails this commit** — which is why
   group F runs after C and D, and why a field with no reader does not ship.
   It is a substring scan and cannot tell a read from a mention in a docstring;
   still the guard that caught three dead knobs, and not the one to strengthen
   here.
2. **`test_every_usher_variable_in_env_example_is_a_setting_or_compose_reserved`**
   (`test_deployment_config.py:214`) and its `compose.yml` twin. `.env` has two
   readers with different vocabularies — compose substitutes `${...}` from it,
   pydantic-settings reads it with `extra="forbid"` — so a `USHER_` key that is
   not a field makes `cp .env.example .env` fail **every** entry point.
   `USHER_COMPOSE_*` is the reserved namespace the application drops before
   validation; nothing here needs it or may add to it. Its partner
   `test_env_example_documents_every_setting` asserts set-equality, so a field
   with no `.env.example` line fails too.
3. **`test_env_example_ships_the_defaults`** (`:270`) builds `Settings` from
   `.env.example` and from a two-line minimal file and compares `model_dump`s.
   Every documented value must be the field's own default through pydantic's
   coercion — `16`, not `16.0`; `true`, not `True`.

### The fields

```python
# src/usher/config.py  (modify, after the `sse_*` block)

    # Search and embeddings (PRD 05, M6). Same reasoning as every block above:
    # PRD 08's TOML config layer does not exist yet. Named `search_*` /
    # `embedding_*` rather than `postgres_*` or `fastembed_*` -- config.py is
    # not an adapter, and ADR-0002's whole position is that the engine behind
    # these is replaceable.

    # The checkpoint *and the runtime that served it*, which is why it is one
    # string. ADR-0022 measured fastembed against sentence-transformers over
    # 205 documents at min cosine 0.99999619 and top-1 identical 205/205 --
    # close, but the max pairwise-similarity delta of 1.41e-03 is 6x the
    # halfvec quantisation error, so the two are not interchangeable without a
    # re-embed. Recording the runtime makes an implementation swap invalidate
    # every vector through `title_embeddings.model_name` and the stale
    # predicate automatically -- the fingerprint scheme doing its job instead
    # of a migration. `min_length=1` because an empty name makes the stale
    # predicate match everything, forever.
    embedding_model: str = Field(default="fastembed:BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5", min_length=1)
    # Whether this process runs the index lane. Same shape as `push_enabled` /
    # `worker_enabled` and the same PRD 01 reason -- one image serves an
    # all-in-one deployment and a split one -- with one extra consequence
    # ADR-0022 states: **a deployment with this off still has search.**
    # Full-text and trigram are PRD 05's catalog-lookup tier, they serve all
    # 1,271,138 titles, and they need no model. Read by
    # `composition.build_worker`, which registers `JobKind.INDEX` conditionally
    # exactly as it guards `ENRICH` on `provider is not None`.
    index_enabled: bool = True
    # Texts per `Embedder.embed` call. **16 is measured, not chosen**
    # (ADR-0022): 229.5 texts/s at 38 tokens on this host's CPU, flat from 16
    # to 64, degrading at 128. `le=64` is therefore the top of the measured
    # flat region rather than a round number -- above it is slower, and a
    # value an operator could set to 1,024 holds a thousand documents in
    # memory to go no faster. `ge=1` because zero embeds nothing while looking
    # configured.
    embedding_batch_size: int = Field(default=16, ge=1, le=64)
    # The ceiling on `SearchRequest.limit`, applied by `SearchService` before
    # a request reaches an index -- not the default, the most a caller may
    # ask for. `le=200` because every candidate is a row assembled into a
    # `SearchResult` and RRF fuses two lists of this size in application code;
    # 10,000 is a scan wearing a search's name.
    search_result_limit: int = Field(default=50, ge=1, le=200)
    # Reciprocal Rank Fusion's smoothing constant: `1 / (k + rank)`. It sets
    # how fast a hit's contribution decays with rank, so a small k makes rank 1
    # dominate and a large one flattens both lists into near-equal votes. 60 is
    # the value RRF's original paper uses and the one ADR-0002 assumes. `ge=1`
    # because k=0 makes the top rank's weight unbounded against the second's,
    # which is "return the first list".
    search_rrf_k: int = Field(default=60, ge=1, le=1000)
    # `pg_trgm`'s `similarity()` floor for the suggest path. Bounded to (0, 1]
    # because that is `similarity()`'s own range: 0 admits every row in
    # `titles` as a candidate, which is the latency cliff PRD 05 says the
    # narrow path exists to avoid, and 1.0 admits only exact matches, which is
    # `LIKE`.
    search_trigram_threshold: float = Field(default=0.3, gt=0.0, le=1.0)
    # How many trigram candidates are collected before the `levenshtein`
    # re-rank. **This is the cap `test_the_candidate_set_is_capped_before_the_
    # rerank` exists for**: edit distance over the whole table is the exact
    # cliff ADR-0002 names. It must exceed `search_result_limit` or the
    # re-rank can only reorder what the cap already chose -- see the validator
    # below.
    search_suggest_candidates: int = Field(default=200, ge=1, le=2000)
    # pgvector's `hnsw.ef_search`, set per statement rather than globally. The
    # GUC's own default is 40 and is not what this wants: with a filtered
    # query HNSW can return fewer rows than asked for. Larger is more accurate
    # and linearly slower. `ge=1` is pgvector's floor; `le=1000` because beyond
    # that the index is a scan with extra steps.
    search_hnsw_ef_search: int = Field(default=100, ge=1, le=1000)
```

### The cross-field rule

Some constraints are not expressible on one field, and one here matters: **the
suggest candidate cap must exceed the result limit, or the cap is decorative.**
At or below it, the `levenshtein` re-rank is handed exactly the rows it is
meant to choose *among*, so it can reorder but never discard — and a suggest
path that cannot discard is one whose trigram floor is doing all the work. Not
hypothetical: it is what raising `search_result_limit` to 200 and leaving the
cap alone produces.

```python
    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def _suggest_cap_leaves_room_to_choose(self) -> "Settings":
        """A cap at or below the result limit is a cap that cannot cut.

        `PostgresSuggestIndex` collects `search_suggest_candidates` trigram
        matches, re-ranks by edit distance, keeps the best
        `search_result_limit`. With the cap at or below the limit the ordering
        is whatever the trigram floor admitted -- the implementation
        `test_results_are_ordered_by_popularity_within_equal_distance` and
        `test_a_single_character_typo_still_finds_a_short_title` exist to rule
        out, reachable by configuration rather than by code.
        """
        if self.search_suggest_candidates <= self.search_result_limit:
            raise ValueError(
                "USHER_SEARCH_SUGGEST_CANDIDATES must exceed USHER_SEARCH_RESULT_LIMIT "
                "-- the re-rank has to have more candidates than it keeps"
            )
        return self
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```python
# tests/unit/test_config.py  (append)


def test_the_search_settings_have_the_measured_defaults(...) -> None:
    """Eight fields pinned together. `embedding_batch_size` is *measured*
    rather than chosen -- ADR-0022 puts CPU throughput at 229.5 texts/s at
    batch 16, flat 16-64, degrading at 128 -- so the default and the ceiling
    are both facts, and an edit to either is visible here."""


def test_the_embed_batch_stays_inside_the_measured_flat_region(...) -> None:
    """`le=64` is the top of the measured flat region, not a round number: a
    value above it is *slower* while reading like a tuning win. Zero is not "no
    batching", it is a call that embeds nothing. Rejects "0", "128", "1024"."""


def test_the_trigram_floor_stays_inside_similaritys_own_range(...) -> None:
    """`similarity()` returns [0, 1], so a floor outside it is not a strict
    setting but one that silently means "everything" or "nothing". Zero admits
    every row in `titles` to the `levenshtein` re-rank -- the exact cliff PRD
    05 says the narrow path exists to avoid. Rejects "0", "-0.1", "1.5";
    accepts "1.0"."""


def test_the_suggest_cap_is_above_the_result_limit(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
    """The cross-field rule, in the shape
    `test_the_sse_heartbeat_is_under_every_proxy_idle_timeout` established: a
    constraint no single field can express, asserted as a type rather than left
    in a comment. An operator reaches the bad state by raising the limit alone,
    which is the ordinary thing to do."""
    monkeypatch.setenv("USHER_SEARCH_RESULT_LIMIT", "200")
    monkeypatch.setenv("USHER_SEARCH_SUGGEST_CANDIDATES", "200")
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        Settings()
    monkeypatch.setenv("USHER_SEARCH_SUGGEST_CANDIDATES", "201")
    assert Settings().search_suggest_candidates == 201
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Document them in `.env.example`, at their own defaults**

```bash
# Search and embeddings (PRD 05). The index lane is optional: with
# USHER_INDEX_ENABLED=false a deployment still has full-text and trigram
# search over the whole catalog -- those need no model at all -- and only the
# semantic tier and "more like this" go dark.
USHER_INDEX_ENABLED=true
# The checkpoint *and the runtime that served it*. Changing either invalidates
# every stored vector through the stale predicate, deliberately: the two
# runtimes' vectors differ by more than the halfvec quantisation error, so they
# are not interchangeable without a re-embed.
USHER_EMBEDDING_MODEL=fastembed:BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5
# Texts per embed call. 16 is measured on CPU (flat 16-64, worse at 128),
# which is also why the upper bound is 64.
USHER_EMBEDDING_BATCH_SIZE=16
# The most results one search may ask for, and RRF's smoothing constant.
USHER_SEARCH_RESULT_LIMIT=50
USHER_SEARCH_RRF_K=60
# The type-ahead path: pg_trgm's similarity floor, and how many candidates are
# collected before the edit-distance re-rank. The cap must stay above
# USHER_SEARCH_RESULT_LIMIT or the re-rank has nothing to discard.
USHER_SEARCH_TRIGRAM_THRESHOLD=0.3
USHER_SEARCH_SUGGEST_CANDIDATES=200
# pgvector's hnsw.ef_search, applied per statement. Higher is more accurate
# and linearly slower.
USHER_SEARCH_HNSW_EF_SEARCH=100
```

- [ ] **Step 5: `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` has to reach the container**

The one deployment fact here that is not a `Settings` field and must not become
one. Measured, from the front matter: with the cache populated but no network
and `HF_HUB_OFFLINE` unset, the load **fails** with `RuntimeError: Cannot send
a request, as the client has been closed` — huggingface_hub 1.26.0 reuses a
closed client on its retry path instead of falling back to the cache, and the
message names neither the network nor the cache. Reproduced two independent
ways. It is also the only setting under which a genuine cache miss gives a
comprehensible `OSError`.

- **Not in `Settings`.** `USHER_HF_HUB_OFFLINE` would be a field Usher reads and
  then re-exports into the environment before the library imports — a second
  source of truth for a value the library reads directly.
- **In `compose.yml`'s `environment:` block**, which
  `test_compose_overrides_only_what_the_topology_owns` pins to exactly what the
  topology owns — and this *is* topology: a statement about the container
  having a baked cache and no expectation of network. Add it to
  `_TOPOLOGY_OWNED` in `tests/unit/test_deployment_config.py` in the same
  commit with its reason, or that test fails. `environment:` wins over
  `env_file:`, which is the property wanted: an operator cannot unset it from
  `.env`. It goes in CI too, same value.
- **Deliberately not in `.env.example`**: it is not a `USHER_` key, so
  `test_env_example_documents_every_setting` would not see it, and putting a
  container-topology fact in the operator's file is how the `USHER_HOST_PORT`
  outage happened.

## Task 24: Telemetry, and the seventh import-linter contract

PRD 10 owes M6 exactly three metrics:

| Metric | Type | Labels | PRD 10 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `usher.search.duration` | histogram | `mode` | `:88` |
| `usher.search.results` | histogram | `mode` | `:89` |
| `usher.embedding.duration` | histogram | — | `:108` |

**Byte-exact, or the panel is permanently empty and indistinguishable from a
healthy zero.** That is PRD 10's own sentence at `:81-83`, and it is not
theoretical here: M4 found three defects in this table — two documented gauges
that did not exist and one metric emitted under a different name from the one
the dashboard queried. `usher.search.results` in particular invites
`usher.search.result` (singular, matching `usher.enrich.result` two rows up)
and `usher.search.hits`. Neither raises; neither fails a test that asserts "a
histogram was recorded"; both leave a panel that looks like a quiet search box.

`usher.search.results` is a **histogram, not a counter**, and the distinction is
the question it answers: "how many results did a search return" is a
distribution whose interesting values are the zeroes and the ones that hit the
limit. A counter answers "how many results have ever been returned", which
nobody asks. Same reasoning `register_push_gauges` records for choosing an
observable counter over a gauge — a different instrument type under a
documented name is the same class of failure as a near-miss name.

**Spans**, per PRD 10's tree at `:49-50`:

```
index.title                       ← M6
├── index.fulltext · index.embed
```

`index.title` is **not** a root: `job.index` is the worker's root (a root with
a `Link`, PRD 10's documented exception, because the request that enqueued it
has usually returned) and `index.title` is its descendant. That is what makes
"why did indexing this title take 40 seconds" one query, and
`tests/integration/test_pipeline_spans.py` asserts parentage rather than
existence precisely because a service that started its own roots produces valid
ids, exports fine, and passes every other assertion in the repository.
`index.embed` is simply absent when `index_enabled` is false — the work did not
happen.

### Instruments at module scope, following `services/enrich.py:56-72`

```python
# src/usher/services/search.py

_tracer = trace.get_tracer("usher.search")
_meter = metrics.get_meter("usher.search")
# PRD 10's names, not shortened ones, and not `usher.search.result` singular by
# analogy with `usher.enrich.result` two rows up its table. A metric under a
# near-miss name is a dashboard panel that is permanently empty and nothing
# distinguishes it from a healthy zero -- PRD 10 says so at the head of its own
# table, and M4 found three instances of it there.
_search_duration = _meter.create_histogram(
    "usher.search.duration", unit="s", description="Wall time per search, by mode"
)
# A histogram rather than a counter, and the label says why: the useful
# question is the *distribution* of result-set sizes per mode -- how often
# FUSED comes back empty, how often it saturates the limit -- not a running
# total nobody plots.
_search_results = _meter.create_histogram(
    "usher.search.results", unit="1", description="Results returned per search, by mode"
)
```

```python

# No labels, per PRD 10's table, and that is a decision rather than an
# omission: the obvious label is `model`, and adding one makes the series
# unqueryable by the documented panel while looking like an improvement. The
# model is recorded where it belongs -- `title_embeddings.model_name`, where it
# drives the stale predicate.

    "usher.embedding.duration", unit="s", description="Wall time per embed call"

```

The `mode` label's value is `SearchMode`'s `.value` (a `StrEnum`), never
`str(mode)` and never the member — `telemetry.py`'s `QueueSnapshot` docstring
already establishes that a gauge label is a string and the composition root is
what turns an enum into one.

### The stale-embedding gauge, and the three-part module-global pattern

`register_queue_gauges` (`telemetry.py:226`) is the pattern: a frozen snapshot
dataclass, a `Reader` alias, a module-global reader, a `register_*` setter. It
exists for two rules, both of which have to be restated here rather than
referenced, because both are invisible until they bite.

**1. The reader is synchronous and must not query the database.** OTel invokes
an observable callback from the metric reader's own *background thread*, and
every database call in this project is a coroutine on asyncpg. A callback that
queried would have to bounce a coroutine onto the event loop with
`run_coroutine_threadsafe` and block the exporter thread on it — a deadlock
whenever the loop is itself blocked. The shipped answer is a **held snapshot
refreshed by the caller**, exactly as `composition.QueueGauges` does: `read()`
returns the last complete re-read, `refresh(...)` is a coroutine the caller
awaits where awaiting is safe. Stale, never wrong. Here the callers are `usher
index` after each backfill pass and `IndexService` after each job, and the
refresh is one `COUNT(*)` over the stale predicate — which is a predicate
rather than a cursor precisely so counting it is cheap and re-runnable.

**2. The reader is a module global that gets replaced, never a captured
closure.** The SDK keeps only the **first** observable instrument registered
under a name and silently discards the rest — verified directly, recorded at
`telemetry.py:216-222`. A composition root that registered twice, or a second
test in the same process, would leave the first, now-dead reader reporting
forever. Creating the instrument unconditionally while replacing the global is
what lets a *new* `MeterProvider` — one per test in this suite — get instruments
of its own rather than orphans bound to a discarded provider.

```python
# src/usher/telemetry.py  (modify)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class SearchSnapshot:
    """One reading of the embedding backlog.

    Two numbers, because the second is what stops the first being read
    wrongly. `stale` is titles in the embedded population whose vector is
    missing or was derived from different text or a different model -- the
    backfill's own predicate. `refused` is titles carrying a row with a NULL
    embedding: the deliberate written outcome for a degenerate document. A
    refused title is *not* stale, and without the second series an operator
    watching `stale` settle on a nonzero floor cannot tell "the backfill is
    stuck" from "these titles have no text to embed".
    """

    stale: int = 0
    refused: int = 0


SearchReader = Callable[[], SearchSnapshot]

# A module global, replaced rather than captured -- the SDK keeps only the
# first observable instrument registered under a name and silently discards
# the rest, so a second registration in one process would leave the first,
# now-dead reader reporting forever. Same shape, same reason, as
# `_queue_reader` and `_push_reader` above.
_search_reader: SearchReader | None = None


def register_search_gauges(read: SearchReader) -> None:
    """PRD 10's `usher.search.embeddings.stale`, plus its companion.

    Observable rather than recorded, for `register_queue_gauges`' reason: the
    backlog is a fact about a table, not an event stream, and a counter
    incremented at enqueue drifts the moment anything changes a row without
    going through it -- which here is every enrichment, since a new overview
    changes the fingerprint.

    **`read` is synchronous and returns the caller's most recent full re-read,
    not a query.** OTel calls it from the metric reader's background thread and
    every database call here is an asyncpg coroutine, so a querying callback
    would have to `run_coroutine_threadsafe` onto the event loop and block the
    exporter thread on it -- a deadlock whenever the loop is itself blocked.
    `usher index` refreshes after each pass; `IndexService` after each job.
    """
    global _search_reader
    _search_reader = read
    meter = metrics.get_meter("usher.search")
    meter.create_observable_gauge("usher.search.embeddings.stale", callbacks=[...], unit="1", ...)
    meter.create_observable_gauge("usher.search.embeddings.refused", callbacks=[...], unit="1", ...)
```

Both callbacks obey `_observations`' **no reader means no observation, never a
zero** rule: a gauge reporting 0 before anything has read the table is
indistinguishable from a drained backlog, and "the backfill has drained" is the
only claim this series exists to support.

- [ ] **Step 5: Decide the seventh import-linter contract, and record the decision**

The question is live. Contract 6's `forbidden_modules` is the single entry
`["usher.adapters.emby"]`, and its own comment names the weakness: *"Like every
contract here this one enumerates its sources, so a new top-level package added
later is outside it until someone adds it — the same weakness the allowlist
comment at the top of this section names, unfixable in a `forbidden`
contract."*

**The decision: add it.** The criterion contract 6 encodes is "nothing outside
the composition roots may *name* a concrete adapter, and everything is free to
reach one through the port", and there is no version of that criterion true for
Emby and false for search. `PostgresSearchIndex` is exactly the class ADR-0002
says may one day be a Meilisearch class; `FastEmbedEmbedder` is exactly the
class ADR-0022 says is one of two interchangeable runtimes. The two most
swap-prone implementations in the project are the two currently constrained by
nothing.

```toml
# The same criterion as contract 6, for the two implementations most likely to
# be swapped. Until now nothing stopped `services/search.py` naming one
# directly -- which would type-check, pass every test, and quietly make the
# port decorative.
#
# `allow_indirect_imports` for contract 6's reason and not as a loosening: the
# composition root reaches these through `usher.composition`, and
# `usher.api.deps -> usher.composition -> usher.adapters.search.postgres` is an
# indirect chain a forbidden contract reports by default. With the flag that
# chain is KEPT and a direct import in `usher.services` is still BROKEN, which
# is the line the criterion draws. `usher.composition` is absent from the
# source list for the reason recorded in CLAUDE.md: it is the wiring both roots
# share, and the hole is closed by what it *imports* -- a core module reaching
# it breaks contracts two and three, which report indirect chains.
[[tool.importlinter.contracts]]
name = "no concrete search or embedding implementation escapes its package"
type = "forbidden"
source_modules = [
    "usher.domain",
    "usher.ports",
    "usher.services",
    "usher.api",
    "usher.db",
]
forbidden_modules = ["usher.adapters.search", "usher.adapters.embedding"]
allow_indirect_imports = true
```

import — all five contracts above reported KEPT, because none of them
constrains `usher.api` or `usher.db` against `usher.adapters` at all."* Five
contracts reported green against a violation none of them constrained. A

```bash
  >> src/usher/services/search.py
uv run lint-imports            # expected: 1 BROKEN -- and it must be the *seventh* by name
```

If contract 2 ("adapters are driven, not driving") is what breaks, the new
contract has **not** been shown to do anything: that one already covers this
import. Plant a second violation from `usher.api`, which contract 2 does not

usher.adapters.search.postgres` is KEPT with `allow_indirect_imports` and BROKEN
without it. Both halves, or the run proves nothing. Revert with the `cp` backup,

## Task 25: The CLI surface — `search`, `suggest`, `similar`

**Boundary call 1: M6 adds no HTTP route.** The CLI is this project's second
composition root and it delivers every capability the milestone builds, exactly
as M2 did for `bootstrap` and M4 did for the whole ingest pipeline — `sync`,
`sync-status`, `unmatched` and `work` are all "the same capability delivered
through `usher.cli`" while M9 owns the routers and PRD 07's RFC 9457 error
vocabulary. M5 added two routes because demand promotion is *defined* as a
property of requesting a title and had no caller otherwise; nothing in M6 has
that shape, and M7 is the in-process consumer of similarity. PRD 05's
`GET /titles/{id}/similar` is M9's, over the service and the precomputed table
this milestone finishes.

`usher index` is **Task 15's** and is not this task's — it lands with the
backfill it drives. The four together are the milestone's operator surface.

Follow `cli.py`'s shape precisely, because it is five commands deep and the
shape is what keeps them one program:

| Piece | Where | Why there and not elsewhere |
|---|---|---|
| `sub.add_parser(...)` | `build_parser` | It stays a *pure description* of the surface — no validation, no I/O — so a test can introspect it and `--help` can render it without side effects. |
| Cross-argument rules | `parse_args`, via `parser.error` | argparse cannot express "these two go together". `parser.error` exits 2 with usage on stderr like every other argument failure; a `raise` would exit 1 with a traceback. |
| An `elif` arm | `main` | One dispatch in argument order, so a command is one line in each of three places rather than a new mechanism. |
| `async def _<command>(settings, …)` | module scope | `_session_for(settings)` + `build_pipeline(session, settings)`. One engine, one session, disposed however the command ends — a command is one process doing one thing. |
| `print()` | in the command | **Never `logger`.** `cli.py:153-154`: "Printed, not logged: this is a report an operator asked for, and routing it through the JSON log sink would make it unreadable at a terminal." With `USHER_LOG_JSON=true`, the default, a logged table is a JSON envelope per line. |

### `usher search`

```python
    search = sub.add_parser("search", help="search the catalog")
    search.add_argument("query", help="what to search for")
    # SearchMode's three values, taken from the enum rather than retyped: a
    # hand-copied list drifts silently and offers an operator a mode the
    # service cannot serve.
    search.add_argument(
        "--mode", choices=[mode.value for mode in SearchMode], default=SearchMode.FUSED.value
    )
    search.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=20)
    # `SearchFilters`' closed vocabulary, one flag per field and no more. The
    # vocabulary being closed is 🔶 1's settlement -- a `dict[str, Any]` let
    # two backends invent different keys, and a backend that cannot express a
    # filter must raise rather than ignore it, because an ignored filter
    # returns *more* results and reads as working. So this is not "the useful
    # ones"; it is all of them, and a new filter is a port change before it is
    # a flag.
    search.add_argument(
        "--kind", action="append", dest="kinds", choices=[k.value for k in TitleKind]
    )
    search.add_argument("--year-from", type=int, default=None)
    search.add_argument("--year-to", type=int, default=None)
    search.add_argument("--genre", action="append", dest="genres")
    search.add_argument("--owned-only", action="store_true")
    search.add_argument(
        "--min-enrichment", choices=[state.value for state in EnrichmentState], default=None
    )
```

```python
    if args.command == "search":
        if args.year_from is not None and args.year_to is not None and args.year_from > args.year_to:
            # An empty range is not something argparse can see: each bound is
            # individually valid, so a transposed pair parses cleanly and then
            # returns nothing -- which reads as "the catalog does not have it".
            parser.error("--year-from must not be after --year-to")
        if args.limit < 1:
            parser.error("--limit must be at least 1")
```

`--limit` is checked here rather than left to `SearchService`'s ceiling because
the two failures differ: above `search_result_limit` the service clamps and says
so; at zero the operator asked for nothing and meant something.

### The output, and the one line that is not decoration

```python
async def _search(
    settings: Settings, *, query: str, mode: str, limit: int, filters: SearchFilters
) -> None:
    """PRD 05's search, at a terminal.

    **Reports `SearchOutcome.semantic_coverage`, which is the point of this
    command having a human-readable mode at all.** A `FUSED` search against a
    catalog with no embeddings degrades to full-text -- correctly, because a
    title with no vector is *absent from the semantic candidate list* rather
    than ranked last -- and the result looks exactly like a working hybrid
    search. No error, no empty result, no log line. The coverage figure is the
    only thing distinguishing "the blend ranked these" from "the semantic half
    had nothing to contribute", which is why `SearchOutcome` carries it and why
    this prints it whether it is 1.0 or 0.0.
    """
    async with _session_for(settings) as session:
        pipeline = build_pipeline(session, settings)
        outcome = await pipeline.search.search(
            SearchRequest(query=query, limit=limit, mode=SearchMode(mode), filters=filters)
        )
    for rank, result in enumerate(outcome.results, start=1):
        print(f"{rank:>3} {result.score:6.4f} {result.title_id} {result.name} ({result.year})")
    if not outcome.results:
        print("no match")
    # Always, not only when it is low: a number an operator sees only when
    # something is wrong is a number they have no baseline for.
    print(
        f"mode={outcome.mode.value} results={len(outcome.results)} "
        f"semantic_coverage={outcome.semantic_coverage:.3f}"
    )
    if outcome.mode is SearchMode.FUSED and outcome.semantic_coverage == 0.0:
        print(
            "warning: no title in the filtered population has an embedding, so this "
            "was full-text only -- run `usher index` to build them"
        )
```

The warning names the command that fixes it, which is the difference between a
diagnostic and a complaint.

### `usher suggest` and `usher similar`

```python
    suggest = sub.add_parser("suggest", help="type-ahead over titles")
    suggest.add_argument("prefix")
    suggest.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=10)

    similar = sub.add_parser("similar", help="titles like this one")
    # A title id, not a name: `SimilarityService` reads the precomputed
    # neighbour table by id, and resolving a name here would put a second,
    # differently-behaved search in front of a lookup. An operator holding a
    # name runs `usher search` first, which prints the id in every row -- which
    # is why that column is there.
    similar.add_argument("title_id")
    similar.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=10)
```

`similar` converts with `_as_uuid(args.title_id, "title id")` (`cli.py:443`),
which raises `SystemExit` with a sentence rather than letting `uuid.UUID` raise
`ValueError` — the treatment `--resolve`/`--title` already get.

**`usher similar` must distinguish "no neighbours" from "never computed".** The
neighbour table is a batch artefact (Task 21), so an empty answer has two causes
and only one is a fact about the title. One message for both sends an operator
to look at the wrong thing.

```python
def test_the_filter_flags_are_search_filters_whole_vocabulary() -> None:
    """One flag per `SearchFilters` field, checked against the dataclass rather
    than a list. The wrong implementation: a CLI offering the three filters
    somebody needed on the day. A filter with no flag is a capability the port
    declares, the backend implements, and no operator can reach -- and because
    `SearchFilters` is frozen and slotted, adding a field later without a flag
    is silent."""
    assert {f.name for f in dataclasses.fields(SearchFilters)} <= {
        action.dest for action in _search_parser_actions()
    }


def test_similar_rejects_a_title_id_that_is_not_a_uuid() -> None:
    """`_as_uuid`, the treatment `--resolve`/`--title` already get: a sentence
    naming the argument, not a `ValueError` traceback out of `uuid.UUID`."""
```
