## Task 26: ADR-0002's gate, run against the real catalog

ADR-0002 stakes the whole Postgres-first decision on a measurement, and it is the only ADR in this repository that ends by naming the hole underneath itself:

> **Uncertainty.** No rigorous public benchmark of `pg_trgm` typo tolerance against Meilisearch/Typesense on a labelled dataset appears to exist. That hole sits directly under this decision — which is exactly why the upgrade gate is an empirical test against our own catalog rather than a judgement call.

Five milestones later the hole is still there, and every contract case written in Tasks 4 and 17 is a *synthetic* short title chosen by the person writing the case. `test_a_single_character_typo_still_finds_a_short_title` proves `PostgresSuggestIndex` is not a bare `LIKE 'prefix%'`; it says nothing about what happens when the candidate set is 1.27M real names, most of them foreign-language, many of them one word, and thousands of them differing from each other by a single character. **That distribution is the residual gap the fake table in "Where each new fake is more forgiving" names explicitly, and this task is the only thing that closes it.**

PRD 05 specifies the experiment exactly, and it is worth quoting rather than paraphrasing because both halves are load-bearing:

> Build a typo test set from real catalog titles, **weighted toward short names** — where trigram similarity is genuinely weak (a four-character word yields ~5 trigrams; one typo destroys most of them, and transpositions are close to a blind spot). If **recall@5** on that set falls below the bar after honest tuning, add Meilisearch for the instant-search box only.

**Two things this task must not do.** It must not add Meilisearch — boundary call 7 settles that, whatever the number says. And it must not commit the test set — "ship importers, never data" applies to the *output*, and the test set is by construction a list of real catalog titles.

**Files:**
- Modify: `docs/prd/decisions/0002-postgres-first-search.md`
- Modify: `docs/prd/05-search-and-similarity.md`
- Modify: `CLAUDE.md`
- Modify: `docs/plans/progress.md`
- **Not** in the repository: the driving script, the catalog, the test set, anything the run printed.

- [ ] **Step 1: Rebuild a real catalog, without re-downloading anything**

`data/postgres` no longer exists on this host — the M4/M5 container volumes are gone. `data/bulk` does still hold every dump `bootstrap` needs, downloaded 2026-07-30 and still carrying its `.revision` sidecars:

| File | Size | What it feeds |
|---|---|---|
| `title.basics.tsv.gz` | 224 MB | Phase 0 — the 1,271,138 retained titles |
| `title.ratings.tsv.gz` | 8.6 MB | Phase 0 — `community_rating`, `vote_count` |
| `movie_ids_07_30_2026.json.gz` | 27 MB | Phase 1 — TMDb movie id export |
| `tv_series_ids_07_30_2026.json.gz` | 5.0 MB | Phase 1 — TMDb series id export |

So the bootstrap re-imports from disk. The Wikidata crosswalk phase is the only one that reaches the network, and it is not needed for this gate — the gate reads `titles.name`, `titles.year`, `titles.kind` and `titles.popularity`, none of which the crosswalk writes.

```bash
cd /home/anirudhlath/code/usher
docker run -d --name usher-gate -e POSTGRES_USER=usher -e POSTGRES_PASSWORD=usher \
  -e POSTGRES_DB=usher -p 5433:5432 pgvector/pgvector:pg17

set -a; . ./.env; set +a
export USHER_DATABASE_URL="postgresql+asyncpg://usher:usher@localhost:5433/usher"
uv run alembic upgrade head
uv run usher bootstrap --phase imdb
uv run usher bootstrap --phase tmdb-ids
uv run usher bootstrap-status
```

**`set -a; . ./.env; set +a` rather than a literal value on the command line.** This is the established pattern — `CLAUDE.md`'s Commands section spells `scripts/capture_tmdb_fixture.py` exactly this way, with the reason attached: *"so no credential ever reaches a shell history or a recorded command."* The `USHER_DATABASE_URL` override above is a scratch container on port 5433 and is deliberately the one thing typed literally, because it holds no secret and because pointing the gate at the same database a dev shell uses is how a "measurement" quietly becomes a schema change.

**Expected:** `titles in catalog: 1271138`, with `imdb.title.basics` and `imdb.title.ratings` both `completed`. If the count differs, say so and record the number you got — the dumps carry a `.revision` and IMDb's own file moves. Do **not** silently proceed against a different catalog and report the number as though it were this one; M4's live run's whole discipline is that a figure derived from a moving library is a snapshot with a date on it, not a constant.

Then apply M6's own schema and indexes to it:

```bash
uv run usher index --create-indexes          # Task 8's out-of-transaction path
uv run usher index --backfill --dry-run      # how many titles the stale predicate claims
```

The backfill itself is **not** required for this task. The gate is about `pg_trgm` and `levenshtein_less_equal` over `titles.name`, which is boundary call 4's "catalog lookup" tier and needs no embedding at all. Running it anyway over 1.27M titles is 4–6 hours (the measured ~83 texts/s at a realistic ~100–130-token document) for a number this task does not use.

- [ ] **Step 2: Build the typo test set — outside the working tree, and it does not come back**

The driving script lives at `/tmp/m6-gate/gate.py` or anywhere else outside `/home/anirudhlath/code/usher`. Same rule M3's, M4's and M5's live runs followed and `CLAUDE.md` records: **a live-verification run must not write a credential, a token, a user id or a host into the repo**, and here the rule extends one step further — it must not write a *catalog row* either.

Sampling, stated as a procedure precisely enough to be re-run:

1. **Stratify by name length**, because that is the axis PRD 05 says the weakness lives on. Five bands over `char_length(name)`: 2–4, 5–7, 8–11, 12–19, 20+. Draw an equal number from each — this is the "weighted toward short names" instruction made concrete, and it is *deliberately not* the catalog's natural distribution, which is dominated by long names and would drown the signal.
2. **Restrict to `kind = 'movie'` and a `popularity`/`vote_count` floor** high enough that the name is one a person would plausibly type. A 1.27M-row IMDb skeleton is mostly obscure television and shorts; measuring recall over names nobody searches for measures the wrong population. Record the floor and how many rows survive it.
3. **Exclude names that are not unique in the catalog** at the sampling stage, and say how many were excluded. Recall@5 for a name three titles share is a question about disambiguation, not about typo tolerance, and mixing the two produces a number that means neither.
4. **Generate four typo classes per sampled name**, one mutation each, at a uniformly random position:

   | Class | Mutation | Why it is its own class |
   |---|---|---|
   | **substitution** | one character replaced | the ordinary case; destroys up to 3 trigrams |
   | **deletion** | one character removed | shortens the trigram set as well as breaking it |
   | **transposition** | two adjacent characters swapped | **ADR-0002 names this as close to a blind spot.** Breaking it out is the whole point — averaged into one number it disappears |
   | **doubled letter** | one character repeated | the commonest real typing error and the one an edit-distance re-rank should trivially catch |

   Seed the RNG and record the seed. An unseeded run cannot be re-run, and "we measured 0.87" with no way to get the same 0.87 back is not a measurement.

5. **Query through the shipped code**, not through hand-written SQL. `PostgresSuggestIndex.suggest(prefix, limit=5)` — the real cap, the real `levenshtein_less_equal` re-rank, the real popularity ordering. A gate run against SQL typed into the script measures a query nobody ships. Import it the way the CLI does, through `composition`.
6. **recall@5 is: was the title the typo was generated *from* in the five hits.** Not "a title with a similar name". Not "at least one hit". The identity is known because the script generated the typo, and that is the whole reason a synthetic-typo experiment can be scored automatically at all.

- [ ] **Step 3: Record the measurement, and not the data**

**What may not be committed:** the sampled names, the generated typos, any `titles` row, any IMDb id outside the reserved `tt99\d{6}` band, any file the run wrote. The test set is built from real catalog titles by construction, so **the test set is third-party data** and "ship importers, never data" governs it exactly as it governs a fixture.

**`tests/unit/test_no_third_party_data.py::test_no_dataset_row_is_committed_anywhere` is the control, and it scans `docs/` — which means it scans this plan and it will scan whatever this task writes into `docs/prd/`.** That check is location-independent by design: it matches on the *shape* of a dataset row (a tconst followed by a tab; a JSON object carrying `original_title`/`original_name`) rather than on where the row sits, and it is the check that caught the two the other three missed — one of them a *plan document* that prescribed real IMDb rows verbatim, "data, and the instruction that recreates it, which is the worse half". Pasting a table of real catalog titles into ADR-0002 to illustrate the gate is precisely that failure a second time. Every example name written into the PRD, the ADR or `CLAUDE.md` by this task is synthetic.

**What gets committed is the measurement**, in enough detail to regenerate it:

```markdown
Recall@5, `PostgresSuggestIndex` over 1,271,138 real catalog titles,
measured <date>. N = <n> names per length band × 4 typo classes,
seed <seed>, popularity floor <floor>, <k> non-unique names excluded.

| name length | substitution | deletion | transposition | doubled letter | n |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–4   | | | | | |
| 5–7   | | | | | |
| 8–11  | | | | | |
| 12–19 | | | | | |
| 20+   | | | | | |
| all   | | | | | |
```

Sample size per cell, always. A 0.62 over 20 names and a 0.62 over 2,000 are different claims and the table must not render them identically. And the generation procedure in prose — the five bands, the four mutations, the floor, the uniqueness filter, the seed — so that someone with the same `data/bulk` can reproduce the number rather than take it on trust.

**Also record the shape of the failures, not just the rate.** For each typo class, the median rank of the true title when it *was* returned (2nd? 5th?), and for the misses, whether the true title was absent from the capped candidate set at all or present and out-ranked. Those are different defects with different fixes — the first is the trigram cap, the second is the re-rank — and a single recall number cannot tell them apart. Task 17's cap is the knob for the first and `levenshtein_less_equal`'s bound is the knob for the second; "after honest tuning" in PRD 05's sentence means both were moved and the number recorded at each setting, not that one configuration was tried.

- [ ] **Step 4: Decide, and record the decision either way**

**The bar is stated before the number is known**, in this task, and it is written down before Step 2 runs. Choosing a threshold after seeing the result is not a gate. PRD 05 does not name a figure; this plan proposes **recall@5 ≥ 0.90 on the 8+ bands and ≥ 0.75 on the 2–4 band, with no single typo class below 0.60 in any band** — an autocomplete box that finds the right title four times in five for a short name is usable, and one that finds it half the time is the thing ADR-0002 says only Meilisearch and Typesense handle properly. If the implementer disagrees with those numbers, change them **in this document, before running the experiment**, with the reasoning.

**If it passes:**

- ADR-0002's `## Uncertainty` section is **retired into a measured fact**, not deleted. The section becomes `## Evidence — the gate, measured` (or the paragraph moves under the existing `## Evidence`) and states what was measured, over what, when, and what the number was. The convention is `ports/source.py:151`'s `**Settled in M6** —` form and `.claude/rules/prd-maintenance.md`'s rule that a reversed or resolved position is *updated with the new evidence*, never silently contradicted.
- The honest qualifier goes in with it: this measures `pg_trgm` + `levenshtein_less_equal` against *our own catalog*, which is what the gate asked for; it is still **not** the head-to-head against Meilisearch/Typesense that the Uncertainty paragraph says does not exist. The hole ADR-0002 names is narrowed, not filled, and saying so is the difference between evidence and a laundered claim.
- PRD 05's gate paragraph gains the result and a pointer to the ADR.

**If it fails:**

- **M6 does not add Meilisearch.** Boundary call 7, restated here because this is the step where the temptation lands: *"ADR-0002's gate is a measurement with a decision attached, and PRD 09 lists Meilisearch under post-v1 candidates. If it fails, M6's deliverable is the recorded failure, the ADR updated with it, and a scoped follow-up — not a second stateful service bolted on at the end of a milestone."*
- ADR-0002's **Status** line gains the failure: it stays `Accepted` for the full-text and semantic halves, which the gate says nothing about, and its `## Consequences` gains that the instant-search box specifically did not clear the bar. The decision was always conditional; a conditional decision whose condition fired and was not written down is a decision nobody can act on.
- **The follow-up is scoped in PRD 09 with an owner**, in the post-v1 candidates list, and it names the cost concretely: `SuggestIndex` is a separate port (🔶 2, ADR-0021) precisely so this costs one class plus a write path. Say which class, say that the write path is the dual-write ADR-0002 refused, and say that adding it is the deliberate act the port split exists to make visible.
- The recorded number is what makes the follow-up plannable at all. **Either way the number is committed** — a gate run and not written down is a gate not run.

- [ ] **Step 5: Report guess by guess, including what the run refutes**

M3's run found the write-back route was simply wrong. M4's found the exact-name tier matches ~74% where the plan expected "almost nothing" — *"this reverses the plan's stated expectation and it is the single most load-bearing number the live run produced."* M5's found four of thirteen documented guesses wrong, including `X-Emby-Token` as a header, which the plan expected to work and which turns out to authenticate as nobody.

**A live run that confirms everything it expected is a run that did not look hard enough.** Write the report as a table with a verdict column — **confirmed / refuted / still unverified** — the way `CLAUDE.md`'s TMDb section does, and put the refutations first. Guesses this plan makes that the run can settle, each of which is a real thing to look at rather than a formality:

| # | Guess this plan makes | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Short names are the weak band and transposition is the weakest class | ADR-0002's own reasoning, never measured |
| 2 | The cap is the binding constraint, not the re-rank — i.e. most misses are candidates that never made the capped set | Task 17's whole design; if it is the *re-rank* that misses, the cap is tuned in the wrong direction |
| 3 | Recall degrades monotonically with decreasing name length | plausible and untested; a non-monotone curve would mean length is a proxy for something else (language? word count?) |
| 4 | A one-word name and a multi-word name of the same length behave the same | assumed by the length-only stratification, and *`pg_trgm` pads on word boundaries*, so it may well be false — if it is, the stratification above is the wrong one and the correction belongs in the record |
| 5 | The synthetic contract cases in Tasks 4 and 17 predict real behaviour | the fake table's stated residual gap; check the shipped contract cases' own short titles against the real distribution and say whether they were representative |
| 6 | `usher index --create-indexes` over a populated 1.27M-row `titles` completes in a workable time | Task 8 decided it runs outside a migration because `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` cannot run in one; this is the first time it meets a full catalog. Record the wall clock and the index sizes |
| 7 | The full-text half is unaffected by any of this | it is a different index and a different query, but *say so having checked* rather than by omission — one `websearch_to_tsquery` latency sample at 1.27M against the same catalog costs nothing and is the number PRD 05's match-set-cardinality argument rests on |

Anything the run **could not** settle gets its own paragraph, in the "Not verified in this run, and named rather than implied" form every earlier run used. Candidates: real *typed* queries as opposed to synthetically-mutated ones (which is exactly what `search_queries` is for, and Task 27 assigns it to M9); multi-typo queries; queries against a name in a script `pg_trgm`'s trigram extraction handles differently; and the head-to-head against Meilisearch, which this run deliberately does not build.

## Task 27: The PRD and the three ADRs

**This is not a documentation pass at the end of the milestone.** `CLAUDE.md`'s standing rule is unambiguous:

> **Update the PRD in the same commit as the change that invalidates it.** Not in a follow-up, not "later". A change that alters behaviour and leaves the PRD stale is incomplete.

So what follows is a **checklist of what each earlier task owes**, enumerated file by file so that a reviewer of Task 6 or Task 16 can look up what that task was supposed to have carried with it. Anything still outstanding when this task runs is a task that shipped incomplete, and the fix is to land it here rather than to pretend the rule held.

The three ADRs are the exception and genuinely belong here: an ADR records a decision *and its consequences*, and two of the three (0020, 0022) carry evidence that only exists once the thing is built and measured.

**Files:**
- Modify: `docs/prd/05-search-and-similarity.md`, `09-roadmap.md`, `10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md`, `03-sources-and-sync.md`, `01-architecture.md`, `02-data-model.md`, `decisions/README.md`, `README.md` (repo root), `CLAUDE.md`
- Create: `docs/prd/decisions/0020-derived-state-carries-its-fingerprint.md`
- Create: `docs/prd/decisions/0021-the-suggest-path-is-its-own-port.md`
- Create: `docs/prd/decisions/0022-the-embedder-is-optional-and-its-contract-is-measured.md`

- [ ] **Step 1: `05-search-and-similarity.md` — four corrections and three 🔶 retirements**

This is the section M6 *is*, and four of its statements are now false. Each correction states **what shipped and why**, in the PRD's own voice — the outcome, with the reasoning behind an ADR link. Never a deletion: a reader who remembers the old text needs to find out what happened to it.

**(a) Weight class B is reserved and empty.** The `### Full-text` section specifies "A: name and alternate titles, **B: cast and crew**, C: overview". There is no `Person`, `Credit`, `Collection` or `Image` table, domain model or port anywhere in `src/` — `ports/metadata.py:68-82` defers all four to M7 and M9 by name, and PRD 09's M4 boundary call 2 says the same. The only place credits physically exist is `raw_payloads.payload`, and assembling a search document out of a *provider's* JSON shape would put a TMDb-shaped concept in `services/`.

> The shipped document reserves weight class **B** and leaves it empty. `SearchService`'s document builder takes a `credits: Sequence[str] = ()` argument that is always empty in M6. The class is *reserved rather than repurposed* — moving overview up into B would make the weights mean something different the day credits arrive, and the whole point of a stored generated column is that filling B later is a migration rather than a rewrite. Boundary call 2; `Person`/`Credit` are M7's ([09](09-roadmap.md)).

**(b) There is no narrow `title_search_names` table.** The `### Autocomplete` section specifies "a narrow `(title_id, name, kind, popularity)` table over names and aliases". Its justification is aliases and people names — one title contributing many rows — and neither has a data source in M6, so in M6 the table would hold exactly one row per title duplicating `titles(id, name, kind, popularity)`.

> The trigram indexes go **directly on `titles`**. A second copy of four columns that `titles` already holds is a second thing to keep fresh, and a new instance of exactly the staleness problem this milestone exists to eliminate. Task 12 measured the narrow-table variant against the direct one; the number is recorded here. **When M7 lands aliases and people, the narrow table is the migration that adds them** — and at that point it is a table holding rows `titles` does not, which is what its justification always was. Boundary call 3.

Record Task 12's measured comparison inline. A boundary call with a number attached is a decision; the same call without one is a preference.

**(c) Embeddings cover the enriched tier, not 1.27M.** The `### Semantic` section reads as though every title gets a vector. Boundary call 4 is this section's own two-workload split taken seriously — the table at the top of this very file already says catalog lookup is ~1.3M and the library experience is ~2k–10k.

> The `index` job's population is `enrichment_state <> 'skeleton'`, for which `ix_titles_enrichment_state` is already the partial index that exists. A skeleton title is a name and a year; embedding it produces a vector *of the name*, which full-text already does better and cheaper. **A skeleton title needs no `index` job at all** — its search document is a generated column, so it is fully indexed the moment it is written. At the measured ~83 texts/s for a realistic ~100–130-token document, this is the difference between **4–6 hours** over 1,271,138 titles and **~25 seconds to 2 minutes** over the enriched tier. Boundary call 4.

**(d) `sentence-transformers` is replaced by `fastembed`.** The `### Semantic` section names "a local sentence-transformers model (`bge-small-en-v1.5`)". The checkpoint is unchanged; the runtime is not, and the reason is not taste.

> **`sentence-transformers` cannot be a dependency of this project.** 59 packages, **2.62 GiB downloaded**, **4.8 GiB installed**, 104 s cold install — against a current `usher` image of **332 MB**. It resolves `torch`, and **~4.5 GiB of the 4.8 GiB is GPU runtime** pulled unconditionally onto a host that may never have a GPU. **`fastembed` is better on every measured axis**: 28 packages, **167 MiB installed** (29× smaller), **1.2 s** cold install, **no torch**, a 65 MB model artefact, **252.9 texts/s against 229.5** on identical input (+10%), peak RSS 1,067 MiB against 1,381 MiB. The vectors agree: over 205 documents, **minimum cosine 0.99999619**, **top-1 identical 205/205**, top-10 mean overlap 1.0000. Measured 2026-08-02; [ADR-0022](decisions/0022-the-embedder-is-optional-and-its-contract-is-measured.md) carries the two caveats and the full numbers.

**Retire the three 🔶 blocks in place.** All three name M6 and all three are settled. The established form is `ports/source.py:151`'s — a `**Settled in M6** —` paragraph replacing the marker, recording the answer *and its evidence*, never a silent deletion. `.claude/rules/prd-maintenance.md` is explicit that a marker is how incompleteness is made visible; removing one without saying what happened converts "undecided" into "settled" with no record that anyone decided anything.

| 🔶 | Where | What replaces it |
|---|---|---|
| autocomplete's own port | `### Autocomplete` | **Settled in M6 — yes.** `SuggestIndex` is a separate ABC with one method. The argument is dual-write visibility, not tidiness. [ADR-0021](decisions/0021-the-suggest-path-is-its-own-port.md) |
| the `Embedder`'s query/document split and its normalisation contract | `### Semantic` | **Settled in M6 by measurement, and both halves resolve *against* the previous wording.** One `embed`; the prefix is measured at −0.0028 MRR and prefixing both sides at **−0.0663**. Normalisation is real but is a property of *this checkpoint*, and is load-bearing only under the inner-product operator. [ADR-0022](decisions/0022-the-embedder-is-optional-and-its-contract-is-measured.md) |
| `SearchIndex`'s shape | `## The upgrade path` | **Settled in M6.** All four named defects fixed: `index_many(documents)` not `index(title_id)`; `SearchFilters` with a closed vocabulary replacing `filters: dict[str, Any]`; `query_vector` on the request; and **no `rebuild`**, deliberately — the predicate-driven backfill already rebuilds from scratch, and a port method whose only test is its own test is a liability |

The gate paragraph at the end of the file gains Task 26's result and a link to ADR-0002.

- [ ] **Step 4: `03-sources-and-sync.md` — stage 4 exists now**

The whole of `### 4. Index` is three lines:

> Update the search document and compute the embedding ([05](05-search-and-similarity.md)). Both derive from the Title, so this stage is a pure function of catalog state and can be rebuilt from scratch at any time.

Every other stage in that pipeline carries what it does, what it costs and what it deliberately does not do. Stage 4 now can too. It needs to say:

- **The two halves are asymmetric on purpose, and that is the milestone's central decision.** The search document is a `GENERATED ALWAYS AS (…) STORED` column on `titles`, recomputed by PostgreSQL inside the same statement that writes `name` or `overview` — so no code path, including a bulk `COPY`, a hand-written `UPDATE` or a future migration, can write a title and skip its document. The embedding needs a model, so it cannot be generated; it is a `JobKind.INDEX` job, and `title_embeddings` carries `model_name` and a `source_fingerprint` of the exact text embedded so that "is this stale?" is a SQL predicate. [ADR-0020](decisions/0020-derived-state-carries-its-fingerprint.md).
- **The last sentence is still true and now means something operationally**: "a pure function of catalog state, rebuildable from scratch at any time" is what makes the backfill a *predicate* rather than a cursor over everything — self-draining, idempotent, and re-runnable at zero write cost, because `enqueue`'s `WHERE jobs.priority < excluded.priority` means a re-run writes nothing.
- **What it deliberately does not do**, each with one clause of reason:
  - **it publishes no event** (boundary call 5 — `EnrichService` already publishes `title.updated`, and a second one would have no consumer);
  - **it does not embed skeletons** (boundary call 4 — a skeleton's document is a generated column, so a skeleton needs no job at all);
  - **it does not fill weight class B** (boundary call 2 — no `Credit` table exists, and a generated column makes filling it later a migration);
  - **it does not rebuild the document** (that would make the cheap, always-correct half depend on the expensive, fallible half, and a parked embedding job would then also mean a stale full-text document with the two failures indistinguishable);
  - **it refuses a degenerate document, and records the refusal** — a title with no overview, no genres and no keywords gets a `title_embeddings` row with a `NULL` embedding rather than no row, so it stops matching the stale predicate instead of being re-claimed by the backfill forever. This project has shipped that exact bug once already (the watch-history repair refused by the very row it existed to repair, which then matched `played AND play_count = 0` permanently), and the reason it is worth a sentence in the PRD is that it is a *class* of bug this pipeline keeps producing.

One more correction while in this file: the `index` enqueue is a new write on `EnrichService._apply`'s hot path — one job per enriched title — so the sentence about what enrichment does on completion gains it.

- [ ] **Step 6: `02-data-model.md` — the new tables**

`### Embedding` describes a `TitleEmbedding` with `model`, `dimension`, `vector`, `source_text_hash`. The shipped shape differs in three ways that each carry meaning, and the PRD gets the shipped one:

- **`model_name` records the runtime as well as the checkpoint** — `fastembed:BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5`, not `bge-small-en-v1.5`. The ST↔fastembed vector difference (max pairwise-similarity delta **1.41e-03**) is **6× larger than the halfvec quantisation error**, so the two are not interchangeable without a re-embed. Recording the runtime makes an implementation swap invalidate every vector through the stale predicate automatically — the fingerprint scheme doing its job rather than a hand-written migration. The existing sentence ("Model name is stored so a model change is a detectable, re-embeddable event rather than silent vector-space corruption") is *right* and this is what it looks like when it is load-bearing.
- **`source_fingerprint`** rather than `source_text_hash` — the name the code uses, and it is `md5` of the exact assembled text, which is the *same assembly the builder uses* and is therefore quotable in a SQL predicate.
- **`vector` is nullable**, and that is the degenerate-document rule: a refusal is a written outcome, not a skipped one. A `NULL` embedding with a current `model_name` and a real fingerprint means "composed, refused, and it will be re-claimed exactly once when the fingerprint changes" — a distinct state from "no row", which means "never claimed". Two predicates, deliberately: the stale predicate and an `embedding IS NULL` diagnostic.

Add `title_neighbors` to the supporting-tables table — the precomputed similarity artefact, batch-built, boundary call 8's two signals (embedding cosine + genre/keyword Jaccard) and not the four PRD 05 specifies. Add `search_document` to the `Title` section as a **derived column the domain deliberately does not model**, with `DERIVED_COLUMNS` named and the 1:1 rule's three collision sites pointed at — this is the schema fact most likely to bite the next person who adds a column to `titles`, and PRD 02 is where they will be looking.

Update the relationships block: `Title 1─1 TitleEmbedding` is still right; `Title *─* Title (through title_neighbors)` is new.

- [ ] **Step 7: The three ADRs**

House style, in order, every one of them: **Context → Decision → Consequences → Evidence → Uncertainty.** Short is fine; ADR-0018 is the model — it states the decision in one bolded sentence, splits consequences into **Gained / Given up / Also / Rejected**, and its Evidence section carries measurements with dates. `.claude/rules/prd-maintenance.md`: *"Write one only when the call was contested — where a reasonable person would choose differently, or where the reasoning would otherwise be lost."* All three qualify.

**`0020-derived-state-carries-its-fingerprint.md`** — the milestone's central decision.

- **Context.** Every earlier milestone shipped state that something *wrote*. M6 is the first to ship state that is *derived*, and derived state has a failure mode the others do not: **a stale index does not raise, it answers.** A `Title` enriched last night and never re-indexed produces no error, no log line, no failed job and no degraded health check — it produces a result set that is quietly, confidently wrong. It is the M5 lesson in a new costume: a channel that looks healthy while delivering nothing; here, an index that looks healthy while describing yesterday.
- **Decision.** **Nothing in this milestone infers freshness. Every derived artefact is either fresh by construction, or carries the fingerprint of the input it was derived from so that staleness is a query.** The asymmetry is deliberate and the ADR names which half is which: the full-text document is a `GENERATED ALWAYS AS (…) STORED` column, so half the freshness problem is *deleted* rather than solved; the embedding needs a model, so it is a job, and `title_embeddings` records what was embedded (`md5` of the exact assembled text) and by what (`model_name`) — making stale one predicate with three consumers: the backfill's keyset cursor, the `usher.search.embeddings.stale` gauge, and the test that proves the enqueue-on-enrichment path actually closes.
- **Consequences — Gained.** A backfill that is a predicate rather than a cursor over "everything" is self-draining, idempotent and re-runnable at zero write cost; combined with `enqueue`'s `WHERE jobs.priority < excluded.priority`, re-running it writes nothing at all. A model swap invalidates every vector automatically. Coverage is *reported*: `SearchOutcome.semantic_coverage` is the fraction of the filtered population that had a vector, and `SearchMode.FUSED` against a catalog with zero embeddings degrades to full-text **and says so**, rather than silently becoming full-text with a confident-looking blended score.
- **Consequences — Given up.** A stored generated column is computed on every write of `titles`, including the 1.27M-row bulk bootstrap, and it **cannot** be suspended the way `bulk.py`'s `_SUSPENDABLE_INDEXES` suspends `ix_titles_sort_name`. Record Task 6's measured bootstrap regression here, whichever way it went, along with the named fallback (a `title_search_documents` side table maintained by a trigger — weaker, because it is code and code has bugs, but suspendable). A decision with a number attached is a decision; without one it is a retreat.
- **Consequences — Also: the degenerate-document trap, and the second one the fix creates.** Measured: **every whitespace-only input embeds to the identical vector** — cos(`""`, `" "`) = cos(`""`, `"\n"`) = **1.0000, exactly**. A title whose composed document comes out empty is a perfect unit vector satisfying every clause of the port contract, sitting at cosine 1.0 from every *other* empty-document title: a degenerate cluster of unbounded size pinned to the top of every "more like this" result, invisible to any assertion about norms, dimensions or determinism. So the composer refuses. **And the refusal creates a worse trap:** a refused title with no row keeps matching the stale predicate forever — the backfill re-claims it every pass, the gauge never reaches zero, and the queue churns permanently on rows that can never succeed. **This project has shipped exactly that bug once already** (the watch-history repair whose merge carried the walk's instant was refused by the very row it existed to repair). Hence: **a refusal is a written outcome, not a skipped one** — a `NULL` embedding, the current `model_name`, and the fingerprint of the degenerate text.
- **Consequences — Rejected**, three, each with why: *rebuild the document in the `index` job alongside the embedding* (the obvious symmetry, and wrong — it makes the cheap always-correct half depend on the expensive fallible half, and a parked job then means a stale document with the two failures indistinguishable); *trust the enqueue and skip the fingerprint* (the enqueue is one line in `EnrichService._apply`, and every path that writes a title without going through it produces a silently stale vector with nothing to detect it; the fingerprint costs one `md5` and makes correctness observable instead of assumed); *a `search_version` integer bumped on write* (detects that something changed but not *what*, so a model swap and a text edit are the same signal, and re-embedding after a model change would need a full sweep with no way to confirm it finished).
- **Evidence.** The whitespace cosine measurement; the name-only-skeleton control that shows thin is *not* degenerate (pairwise cosine 0.5867, sd 0.055, against a skeleton retrieving its own enriched form at 0.7638 versus a 0.4751 cross-title mean — crowded but ordered, so the refusal threshold is about *empty*, not about *thin*); Task 6's bootstrap-regression number; and the contract case that pins the whole scheme, `test_a_title_whose_document_is_degenerate_stops_matching_the_backfill`.
- **Uncertainty.** The fingerprint proves the *text* is current; it cannot prove the *assembly rule* is. A change to the document composer that does not change any title's text — a reordering, a separator change — produces identical fingerprints over different documents. In M6 that is unreachable (the composer is one function with one caller) and the honest answer is that it would be a manual sweep. Say so rather than implying the predicate covers it.

**`0021-the-suggest-path-is-its-own-port.md`** — dual-write visibility.

- **Context.** PRD 05 already treats autocomplete as its own narrow path, separate from full-text search — but `SearchIndex.suggest` was one method on the same port as `search`/`index`/`remove`. ADR-0002 gates Meilisearch "for the instant-search box only", which is a hint that the real swap boundary is `suggest` alone rather than the whole class. The 🔶 asked M6 to settle it.
- **Decision.** **Yes — `SuggestIndex` is a separate ABC with exactly one method**, `suggest(prefix, limit) -> list[SearchHit]`. **The argument that decides it is not tidiness, it is dual-write visibility.** ADR-0002's entire case for Postgres-first is "no dual-write synchronisation, no ghost documents, no second stateful service". If the gate fails and Meilisearch is added for the instant-search box, documents must then be written to *both* engines — which is exactly the cost ADR-0002 refused. Splitting the port puts that cost **in the type system**: adding Meilisearch means adding a write path to a port that today has none, and that is a visible, deliberate act. Folded into `SearchIndex`, the same change looks like implementing a method that was already there.
- **Consequences — Gained.** The follow-up ADR-0002's gate might trigger costs one class, and its price is legible before anyone pays it. `SearchIndex` keeps `index_many`/`remove` for the semantic half, which genuinely is a written artefact.
- **Consequences — Given up.** **`SuggestIndex` has no `index`/`remove`.** `PostgresSuggestIndex` queries `titles` directly through a trigram index and writes nothing, so abstract write methods would exist solely to be no-opped by the only implementation — and a port method whose only implementation no-ops it is a port method that documents nothing. The day a second implementation needs them is the day the dual write becomes real and gets paid for on purpose.
- **Consequences — Also.** Two ports means two contract suites and two fakes, and `FakeSuggestIndex` is *more forgiving than the real thing in the dangerous direction*: it computes edit distance in Python over its whole dict, so the one property the real path exists for — capping candidates before the re-rank — is structurally absent, and its typo tolerance is therefore better than the shipped one. The cap case is **skipped against the fake by capability flag** rather than passed, and asserted against real Postgres.
- **Evidence.** Task 26's recall@5 measurement is what a future Meilisearch decision would be made on; the port split is what makes that decision cheap. And the shape of the shipped implementation is itself evidence for the split: `PostgresSuggestIndex` shares no SQL, no index and no ranking rule with `PostgresSearchIndex` — trigram + `levenshtein_less_equal` over a capped candidate set ordered by popularity, against a weighted `tsquery` with `ts_rank`. Two methods on one port that share nothing were already two ports.
- **Uncertainty.** If the gate passes comfortably, the split buys a boundary nobody crosses — a port with one implementation, which ADR-0001's own reasoning says to be careful about spending effort on. Bought back here by one method, no write path, and a named second implementation. Say it plainly rather than pretending the cost is zero.

**`0022-the-embedder-is-optional-and-its-contract-is-measured.md`** — three things settled by measurement, all of them against the previous wording.

- **Context.** PRD 05 names `sentence-transformers`; `ports/embedding.py` carried a 🔶 on the query/document split and an *asserted*, never-checked normalisation contract; and PRD 05's "brute-force exact cosine" is only equal to a dot product if that contract holds.
- **Decision, part 1 — fastembed, and the embedder is optional.** The dependency lives behind an extra and `worker.register(JobKind.INDEX, …)` is guarded exactly as `ENRICH` is guarded on `provider is not None`, so a worker never claims work it cannot run and a deployment without a model still has full-text and trigram — PRD 05's "catalog lookup" tier, the one serving 1.27M titles.
- **Decision, part 2 — one `embed`, and the docstring's current clause is deleted.** Measured over 210 paired observations (24 gold documents + 1,200 distractors per draw, 5 disjoint draws, 42 queries): the documented BGE query prefix moves MRR by **−0.0028**, 95% CI `[−0.0259, +0.0203]` — an interval that excludes any benefit larger than +0.02. Applying it to **both** sides is significantly harmful: **−0.0663**, CI `[−0.1013, −0.0330]`. **The experiment carries a power control** — a deliberately wrong prefix moves MRR **−0.2497**, detected at P(>0) = 0.000 — so this is a measured null and not a blind one. Corroborated twice at the library level: sentence-transformers 5.6.1's `encode_query()`/`encode_document()` and fastembed 0.8.0's `query_embed()`/`passage_embed()` are each **bit-identical to plain `embed()`** for this checkpoint, because it declares empty prompts and its `config_sentence_transformers.json` has no `prompts` key at all. **The deleted clause is the actual hazard**: "callers are responsible for any query-side instruction prefix their chosen model needs" invites a caller to apply the prefix uniformly through one symmetric loop, which is precisely the −0.066 condition, and nothing in the codebase could catch it.
- **Decision, part 3 — normalisation is verified, and the docstring must say which operator it is for.** Norms are 1.0 to within **5.96e-08**, and `normalize_embeddings=False` returns **bit-identical** vectors: the flag cannot turn it off, because normalisation is baked into the *checkpoint* as a third module (`Transformer → Pooling → Normalize`), not applied by the library. Control: the same backbone with `2_Normalize` removed returns norms **8.99–9.46**. Three consequences the port carries: **(1)** it is a property of this checkpoint, not of embedders, so the implementation **asserts the norm on its first batch** rather than trusting a model card — a swap that drops the module silently returns norm-9 vectors and every dot-product score is then ~85× too large; **(2)** after the `halfvec` cast the vectors are **no longer unit** — norm drift goes from 1.19e-07 to **1.21e-04**, a 1000× change — so anything relying on "cosine == dot" must do so *before* the cast; **(3)** the contract is **only load-bearing under the inner-product operator** — verified against real pgvector, `<=>` is normalisation-*invariant* (a vector of norm 5 in the same direction gives the identical cosine distance) while `<#>` is not, so with `halfvec_cosine_ops`/`<=>`, which is what PRD 05 specifies, **normalisation buys speed, not correctness**. A docstring that does not say which operator reads as a correctness requirement the shipped index does not have.
- **Consequences — Gained.** 167 MiB against 4.8 GiB, no torch, 1.2 s cold install against 104 s, +10% throughput, lower peak RSS, and a container that stays in the same order of magnitude as its current 332 MB.
- **Consequences — Given up / Also, two caveats that belong here rather than in a comment.** **(1)** fastembed serves an optimised ONNX conversion from a *third-party* repo (`qdrant/bge-small-en-v1.5-onnx-q`), not BAAI's own weights — a supply-chain fact, with BAAI's own `onnx/model.onnx` as the fallback path. **(2)** The ST↔fastembed vector difference (max pairwise-similarity delta **1.41e-03**) is **6× larger than the halfvec quantisation error**, so the two are **not interchangeable without a re-embed** — which is why `model_name` records the runtime (`fastembed:BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5`) and not just the checkpoint.
- **Consequences — Also: offline behaviour is a trap and it must be configured, not assumed.** A warm cache is **not** sufficient: with the cache populated, no network, and `HF_HUB_OFFLINE` unset, the load **fails** with `RuntimeError: Cannot send a request, as the client has been closed` — huggingface_hub 1.26.0 reuses a closed client on the retry path instead of falling back to the cache, and the message mentions neither the network nor the cache. Reproduced two independent ways. **CI and the container set `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` explicitly**; it is also the only setting under which a genuine cache miss produces a comprehensible `OSError`. And **do not use `snapshot_download`** — it fetches 401 MB / 14 files, three redundant copies of the same weights.
- **Evidence.** All of the above, measured 2026-08-02 on this host (Python 3.13.14, `uv` 0.11.31, Ryzen 7 5800X3D, RTX 4090), corpora synthetic. Plus the throughput invariant, which is the one number people will want and the one most easily misquoted: **throughput is linear in *tokens*, not texts** — CPU holds ~8,000–10,700 tokens/s across the range (412.7 texts/s at 19 tokens; 83.5 at 100; 18.7 at 516), a realistic `name + overview + genres + keywords` document is ~100–130 tokens, best CPU batch size is **16** with the curve flat from 16–64 and degrading at 128.
- **Uncertainty.** **GPU throughput is not measured, deliberately** — the 4090 had 210 MiB free of 24,564 (a live `vllm` container holds 21,764 MiB) and creating a CUDA context alone needs more than was free, so the probe declined to disturb a running service. **No decision in this ADR rests on a GPU number**; the CPU figures are what the backfill is sized against. Re-run when the GPU is free. Also unmeasured: whether a *different* BGE-family checkpoint needs the prefix (this result is about this checkpoint, and a model that genuinely needs asymmetry brings a new `model_name`, a new measurement, and a port change made on evidence).

Add all three to `decisions/README.md`.

The commands section gains `usher search`, `usher suggest`, `usher similar` and `usher index` (with `--backfill` and `--create-indexes`), and the requirements section gains the optional embedding extra with the 167 MiB figure and the `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` note.

## Task 28: Milestone verification

The controller runs this as the smoke test before merging `milestone/m6-search` to `main`. It is modelled on M5's equivalent, and the reason it is modelled on M5's rather than M4's is that **M5's caught three things and one of them had been broken since M1**: `cp .env.example .env` — the README's own first step — produced `1637 passed, 461 errors`, invisible to 2,098 passing tests. A gate that only re-runs the suite the milestone was developed against re-proves what is already known.

```bash
uv run pytest tests/unit -q 2>&1 | tail -3
uv run pytest tests/integration -q 2>&1 | tail -3
```

M5 merged to `main` at **2,112 passed, 2 skipped**. Record M6's three numbers, and — this is the part that is not ceremony — **state the expected move before running it and reconcile any difference**:

| Group | What it adds | Roughly |
|---|---|---|
| A (1–5) | `SearchIndexContract` ×2 drivers, `SuggestIndexContract` ×2, `EmbedderContract` ×2 (one marked opt-in), plus port DTO unit cases | the three contract suites are 9 + 5 + 5 cases, each run against a fake **and** a real backend |
| B (6–9) | the generated column's three collision sites, the extensions, the index set, `title_embeddings`/`title_neighbors`, the stale/degenerate predicate pair, the keyset cursor | mostly integration |
| C (10–15) | `JobKind.INDEX`, the composer and its refusal, the handler, the enqueue on enrichment, the once-per-process embedder, the backfill | both layers |

**Two counts that must move for a specific reason**, and a run where they did not is a finding rather than a pass:

Confirm both directions still fail by planting each, once. A guard that globs nothing passes exactly like a guard that passes.


**The downgrade is not optional and it is the half that is usually broken**, because nothing exercises it in ordinary development. Four things to confirm by eye rather than by exit code:

- **`down_revision` chains correctly.** M6's first migration sets `down_revision = "f1a7d3c9e824"` (the sweep index, the current head). The revision-id convention is *exhausted* — existing ids spell `a…`→`f…` in chain order and the next letter is not hex — so M6 continues with `fa…`, `fb…`, each of which sorts after `f1a7d3c9e824` and keeps `ls` order. **Confirm the first migration's docstring records that**, so M7 does not re-derive it.
- **Do the extensions get dropped?** `CREATE EXTENSION` is M6's first in this repository, and a `DROP EXTENSION vector` in a downgrade will fail if anything else in the database still uses the type. Decide, record the decision in the migration's own docstring, and test whichever way it went. The defensible answer is that a downgrade drops the *tables and columns* and leaves the extensions installed — an extension is a database-level capability, not schema this migration owns — but it must be a decision with a sentence, not an omission.
- **`test_migration_creates_the_updated_at_triggers` asserts the trigger set exactly.** Any new M6 table with an `updated_at` trigger is in that literal set, in the same commit.
- **`test_migration_matches_the_orm_metadata` must be green**, and it is the check with the most ways to be quietly wrong here. `enum_column` compiles to `VARCHAR` with `native_enum=False` and no membership CHECK, so `JobKind.INDEX` needs **no** migration — assert that this test stays green rather than assuming it. And `--autogenerate` is blind to CHECK bodies and triggers, so a generated column's *expression* changing is exactly the category it will not see. Read the migration; do not trust the diff.

- [ ] **Step 5: The clean-checkout smoke test — the step that found the M5 bugs**

```bash
cd $(mktemp -d)
git clone /home/anirudhlath/code/usher usher-smoke && cd usher-smoke
git checkout milestone/m6-search
cp .env.example .env
sed -i "s/^USHER_SECRET_KEY=.*/USHER_SECRET_KEY=$(openssl rand -hex 32)/" .env
uv sync
uv run pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -5
uv run usher --help
uv run usher bootstrap-status
uv run usher search --help && uv run usher suggest --help && uv run usher similar --help
uv run usher index --help
```

**`cp .env.example .env` is the README's own first step and it is what produced 461 errors at M5.** The mechanism, restated because M6 reintroduces the identical hazard: `.env` has two readers with different vocabularies. Docker Compose reads it to substitute `${...}` into `compose.yml`; pydantic-settings reads the same file as a settings source with `extra="forbid"`. A compose-only variable is therefore an *extra* input to `Settings`, and `USHER_HOST_PORT` — shipped in `.env.example` since M1 — made every entry point fail: `uv run pytest` at 1637 passed / 461 errors, `usher bootstrap-status` and `usher push --probe` with a raw traceback and exit 1.

**Why the suite could not see it, and why it still cannot.** `tests/conftest.py::clean_environment` neutralises the `env_file` source so a developer's own `.env` cannot fail the suite — deliberately. The 461 errors came from the one path that fixture cannot reach: `tests/integration/conftest.py::_upgrade_head`, session-scoped, which saves and restores `os.environ` but has no way to hide a *file*. So a clean-checkout run is not redundant with `uv run pytest`; it is the only thing that exercises the file source at all.

**M6's settings are the same hazard**, and there are more of them than M5 had — `search_*` and `embedding_*`, at least one of which (the embedding model name, or the extra's presence) is a plausible candidate for someone to spell into `compose.yml` as a substitution. Three checks, all of which must pass and two of which are the ones with teeth:

- `test_every_setting_is_read_by_something` — every new `Settings` field is *read* in `src/` in the same commit. This one is pre-existing and it is also a **collateral kill** that ruled out the naive fix at M5 ("just make the compose variable a Settings field" dies here).
- `test_every_usher_variable_in_env_example_is_a_setting_or_compose_reserved` and `test_every_variable_compose_substitutes_is_a_setting_or_compose_reserved` — the two scanners, one per file. **Both are needed**: the M1 commit that introduced `USHER_HOST_PORT` touched both files, and the compose-side scanner regex-scans the *whole* of `compose.yml` for `${...}`, not just `ports:`, because a variable added to a `volumes:` or an `image:` line is the same hazard.
- `test_env_example_ships_the_defaults` — `.env.example` documents every setting at its own default.

**Any case written for this must pass `_env_file=` explicitly.** The autouse fixture neutralises the class-level `env_file`, so a case that relies on it proves nothing — the same shape as the `sitecustomize.py` installation proof.

Also confirm from the clean checkout that **a deployment with no embedder still works**, because that is ADR-0022's claim and this is the only place it is exercised end to end: `uv sync` without the embedding extra, then `usher search` against a seeded catalog, and `usher work --once` with `INDEX` unregistered. A worker never claims work it cannot run — `worker.register(JobKind.INDEX, …)` is guarded the way `ENRICH` is guarded on `provider is not None` — so the expected outcome is full-text and trigram answering normally and `index` jobs sitting pending, not a crash and not a park.

- [ ] **Step 6: The whole-suite mutation sweep**

Every mutation named in Tasks 1–25, run in sequence against the **full** suite rather than its own task's selection. A per-task sweep cannot see collateral in another file, which is the entire point of a final sweep — M4's found `usable-ids-filters-nothing` was caught by a case in a different file's *second* item, and M5's found the sixth predicted survivor was killed for a different reason than predicted.

```bash
cd /home/anirudhlath/code/usher
find src tests -name __pycache__ -type d -exec rm -rf {} +
export PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1
git status --porcelain          # must be empty before starting
uv run pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -1  # baseline green, and record the time
```

**The three trap-disarming rules, from this plan's own front matter, enforced by the harness rather than by the operator's attention:**

1. **Stale `.pyc`.** Clear `__pycache__` and export `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` before the sweep starts, not between mutations.
2. **Mutate in place, never in a `/tmp` copy.** `cp -r` copies `.venv/bin/*`, whose shebangs point back into the *original* venv, so a mutation in the copy is never imported and the suite runs green proving nothing. Revert with `cp` backups, **never `git checkout --`**, which clobbers uncommitted work in the same file. **If every mutation in a sweep survives, that is this trap, not a finding.**
3. **A run that collected zero tests is `DID-NOT-RUN`, never `KILLED`.** M4's sweep scored three mutations as caught having measured nothing, because zsh does not word-split an unquoted `$VAR` and a selection passed that way reached pytest as one bogus path — non-zero exit, nothing run, recorded as a kill. The harness asserts a run actually ran, and additionally: the target string must appear **exactly once** in the file before substitution, every mutation is dry-run through `ast.parse` before the sweep starts so an `IndentationError` cannot be scored as a kill (`BROKEN-MUTATION`), and a mutation that exceeds a wall-clock bound is `HUNG` rather than either.

**`HUNG` is a real outcome in this milestone and it must be distinguishable.** M5 hit it twice: an unbounded burst turns a `put_nowait` → `await put` mutation from KILLED into HUNG, "which in a sweep log reads like a mutation nothing observed rather than one everything caught". M6's candidates are the backfill loop (a predicate that does not converge) and any concurrency case around the staging lock.

**Report every mutation with its outcome and the case that killed it**, in the shape M4's and M5's sweeps are recorded in `CLAUDE.md`. Four results are worth naming specifically because they are this milestone's headline claims and a sweep that does not kill them has found something:

| Mutation | Must fail |
|---|---|
| the `setweight` classes collapsed to one unweighted document | `test_a_name_match_outranks_an_overview_match` — the milestone's central retrieval claim, and no membership assertion can see it |
| RRF replaced by weighted score addition | `test_fusion_does_not_add_scores_from_different_scales` — ADR-0002's "never by adding scores from incompatible scales" |
| a missing vector treated as a zero vector | `test_a_title_with_no_vector_is_absent_from_semantic_results_not_last` — makes every unembedded title a mediocre match for every query |
| the composer's refusal made a *skip* instead of a written `NULL` row | `test_a_title_whose_document_is_degenerate_stops_matching_the_backfill` — the bug this project has already shipped once |

**Named survivors are recorded as equivalent, with the argument, and never quietly dropped.** This plan names its expected survivors; each must survive **for the stated reason** rather than for a different one, and a survivor that this plan did not name is a **missing test, and writing it is part of this step**. The precedent is exact and it is the reason the rule is worded this way: M5's plan named six expected survivors, five survived for the stated reason, and the sixth — `propagate = False` — was *killed*, by a case that pins the field directly rather than by the security property the plan reasoned about. That was recorded, "so nobody reads that kill as evidence the propagate flag is load-bearing for the token". M4's single survivor (`GREATEST` in `_ENQUEUE`) is recorded the same way, with the demonstration that removing *both* it and the `WHERE` clause fails 2 cases — so the property is covered, by the predicate, and the case whose name suggests otherwise is really a test of something else.

So for each survivor, write: **what it is, why it cannot be observed today, what would make it observable, and what changes in the repository would turn it into a real bug.** "Equivalent mutant" with no argument attached is indistinguishable from "we did not write that test".

Finally, restore green and prove it:

```bash
git status --porcelain          # empty again
uv run pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -1
```

```bash
bad = []
for md in roots:
    for link in re.findall(r'\]\(([^)#][^)]*\.md)\)', md.read_text()):
        if not (md.parent / link).resolve().exists():
            bad.append(f"{md}: {link}")
print("\n".join(bad) if bad else "OK")
EOF
```

Expected: `OK`, and every check green. M6 adds three ADRs and touches seven PRD files, so both are live checks rather than formalities — and `test_no_dataset_row_is_committed_anywhere` scans `docs/`, which is where Task 26's measurement now lives.

- [ ] **Step 8: Write `docs/plans/progress.md`, and state what M7 inherits**

The progress log is the running record between milestones and it is written in the voice of *what was found*, not *what was done* — capitals for reversals, `REFUTED` where a guess did not survive. Append an M6 section carrying:

- **The headline:** the milestone shipped derived state, and the one thing it existed not to get wrong was that a stale index answers rather than raising. Whether the generated column's bootstrap cost came in under the bar Task 6 set, with the number.
- **Task 26's gate result**, per typo class, per band, with the sample size and the seed — and what it obliged. If it failed, the scoped follow-up and its owner; either way, that **M6 did not add Meilisearch** and why that was decided before the number was known.
- **Every guess the live run refuted**, first. A run that confirmed everything did not look hard enough.
- **The mutation sweep's tally** — killed / survived / DID-NOT-RUN / HUNG / BROKEN — and every survivor with its argument.
- **The smoke test's findings**, in M5's shape: what `cp .env.example .env` did, what `docker compose config` rendered, and any settings that did not reach the container.
- **The three ADRs** and the four PRD 05 corrections, in one line each.
- **The MovieLens tag-genome gap**, called out separately from M6's own work, with the owner Task 27 assigned it. It is the one item in this milestone that is about a *different* milestone, and burying it inside an M6 summary is how it stayed invisible for five milestones in the first place.
- **The final counts**: tests, import contracts, image size, migration head.

**What M7 inherits**, stated as capabilities rather than as a summary — this is the section M7's plan will actually read:

| M7 gets | From |
|---|---|
| `SimilarityService` and a populated `title_neighbors`, so a similarity row is a lookup rather than a computation | Task 21 |
| a blend written as a **sum of weighted terms over an explicit signal list**, so landing a third signal is adding a term and a weight rather than rewriting a scorer | boundary call 8 |
| a **stored generated** search document, so the day `Credit` lands, filling weight class B is a migration and not a rewrite | boundary calls 2 and 3, ADR-0020 |
| `title_search_names` **not** built, and the reason: it is the migration that adds aliases and people, and M7 is the milestone that has them | boundary call 3 |
| a keyset cursor on `TitleRepository`, which nothing had before — `list_unmatched`'s `OFFSET` is 43.7 ms at offset 0 and 388.9 ms at offset 1,126,574 | Task 9 |
| the staging small-batch path, so a per-title enqueue is not a table-level lock on the pipeline's hot path | Task 22 |
| a **named, owned** MovieLens tag-genome obligation instead of five documents assuming it exists | Task 27 |
| the embedder as an **optional** dependency behind an extra, with a worker that never claims work it cannot run | ADR-0022 |

And what M7 does **not** get, named rather than implied: no `GET /titles/{id}/similar` (M9's, boundary call 1); no query expansion (boundary call 6 — the seam is `SearchService.search`'s query string, and M8 or M9 wraps it); no `Person`/`Credit`/`Collection`/`Image`, which M7 itself owns; no `search_queries` table, which Task 27 assigned to M9 whole; and no measured GPU embedding throughput, which the probe deliberately declined to take from a running service.

Then hand back to the controller with: the four counts (tests, contracts, image size, migration head), the gate's number and what it obliged, the sweep's tally, and any smoke-test finding that is not yet fixed. **A finding that is recorded but unfixed is a merge blocker unless it is named as such and accepted** — M5 fixed all three of its smoke-test findings before merging, in two commits, and that is the bar.
